E E -theory is compactly assembled
Ulrich Bunke and Benjamin Dünzinger

Abstract
We show that the equivariant E E -theory category E sep G \mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}} for separable C ∗ C^{*} -algebras is a compactly assembled stable ∞ \infty -category. We derive this result as a consequence of the shape theory for C ∗ C^{*} -algebras developed by Blackadar and Dardarlat and a new ∞ \infty -categorical refinement of the category of asymptotic morphisms A sep G \mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}} . As an application we investigate a topological enrichment of the homotopy category of a compactly assembled ∞ \infty -category in general and argue that the results of Carrión and Schafhauser on the enrichment of the classical E E -theory category can be derived by specialization.



1
Introduction

1.1
Statement of the main result

The
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}
of stable presentable
\infty
-categories and colimit-preserving functors is equipped with the symmetric monoidal structure given by the Lurie tensor product and therefore admits a notion of dualizable objects. According to

[
Lurb
, D.7.0.7]
the dualizable objects in
\mathbf{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}
can equivalently be characterized as the compactly assembled
presentable stable
\infty
-categories. In particular, compactly generated presentable stable
\infty
-categories are
examples of compactly assembled
stable
\infty
-categories. They comprise the image
of the ind-completion functor
{\mathtt{Ind}}:\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{\infty}\to\mathbf{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}
. This functor identifies the category of
small idempotent complete stable
\infty
-categories with the subcategory

\mathbf{Pr}^{LL}_{\omega,\mathrm{st}}\subseteq\mathbf{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}
of compactly generated stable presentable
\infty
-categories and
strongly cocontinuous functors (colimit preserving functors whose right-adjoints also preserve colimits).

Dualizable stable
\infty
-categories
recently attracted great attention since
[
Efia
]
has shown that
every localizing invariant
L:\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{\infty}\to\mathbf{Sp}
(a reduced functor preserving bi-fibre
sequences) has a unique extension
\hat{L}
as in

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 19.32983pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.32983pt\raise 85.85486pt\raise-22.6989pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{L}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 69.06465pt\raise-26.91269pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 50.28694pt\raise-32.17557pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{Sp}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ ,

where
\mathbf{Pr}^{{\mathrm{dual}}}\subseteq\mathbf{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}
is the subcategory of dualizable
presentable stable
\infty
-categories and strongly cocontinuous functors.
Known classes of examples of dualizable presentable stable
\infty
-categories
beyond the compactly generated ones are given by
sheaves on locally compact topological spaces with values in spectra
[
Lurb
, Proposition 21.1.7.1]
, certain
“nuclear” module categories appearing in
analytic geometry
[
Efi22
]
or the theory of analytic stacks developed by Clausen-Scholze, but also the
target of the universal finitary localizing invariant

{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathrm{loc}}:\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{perf}}_{\infty}\to\mathrm{Mot}_{\mathrm{loc}}

(1.1)

[
Efic
]
. We refer to
[
KNP
]

for a good introduction to many of these aspects.

The main goal of the present paper is to contribute a new class of examples
of dualizable presentable stable
\infty
-categories coming from non-commutative homotopy theory of
C^{*}
-algebras. Let
G
be a countable group and
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
be the category of
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras. By
3.23
.
2

we then have the universal
homotopy invariant,
K_{G}
-stable, exact, s-finitary and countable sum-preserving functor

\mathrm{e}^{G}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}

(1.2)

to a cocomplete stable
\infty
-category. This functor is an
\infty
-categorical enhancement of the classical equivariant
E
-theory functor
[
GHT00
]
.
The following is the main result of the present paper.

Theorem 1.1
.

For every countable group
G

the stable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{E}^{G}
is presentable and dualizable.

The equivariant
E
-theory functor (
1.2
) could be viewed as the non-commutative homotopy theoretic analogue of the universal localizing invariant (
1.1
), and then
Theorem

1.1
becomes the analogue of the dualizability of
\mathrm{Mot}_{\mathrm{loc}}
from
[
Efic
]
.

As shown in
[
Efic
]
every compactly assembled presentable stable
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}

can be written in the form

{\mathbf{C}}\simeq{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\aleph_{1}})

where
{\mathbf{C}}^{\aleph_{1}}\subseteq{\mathbf{C}}
is the full
subcategory of
\aleph_{1}
-compact objects and
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\aleph_{1}}
is the functor which freely adds all
\aleph_{1}
-filtered colimits.
In fact, the
\aleph_{1}
-
{\mathtt{Ind}}
-completion functor

{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\aleph_{1}}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{ex},\mathrm{casmbl}}\to\mathbf{Pr}^{{\mathrm{dual}}}

induces an equivalence from the category of small countably cocomplete compactly assembled
stable
\infty
-categories and countable colimit- and compact maps preserving functors to
\mathbf{Pr}^{{\mathrm{dual}}}
.

In the present paper we will mainly work on the level of small
catergories. In the case of
equivariant
E
-theory by
3.21
we have an equivalence
(\mathrm{E}^{G})^{\aleph_{1}}\simeq\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
, where
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is the target of the universal
homotopy invariant,
K_{G}
-stable, exact and countable sum-preserving functor

\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}

from separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras to a countably cocomplete stable
\infty
-category.
In order to show
Theorem

1.1
we must therefore show that
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is compactly assembled (in the sense of countably cocomplete stable
\infty
-categories). To this end
we generalize and apply the shape theory for
C^{*}
-algebras
[
Bla85
]
,
[
Dad94
]
.

In addition to the verification of
Theorem

1.1
, this paper
develops some foundational material on compact maps and shape systems in countably cocomplete
\infty
-categories
in
Section

2
, and it also discusses applications to the homotopy theory of
C^{*}
-algebras in
Section

4
.

1.2
A detailed introduction

This subsection gives a more detailed overview on the results of this paper including the main definitions and precise statements.
We let
G
be a countable group and denote by
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
the category of separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras.
The homotopy theory in this category will be captured by
functors

a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\ ,\qquad\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\ .

(1.3)

Here
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is defined as
the universal homotopy invariant and countable filtered colimit preserving functor to an
\infty
-category admitting countable filtered colimits, and
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is defined as the universal homotopy invariant,
K_{G}
-stable, exact and countable sum-preserving functor to a countably cocomplete stable
\infty
-category (see
3.14
for a description of these properties).
These functors turn out to be
\infty
-categorical enhancements of classically known functors

a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}\ ,\qquad\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}\ ,

(1.4)

where

\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
is the category of separable
C^{*}
-algebras and homotopy classes of asymptotic morphisms introduced in
[
GHT00
]
(see also
[
Dad94
]
for the case of a trivial group), while
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
is the classical equivariant
E
-theory category constructed in the same reference (see also
[
Hig90
]
,
[
CH90
]
for the case of a trivial group
G
).
The proof of the existence of the functors in (
1.3
) by providing constructions is a central topic of this paper.

We next introduce the notion of a compactly assembled
\infty
-category.
If
{\mathbf{D}}
is an
\infty
-category admitting countable filtered colimits, then
the canonical morphism
y:{\mathbf{D}}\to{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})
has a left-adjoint
|-|:{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})\to{\mathbf{D}}

called the realization functor. Here
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})
is the closure of the essential image of
y:{\mathbf{D}}\to{\mathtt{Ind}}({\mathbf{D}})
under countable filtered colimits. Adapting the characterization
[
Lurb
, Sec. 21.1.2.10]
of being compactly assembled for large
\infty
-categories to the case of small
\infty
-categories just admitting countable filtered colimits we make the following definition.

Definition 1.2
.

The
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
is called compactly assembled if the realization functor admits a left-adjoint.

In order to highlight the analogy with the shape systems for
C^{*}
-algebras as described below this left-adjoint
S:{\mathbf{D}}\to{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})
will be called the shape functor.

Remark 1.3
.

In our context of an
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
just admitting countable filtered colimits an object
A
of
{\mathbf{D}}
will be called compact if the functor
{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(A,-):{\mathbf{D}}\to\mathbf{An}
preserves countable filtered colimits. We say that
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly generated if every object of
{\mathbf{D}}
is equivalent to a countable filtered colimit of a system of compact objects.
If
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly generated, then it is compactly assembled in the sense of
1.2
. But a general
\infty
-category with countable filtered colimits might have very few compact objects and being compactly assembled is a good replacement of the condition of being compactly generated.
∎

Example 1.4
.

If
H
is a finite group, then by
3.73
we know that
\mathrm{e}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}})
is a compact object of
\mathrm{E}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.
The induction functor
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}:\mathrm{E}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
has a countable colimit preserving right-adjoint
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}
(see
3.62
) and therefore preserves compact objects. In particular the objects
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(\mathrm{e}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}}))\simeq\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(C_{0}(G/H))

are compact in
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
for all finite subgroups
H
of
G
. Further note that the collection of compact objects is closed under finite colimits and retracts. But in general
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is not generated by these compact objects. If it were, then as a consequence of
[
GHT00
, Thm. 13.1]
the Baum-Connes assembly map for the maximal crossed product would be an equivalence, but this is known to be false e.g. for groups with property
T
having a
\gamma
-element
[
AJV19
, Cor. 5.6]
. We also do not expect that
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is compactly generated at all.
∎

The central result of the present paper is the following (see
Theorem

3.67
and
3.68
).

Theorem 1.5
.

The
\infty
-categories
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
are compactly assembled.

If
{\mathbf{D}}
is an
\infty
-category admitting countable filtered colimits, then according to
2.2
we have various notions of compactness of morphisms in
{\mathbf{D}}
. The following definition is a translation of
2.2
.
3
.

Definition 1.6
.

A map
f:A\to B
in
{\mathbf{D}}
is weakly compact if for every system
(C_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
with colimit
C:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}C_{n}
and map
B\to C
in
{\mathbf{D}}
there exists a lift

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{C_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C}

for some
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.

Definition 1.7
.

A system
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
{\mathbf{D}}
is called a slp-compact exhaustion of
A
if
A\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}A_{n}

and the structure maps
A_{n}\to A_{n+1}
are slp-compact for all
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.

The addition of
slp
indicates a strengthening of weak compactness
2.2
.
2
.
We will use the following characterization of being compactly assembled in terms of slp-exhaustions
which follows from combination of
2.25

and
Theorem

2.26
. This characterization provides a version of the internal characterization of compactly assembled categories due to Clausen in the least structured setting possible.

Proposition 1.8
.

If
{\mathbf{D}}
is an
\infty
-category admitting countable filtered colimits, then
it is compactly assembled if and only if every object admits an slp-compact exhaustion.

We now turn attention to
C^{*}
-algebras.
The basic notion of the shape theory in the category
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is that of a semi-projective homomorphism
f:A\to B
which was introduced in the non-equivariant situation by
[
Bla85
]
.

Definition 1.9
.

A homomorphism
f:A\to B
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is semi-projective if for every separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
Z
and increasing sequence
(I_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
of invariant ideals in
Z
with direct limit ideal
I:=\overline{\bigcup_{n\in{\rm Nature}}I_{n}}
and map
B\to Z/I
there exists a factorization

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Z/I_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{Z/I}}

for some
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.

The similarity of
1.9
and
1.6
is not only formal.
The following theorem (see
3.70
) directly relates the semi-projectivity of maps in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}

with the strong compactness (this is our strongest notion of compactness
2.2
.
1
) of the induced morphism in
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
or
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.
We call
B
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
twice suspended and
K_{G}
-stable if

B\cong B^{\prime}\otimes C_{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})\otimes K_{G}
for some

B^{\prime}
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
, where
K_{G}:=K(L^{2}(G)\otimes\ell^{2})
.

Theorem 1.10
.

Let
A\to B
be a semi-projective morphism in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

1.

The induced morphism
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(f):a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)\to a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(B)
is strongly compact in
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

2.

If
B
is twice suspended and
K_{G}
-stable, then
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(f):\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)\to\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(B)
is strongly compact in
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

The proof of
Theorem

1.5
is based on the
shape theory for
C^{*}
-algebras developed by
[
Bla85
]

and the insights of
[
Dad05
]
.
Let
A
be a separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra.

Definition 1.11
.

A system
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is called a shape system for
A

if
A\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}A_{n}
and the structure maps
A_{n}\to A_{n+1}
are semi-projective for all
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.

Note the analogy with
1.7
.
In
Section

3.1
we extend the theory of
[
Bla85
]
to the equivariant case. In particular we will show in
3.12
:

Proposition 1.12
.

Every object of
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}

admits an explicit shape system.

The additional adjective explicit is technically relevant but not important for the purpose of this introduction.
In order to understand why
Theorem

1.5
is true in the case of
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
one could observe that
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
by
Theorem

1.10
maps shape systems in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
to slp-compact exhaustions in
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and then apply the combination of
1.12
and
1.8
. In order to deduce that
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is compactly assembled we use that this category is obtained from
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
by a sequence of Bousfield localizations
which preserve the property of being compactly assembled.
But note that our actual argument is more indirect as we first show
Theorem

1.5
and then use it in order to deduce

Theorem

1.10
.

In the present paper we will discuss some consequences of being compactly assembled which are interesting in the context of
C^{*}
-algebra theory.

By
2.38
a morphism
B\to C
in a pointed countably cocomplete
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
is called a strong phantom map if the composition
A\to B\to C
vanishes for every weakly compact map
A\to B
. In the present paper we will discuss some consequences of the fact that
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly assembled for the calculus of strong phantom maps.

Strong phantom maps in equivariant
E
-theory occur naturally. For example, if
two homomorphisms
f,g:A\to B
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
are approximatively unitary equivalent, then
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(f)-\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(g)
is a strong phantom map in
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
(see
4.46
). In
4.51
we will show that the boundary map
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(C)\to\Sigma\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)
associated to a weakly quasi-diagonal extension
0\to A\to B\to C\to 0
is a strong phantom map.
Strong phantom maps in equivariant
E
-theory were also used in
[
Bun23
]
in order to capture the Rokhlin property of
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras for finite groups
G
.

If
{\mathbf{D}}
is an
\infty
-category
admitting countable filtered colimits, then the morphism sets of its homotopy category
\mathrm{ho}({\mathbf{D}})
have two interesting topologies, namely the shape topology and the light condensed topology (the latter is only defined under the additional assumption of the existence of finite products), see
Section

4.1
and
Section

4.2
. If
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly assembled, then these topologies coincide and have very nice properties. In the case of
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
the topologies on the morphism sets of their homotopy categories
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
and
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
have previously been introduced in
[
CS24
]
. In
Section

4.4

we will argue that most of the statements about these topologies obtained in
[
CS24
]
are specializations of general statements which are true for the homotopy category of any compactly assembled
\infty
-category.

1.3
Description of the contents of the paper

The first section provides a reference for the basic definitions and results from the theory of compactly assembled
\infty
-categories with countable filtered colimits. In
Section

2.1
we introduce the notions of weakly compact, slp compact and strongly compact morphisms. In
Section

2.2
we introduce the notion of a shape of an object.
If every object of the category admits a shape, then the category turns out to be compactly assembled. We then analyse
the interaction of compactness and shapes with functors. In particular for any
\infty
-category with countable filtered colimits
{\mathbf{D}}
we find a maximal full subcategory which is compactly assembled, see
2.18
for a precise formulation. In
Section

2.3
we give a sufficient criterion for the existence of shapes of an object in terms of the existence of slp-compact exhaustions (see
Theorem

2.24
).
Note that some of the statements shown in these sections would simplify if one assumed that the
\infty
-category were compactly assembled. But we decided to
develop this theory in the general case in order to make it applicable also to the stable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{KK}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
from
[
BEL
]
which is not known to be compactly assembled. So we can now form and study (in future) the largest compactly assembled subcategory
(\mathrm{KK}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})^{\mathrm{casmbl}}
of
\mathrm{KK}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
or consider those objects in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
which are known to admit
shapes.
In
Section

2.4
we show a technical result (crucially used in
Section

2.3
) ensuring that a slp-map of towers
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}\to(B_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}
of anima induces an equivalence on the level of limits. Finally, in
Section

2.5
we introduce the notion of strong phantom maps in a pointed
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
admitting countable filtered colimits (see
2.38
). If
{\mathbf{D}}
is stable, then we show in
2.46
that the existence of shapes of objects (or even the weaker condition of weakly compact approximations) allows
to form countable infinite sums of strong phantoms.
If
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly assembled, then as a consequence of
2.47
the composition of any two phantom morphisms in
{\mathbf{D}}
is trivial. We furthermore show in
2.50
that a filtered colimit preserving exact functor between compactly assembled stable
\infty
-categories preserves strong phantom maps.

The
Section

3
is devoted to the construction of the functors
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
in (
1.3
) and the verification of our main result
Theorem

1.5
.
As a start, in
Section

3.1
we extend the theory of
[
Bla85
]
to the equivariant case.
The main new insight is that the homotopy lifting results for explicit semi-projective maps shown in
[
Bla85
]

can be interpreted as the slp lifting property for squares (see
2.1
) of mapping spaces in the homotopy localization
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
, see
3.8
. This is the basis for translating
semi-projectivity of
f
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
to versions of compactness of the maps
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(f)
or
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(f)
in
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
or
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

In
Section

3.2
we characterize the equivariant
E
-theory functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
essentially uniquely by universal properties. In particular we explain in detail
all
C^{*}
-algebraic notions appearing in this characterization.
This sets up the task of showing the existence of such a functor
by providing a construction. This construction is the program of the subsequent
Section

3.3
,
Section

3.4
and
Section

3.5
. We provide three essentially independent constructions of
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
which highlight different properties and foundations.

In
Section

3.3
we construct a functor

\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
as a Dwyer-Kan localization of
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
at the morphisms which are inverted by the classical
E
-theory functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
. This construction closely follows
[
LN18
]
,
[
BEL
]
giving analogous constructions of stable
\infty
-categories representing
KK
-theory. The subscript
GHT
abbreviates Guentner-Higson-Trout
and indicates that this functor is designed to be an
\infty
-categorical enhancement of the classical functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
constructed by these authors in
[
GHT00
]
. The verification that it has the desired universal properties depends heavily on the classical equivariant
E
-theory
[
GHT00
]
.

In
Section

3.4
we give a purely homotopy theoretic construction of a functor
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
by forcing the desired universal properties. This construction is an equivariant generalization of the construction of the non-equivariant
E
-theory
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
in
[
Bun24b
]
. It is independent of the classical
E
-theory.
In the following we stress two aspects which are new in comparison with
[
Bun24b
]
. In
the non-equivariant case the
K
-stabilization was a left Bousfield localization
L_{K}
induced by an idempotent
algebra in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
represented by the algebra
K
of compact operators.
The
K_{G}
-stabilization in the case of finite groups is completely analogous. But for infinite groups
G

the corresponding
K_{G}
-stabilization consists of the composition of a left Bousfield localization induced by an idempotent algebra
\hat{K}_{G}=K((L^{2}(G)\oplus{\mathbb{C}})\otimes\ell^{2})
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
and a right Bousfield localization induced by
K_{G}
considered as an idempotent coalgebra in
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
. The problem in the case of an infinite group
G
is that

K_{G}
lacks invariant projections. They are added to
\hat{K}_{G}
by adding the artifical summand
{\mathbb{C}}
to
L^{2}(G)
,
but then
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}
does not invert all
K_{G}
-equivalences so that the second localization becomes necessary.
The details are explained in
3.34
.
The second difference to
[
Bun24b
]
is that we modify the step forcing exactness in order to ensure that the
E
-theory functor
preserves countable sums. This property is indicated by the subscript
\oplus
in the notation.

Finally in
Section

3.5
we first construct the universal homotopy invariant and countable filtered colimit preserving
functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. This construction is strongly inspired by but technically independent from

[
Dad94
]
. We then proceed similarly as for
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
and force the remaining universal properties
in order to get a functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
. The subscript
\mathrm{fin}
stands for finitary and indicates the relevance of the property of preserving countable filtered colimits. The step in which we enforce Bott periodicity
depends technically on the identification of
\mathrm{ho}\circ a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
with the classical functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}

and facts about the asymptotic morphism representing the Bott element shown in
[
GHT00
]
.

In
Section

3.6
we then verify that the functors
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
,
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
and
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
are really equivalent in an essentially unique manner. We can now savely use the notation
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
for any of them.
Note that each of the constructions adds some additional information. So
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
provides the information that
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is a Dwyer-Kan localization. To understand the functor
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
does not require to understand asymptotic morphisms so that this construction may make equivariant
E
-theory more accessible to homotopy theory oriented readers. Finally, the construction of the functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
is crucial for our proof of the main
Theorem

1.5
since
it allows to follow through the consequences of the existence of shape systems for separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras to the existence of shapes of the objects in
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
.

In
Section

3.7
we finally show our main
Theorem

1.5
.
The idea is to present
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
as the result of a sequence of left- and right Bousfield localizations with filtered colimit preserving right adjoints of
the category
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})
. We then use that a category of the form
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(\dots)
is always compactly generated and therefore compactly assembled, and that the localizations preserve the property of being compactly assembled.
In addition in
3.69
we describe explicit representatives for the shapes of objects in
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
or
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

In
Section

3.8
we discuss further properties of the category
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. Using the classical example of Skandalis
[
Ska88
]
we observe in
3.85
.
2
that

\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is strictly larger than its bootstrap class.
This shows that
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is compactly assembled for a non-trivial reason. The question whether

\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is even rigid remains open for the moment. But in
3.85
.
4
-
5
. we can answer the analogous question for

KK
-theory by showing that
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
with one of
\otimes
or
\otimes_{\min}
is not rigid.

As an application of
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
being compactly assembled we can show that
a map of separable
C^{*}
-algebras
f:A\to B
is an
E
-theory equivalence if and only if it is inverted by all functors
L:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{{\mathrm{sep}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
that are
K
-stable, homotopy invariant, exact and preserve filtered colimits
3.93
. Note that these are precisely the formal properties of the
K
-theory functor, see
3.92
.

In
Section

4
we study topologies on the morphism sets
[A,B]
of the homotopy category of an
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
with countable filtered colimits.
In
Section

4.1
we introduce (under the additional assumption of the existence of finite products) the light condensed topology. The name is motivated by the recent development of
condensed mathematics
advertised by D. Clausen and P. Scholze. If
X
is a light profinite space (i.e., countable cofiltered limit
X\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{i\in I}X_{i}
of finite discrete spaces), then we can define the power
B^{X}:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I^{\mathrm{op}}}\prod_{X_{i}}B
. Given an element of
[A,B^{X}]
the composition with the evaluations at the points of
X
gives a map
X\to[A,B]
.
The light condensed topology on the morphism sets is defined by declaring all these maps to be continuous.

In
Section

4.2
we introduce a second topology called the shape topology on the morphisms sets by the condition that
[A,B]\to[A^{\prime},B]^{\mathrm{disc}}
should be continuous for all weakly compact morphisms
{A^{\prime}\to A}
.
We will see that in the presence of weakly compact approximations (so in particular if
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly assembled) the light condensed topology coincides with the shape topology. This interplay has nice consequences:
the topology is fist countable, the composition map is continuous, the set of converging sequences in
[A,B]
is modelled by the set
[A,B^{{\rm Nature}_{+}}]
, and in the stable case
[A,B]
is a topological group. The study of the topological morphism sets of
\mathrm{ho}({\mathbf{D}})
is just a one-categorical reflection of a light condensed anima structure on the mapping anima of
{\mathbf{D}}
which will be investigated in a future work.

In
Section

4.3
we define a category
\bar{\mathbf{D}}
by forming the Hausdorff quotient of the morphism spaces. If
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly assembled, then
\bar{\mathbf{D}}
is topologically enriched, i.e., the composition is continuous. Furthermore, the functor
\mathrm{ho}({\mathbf{D}})\to\bar{\mathbf{D}}
preserves filtered colimits
4.39
and is conservative by
4.41
provided that
{\mathbf{D}}
is stable.

Again we formulate the statements in
Section

4
for general

\infty
-categories admitting countable filtered colimits and require additional assumptions about existence of weakly compact approximations etc locally in order to make it applicable e.g. to categories like
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
which are not known to be compactly assembled.

In the final
Section

4.4
we specialize the results of the preceding sections to the categories
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and explain how they are related with the results of
[
CS24
]
. A new aspect in the case of
C^{*}
-algebras is that one can relate the analysis in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
directly with analysis in the
\infty
-categories
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
or
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
(in the sense of condensed mathematics). This applies e.g. to the construction of function objects.
By
4.42
for every
B
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and light profinite space
X
we have equivalences

a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(C(X,B))\simeq a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(B)^{X}\ ,\qquad\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(C(X,B))\simeq\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(B)^{X}\ .

Furthermore, by
4.44
the functors

a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}\ ,\qquad\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}

are continuous with respect to the topological enrichments of the domain (point-norm topology on the sets of homomorphisms) and the shape topology on the morphism sets.

2
\infty
-categories

2.1
Compact maps

In this section we introduce variants of the concept of a compact map in the context of countably cocomplete
\infty
-categories.
As a preparation we introduce the following two conditions on commutative squares in the
\infty
-category of anima
\mathbf{An}
.
We call the bold commutative square

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

of anima liftable if it admits the dashed completion. Note that this involves the condition that
the composition of the fillers of the two triangles is equivalent to the filler of the square.
Equivalently, a bold square as above
is liftable if and only if the associated bold diagram

\textstyle{K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y}

(2.1)

in the slice category
\mathbf{An}_{/Y}
of anima over
Y
admits a dashed filler.

Definition 2.1
.

We say that the upper bold commutative square

\textstyle{X_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y_{0}}
\textstyle{X_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

satisfies the shifted lifting property (slp), if for every dotted square with finite anima
K
and
L

the outer square is liftable as indicated by the dashed arrow.

If we fix
K\to L
, then we will also say that it has the slp for
K\to L
.

We consider an
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
which admits all countable filtered colimits.

By a system
(Z_{i})_{i\in I}
in
{\mathbf{D}}
we mean a functor
Z:I\to{\mathbf{D}}
from a countable filtered
\infty
-category.

We consider a morphism
f:A\to B
in
{\mathbf{D}}
.
If
(Z_{i})_{i\in I}
is a system in
{\mathbf{D}}
, then we can consider the square of anima

\textstyle{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(A,Z_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(A,\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}Z_{i})}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(B,Z_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(B,\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}Z_{i})}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}

(2.2)

Definition 2.2
.

The morphism
f:A\to B
is called:

1.

strongly compact if (
2.2
) is liftable

2.

slp compact, if (
2.2
) has the slp

3.

weakly compact if (
2.2
) has the slp for
\emptyset\to*

for every system
(Z_{i})_{i\in I}
.

For all three versions of compactness the sets of corresponding morphisms form a two-sided ideal in
{\mathbf{D}}
.
We have the following implications:

\mbox{strongly compact}\implies\mbox{slp compact}\implies\mbox{weakly compact}\ .

If
A
is a compact object, then any map
A\to B
is strongly compact since the upper map in (
2.2
) is an equivalence.

Remark 2.3
.

The condition of being weakly compact is equivalent to the condition that
for every
system
(Z_{i})_{i\in I}

and bold part of the diagram

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Z_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}Z_{i}}

(2.3)

there exists
k
in
I
and a commutative completion by the dotted part.
∎

Remark 2.4
.

Strongly and weakly compact maps are usually considered in the context of large presentable
\infty
-categories
[
KNP
]
where the definitions are attributed to J. Lurie and D. Clausen.
In this case one drops the countability condition for the systems
(Z_{i})_{i\in I}
.

A typical example for the
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
is the category of
\aleph_{1}
-compact
objects in a large presentable
\infty
-category. In this case (weak) compactness of a morphism in
{\mathbf{D}}
is equivalent to (weak) compactness (as defined in
[
KNP
]
) of the morphism considered in the large category.

We introduce the intermediate notion of slp compactness since it is the slp condition which can
directly be checked in our applications to
C^{*}
-algebras, see e.g.
3.8
.

∎

Remark 2.5
.

If
{\mathbf{D}}
is a stable countably cocomplete
\infty
-category, then weak compactness of a morphism
morphism
f:A\to B
can also be characterized by the following conditions:

1.

For any system
(F_{j})_{j\in J}
with
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{j\in J}F_{j}\simeq 0
and map
B\to F_{k}
for some
k
in
J
there exists
l
in
J
with a morphism
k\to l
such that

A\xrightarrow{f}B\to F_{k}\to F_{l}
vanishes.

2.

For every family
(Y_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
{\mathbf{D}}
and bold part of the diagram

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{n=0}^{k}Y_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{n\in{\rm Nature}}Y_{n}}

there exists an integer
k
and the dotted completion.

Furthermore, in the stable case it follows from
2.27
that
weakly compact maps are slp compact.

∎

The following is straighforward to check for all three cases of
?
in {strongly, slp, weakly}.

Lemma 2.6
.

A left-adjoint functor
L:{\mathbf{D}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
with a countable colimit-preserving right-adjoint
R:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{D}}
preserves
?
-compact maps.

2.2
Shapes and compactly assembled
\infty
-categories

In this section we introduce the concept of a shape of an object in an
\infty
-category which admits countable filtered colimits.
The word shape is motivated by our application of the theory to
C^{*}
-algebras culminating in
3.69
asserting
that a shape system for a
C^{*}
-algebra represents the shape of its image in equivariant
E
-theory.

The condition that every object of
{\mathbf{D}}
admits a shape turns out to be one of many equivalent characterizations of
{\mathbf{D}}
being compactly assembled. But since we also want to apply the theory to the stable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{KK}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
from
[
BEL
]
which is not known to be compactly assembled
we develop the notions as much as possible locally in
{\mathbf{D}}
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}
be a small
\infty
-category admitting all countable filtered colimits.
By
y:{\mathbf{D}}\to{\mathtt{Ind}}({\mathbf{D}})
we denote the ind-completion of
{\mathbf{D}}
. We then let

{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})
denote the full subcategory of
{\mathtt{Ind}}({\mathbf{D}})
obtained as the closure of the image of
y
under countable filtered colimits. Note
that
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})
is essentially small.

From now one we consider the functor
y:{\mathbf{D}}\to{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})
.
Below we will use the following facts.

1.

For every object in
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})
there exists a system
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
{\mathbf{D}}
such that

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{n\in{\rm Nature}}}y(A_{{n}})\simeq A\ .

This can be shown using
[
Lur09
, Prop. 5.3.1.18]
.

2.

The functor
y
is fully faithful and preserves all limits.

3.

The objects in the image of
y
are compact.

4.

If
{\mathbf{D}}
is stable, then so is
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})
and
y
preserves finite colimits.

Since we assume that
{\mathbf{D}}
admits all countable filtered colimits
we have an adjunction

|-|:{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})\leftrightarrows{\mathbf{D}}:y\ ,

(2.4)

where
|-|
is called the realization.
Since
y
is fully faithful the counit of this adjunction is an equivalence

|-|\circ y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\ .

(2.5)

Definition 2.7
.

We say that an object
A
in
{\mathbf{D}}
admits a shape if the functor

{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(A,|-|):{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})\to\mathbf{An}

is corepresentable.
We let
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}}
denote the full subcategory of objects which admit a shape.

By definition, the shape of
A
is an object
S(A)
in
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})
together with a map

\eta_{A}:A\to|S(A)|
such that the induced map

{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})}(S(A),\hat{B})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle|-|}}{{\to}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(|S(A)|,|\hat{B}|)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\eta_{A}^{\ast}}}}{{\to}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(A,|\hat{B}|)

(2.6)

is an equivalence for all
\hat{B}
in
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})
.
The collection of shapes and maps assemble to the shape functor
and natural transformation

S:{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}}\to{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})\ ,\qquad\eta:\mathrm{incl}_{{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}}\to{\mathbf{D}}}\to|-|\circ S\ .

(2.7)

Lemma 2.8
.

The transformation
\eta
in (
2.7
) is an equivalence.

Proof.

We consider
A
in
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}}
and
B
in
{\mathbf{D}}
and the following diagram

\textstyle{{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})}(S(A),y(B))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{|-|}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(|S(A)|,|y(B)|)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{counit}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{A}^{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(A,|y(B)|)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{counit}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(|S(A)|,B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{A}^{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(A,B)}

where the equivalences denoted by
counit
are induced by post-composition with the counit map of the adjunction (
2.4
). This adjunction also implies that that the upper right diagonal map is an equivalence.
The upper left diagonal map is the equivalence (
2.6
) applied to
\hat{B}:=y(B)
.
We conclude from the upper right triangle that the upper vertical map
|-|
is an equivalence.
Consequently the left diagonal map
\eta_{A}^{*}
is an equivalence.
We finally conclude that the right diagonal map
\eta_{A}^{*}
is an equivalence.
This implies the assertion.
∎

Lemma 2.9
.

{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}}
is closed under countable filtered colimits.
If
{\mathbf{D}}
is stable, then so is
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}}
.

Proof.

Let
(A_{i})_{i\in I}
be a system in
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}}
.
Then using (
2.6
) one checks that
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}S(A_{i})
represents a shape for
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}A_{i}
.
Hence
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}}
is closed under countable filtered colimits.
If
{\mathbf{D}}
is stable, then one furthermore checks that
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}}
is closed under finite colimits, and
that
\Omega S(A)
represents the shape of
\Omega A
.
Consequently
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}}
is a stable subcategory of
{\mathbf{D}}
.
∎

Lemma 2.10
.

We assume that
A
is in
{\mathbf{D}}
,
B
is in
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}}
, and that
f:y(A)\to S(B)
is any map. Then the map

|y(A)|\xrightarrow{|f|}|S(B)|

(2.8)

is strongly compact.

Proof.

Let
(Z_{i})_{i\in I}

{\mathbf{D}}
.

(2.9)

Here, we have implicitly used the natural identifications
\lvert y(Z_{i})\rvert\simeq Z_{i}
and
\lvert\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}y(Z_{i})\rvert\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}Z_{i}
.
The marked map is an equivalence since
y(A)
is compact, and the two lower vertical maps are equivalences due to the universal property of the shape (
2.6
). It is straightforward to see that the composite of the vertical maps is given by
\lvert f\rvert^{\ast}
. Using that the marked map is an equivalence one find the desired dashed arrow. ∎

Lemma 2.11
.

If
A,B
are in
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}}
, then any weakly compact map
f:A\to B
is automatically strongly compact.

Proof.

Let
f:A\to B
be a weakly compact map. Then
S(f):S(A)\to S(B)
is
weakly compact by
2.6
. We consider a system

(B_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
{\mathbf{D}}
such that
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}y(B_{n})\simeq S(B)
. Using
2.3
we see that there exists

n
in
{\rm Nature}
such that
S(f)
factorizes over
S(A)\to y(B_{n})\to S(B)
. We recover
f
by applying
|-|

and use
2.10
in order to conclude that
f
is strongly compact.
∎

Definition 2.12
.

We say that
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly assembled if
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}}={\mathbf{D}}
.

If
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly assembled, then the shape functor is the left-adjoint in the adjunction

S:{\mathbf{D}}\leftrightarrows{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}}):|-|\ ,\qquad\eta:{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}|-|\circ S

(2.10)

whose
unit
\eta
is an equivalence by
2.8
.

Remark 2.13
.

A small compactly assembled
\infty
-category is the category of
\aleph_{1}
-compact objects in a
large
\infty
-category which is compactly assembled in the sense of Lurie
[
Lurb
, Sec. 21.1.2.]
. The latter notion
is an
\infty
-categorical version of the notion of a continuous category by Johnstone-Joyal
[
JJ82
]
.

∎

The following is a consequence of
2.11
.

Corollary 2.14
.

If
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly assembled, then weakly compact morphisms are automatically strongly compact.

Thus in a compactly assembled category all our compactness variants from
2.2
coincide.

Let now
{\mathbf{D}}
be an
\infty
-category admitting countable filtered colimits and
S:{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}}\to{\mathbf{D}}
be the partial shape functor from (
2.7
).
If
{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}
is a full subcategory of
{\mathbf{D}}
, then we can consider
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}}^{\prime})
as a full subcategory of
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})
.
In the following we will construct the maximal compactly assembled full subcategory
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{casmbl}}
of
{\mathbf{D}}
, see
2.18
for a precise statement.
Note that in general we do not expect that the full subcategory
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}}
of
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly assembled since it is not clear that the functor
S
takes values in the full subcategory
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}})
of
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})
.
We therefore define by transfinite recursion a decreasing family
({\mathbf{D}}^{(i)})_{i}
of full subcategories
of
{\mathbf{D}}
. We set
{\mathbf{D}}^{(0)}:={\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}}
. Let
i
be an ordinal and assume that
{\mathbf{D}}^{(j)}
has been defined for all ordinals
j<i
.

1.

If
i
is a successor ordinal, then
i=j+1
for some ordinal
j
and we define
{\mathbf{D}}^{(i)}
to be the subcategory of
{\mathbf{D}}^{(j)}
on objects
A
with
S(A)
in
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}}^{(j)})
.

2.

If
i
is a limit ordinal, then we define
{\mathbf{D}}^{(i)}:=\bigcap_{j<i}{\mathbf{D}}^{(j)}
.

Since
{\mathbf{D}}
is small there exists an ordinal
i^{\prime}
such that
{\mathbf{D}}^{(i^{\prime})}={\mathbf{D}}^{(i^{\prime}+1)}
. We then define the full subcategory

{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{casmbl}}:={\mathbf{D}}^{(i^{\prime})}

of
{\mathbf{D}}
. This is independent of the choice of
i^{\prime}
.

Lemma 2.15
.

The subcategory
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{casmbl}}
is closed under countable filtered colimits and compactly assembled. If
{\mathbf{D}}
is stable, then so is
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{casmbl}}
.

Proof.

The subcategory
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}}
is closed under countable filtered colimits by
2.9
.
Since the functor
S
preserves filtered colimits we can conclude by transfinite induction that

{\mathbf{D}}^{(i)}
is closed under countable filtered colimits for every ordinal
i
.
This implies that
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{casmbl}}
is closed under countable filtered colimits.
By construction, if
A
is in
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{casmbl}}
, then
S(A)\in{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{casmbl}})
. Consequently,

{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{casmbl}}
is compactly assembled.

For stability we argue similarly, using the second assertion of
2.9
.
∎

Let
F:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{D}}
be a countable filtered colimit preserving functor between
\infty
-categories addmitting countable filtered colimits.
It essentially uniquely extends to a countable filtered colimit preserving functor

\hat{F}
fitting into

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 23.59717pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.15276pt\raise 29.65166pt\raise 5.39168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{F}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 64.04158pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-16.08778pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{y_{{\mathbf{C}}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.18555pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 64.04158pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbf{D}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 71.45128pt\raise-16.08778pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{y_{{\mathbf{D}}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 71.45128pt\raise-23.18555pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-23.59717pt\raise-32.17557pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.94785pt\raise-25.56445pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{F}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.59717pt\raise-32.17557pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 47.59717pt\raise-32.17557pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

(2.11)

The condition that
F
preserves countable filtered colimits is equivalent to the condition that the natural comparison morphism is an equivalence

\lvert-\rvert_{\mathbf{D}}\circ\hat{F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}F\circ\lvert-\rvert_{\mathbf{C}}\ .

(2.12)

Definition 2.16
.

We say that
F
preserves shapes if for every
A
in
{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{shp}}
the object
\hat{F}(S_{{\mathbf{C}}}(A))
in

{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})
together with the map

F(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F(\eta_{A})}}{{\to}}F(|S_{{\mathbf{C}}}(A)|_{{\mathbf{C}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}|\hat{F}(S_{{\mathbf{C}}}(A))|_{{\mathbf{D}}}

represents the shape of
F(A)
.

Note that for a shape preserving functor
F
we have

F({\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{shp}})\subseteq{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{shp}}\ .

The following result provides examples of shape preserving functors.
Assume that

L:{\mathbf{C}}\leftrightarrows{\mathbf{D}}:R

is an adjunction.

Lemma 2.17
.

If
R
preserves countable filtered colimits, then

L
preserves shapes.

Proof.

We have an induced adjunction

\hat{L}:{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})\leftrightarrows{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}}):\hat{R}\ .

The equivalence of functors
{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{shp},\mathrm{op}}\times{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})\to\mathbf{An}

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})}(\hat{L}(S_{{\mathbf{C}}}(-)),-)\simeq{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})}(S_{{\mathbf{C}}}(-),\hat{R}(-))

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(-,|\hat{R}(-)|_{{\mathbf{C}}})

\displaystyle\ \simeq{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(-,R(|-|_{{\mathbf{D}}}))

\displaystyle\>\>\simeq{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(L(-),|-|_{{\mathbf{D}}})

sends for every
A
in
{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{shp}}
the identity
{\mathtt{id}}_{\hat{L}(S_{{\mathbf{C}}}(A))}
to the map

L(\eta_{A}):L(A)\to|\hat{L}(S_{{\mathbf{C}}}(A))|_{{\mathbf{D}}}
which presents

\hat{L}(S_{{\mathbf{C}}}(A))
as the shape of
L(A)
.
∎

Let
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{shp}}
be the subcategory of
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{\infty}
of small
\infty
-categories admitting countable filtered colimits
and countable filtered colimit and shape preserving functors and

{\mathbf{Cat}}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{casmbl}}
be the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{shp}}
of compactly assembled categories.
Similarly, we let
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{ex},\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{shp}}
be the subcategory of
{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{ex}}_{\infty}
of small countably cocomplete stable
\infty
-categories
and exact, countable filtered colimit and shape preserving functors and

{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{ex},\mathrm{casmbl}}_{\infty}
be its full subcategory
of compactly assembled categories.

Proposition 2.18
.

We have right Bousfield localizations

\mathrm{incl}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{casmbl}}\leftrightarrows{\mathbf{Cat}}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{shp}}:(-)^{\mathrm{casmbl}}

and

\mathrm{incl}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{ex},\mathrm{casmbl}}\leftrightarrows{\mathbf{Cat}}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{ex},\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{shp}}:(-)^{\mathrm{casmbl}}\ .

Proof.

We first show that the construction
{\mathbf{D}}\mapsto{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{casmbl}}
extends to a functor.
In the statement below we use the following notation: If
\Phi:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
is a functor between
\infty
-categories and
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
is a full subcategory of
{\mathcal{D}}
, then we write
\Phi({\mathcal{C}})\subseteq{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
for the assertion that the essential image of
\Phi
is contained in
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
.

Lemma 2.19
.

If
F:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{D}}
preserves shapes, then
F({\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{casmbl}})\subseteq{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{casmbl}}
.

Proof.

We argue by transfinite induction that
F({\mathbf{C}}^{(i)})\subseteq{\mathbf{D}}^{(i)}
.
For
i=0
the assertion is true by assumption.
Let
i
be an ordinal.

1.

If
i
is a successor ordinal, then
i=j+1
for some ordinal
j
. Then we have
F({\mathbf{C}}^{(j)})\subseteq{\mathbf{D}}^{(j)}
by induction hypothesis. Let
A
be in
{\mathbf{C}}^{(i)}
so that
S_{{\mathbf{C}}}(A)\in{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}}^{(j)})
.
Then
S_{{\mathbf{D}}}(F(A))\simeq\hat{F}(S_{{\mathbf{C}}}(A))\in\hat{F}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}}^{(j)}))\subseteq{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}}^{(j)})
.
Hence
F(A)\in{\mathbf{D}}^{(i)}
.

2.

If
i
is a limit ordinal, then clearly

F({\mathbf{C}}^{(i)})=F(\bigcap_{j<i}{\mathbf{C}}^{(j)})\subseteq\bigcap_{{j<i}}F({\mathbf{C}}^{(j)})\subseteq\bigcap_{{j<i}}{\mathbf{D}}^{(j)}={\mathbf{D}}^{(i)}\ .

∎

In the following statement the superscripts
\mathrm{ex}
,
\mathrm{cfil}
,
\mathrm{shp}
stand for exact,
countable filtered colimit, and shape preserving functors respectively.
Note that the inclusion
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{casmbl}}\to{\mathbf{D}}
preserves countable filtered colimits and shapes
(and also finite colimits and limits in the stable case).
The following result completes the proof of
2.18
.

Lemma 2.20
.

If
{\mathbf{C}}
is compactly assembled and
{\mathbf{D}}
is an
\infty
-category admitting countable filtered colimits, then
the inclusion
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{casmbl}}\to{\mathbf{D}}
induces an equivalence

{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{shp}}({\mathbf{C}},{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{casmbl}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{shp}}({\mathbf{C}},{\mathbf{D}}).

If
{\mathbf{C}}
and
{\mathbf{D}}
are in addition stable, then the inclusion also induces an equivalence

{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{ex},\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{shp}}({\mathbf{C}},{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{casmbl}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{ex},\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{shp}}({\mathbf{C}},{\mathbf{D}})

Proof.

Let
F:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{D}}
be a countable colimit and shape preserving functor. We must show that

F
takes values in
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{casmbl}}
. But since
{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{casmbl}}={\mathbf{C}}
this immediately follows from
2.19
.
The argument in the stable case is analoguous.
∎

∎

2.3
Compact approximations and exhaustions

We consider an
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
admitting countable filtered colimits.
In this section we characterize the existence of a shape of an object
A
of
{\mathbf{D}}
in terms of the existence of suitable exhaustions of
A
. Conversely we discuss the consequences of the assumption that
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly assembled to the existence of exhaustions of its objects.

In the following we will say that a system
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
{\mathbf{D}}
is an approximation of
A:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}A_{n}
.
Let
?
be in {weakly, slp, strongly}.

Definition 2.21
.

1.

A ?-compact approximation of
A
in
{\mathbf{D}}
is an approximation
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
such that the structure maps
A_{i}\to A
are ?-compact for all
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.

2.

A ?-compact exhaustion of
A
is an approximation
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
such that
the structure maps
A_{n}\to A_{n+1}
are ?-compact for all
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.

Note that a ?-compact exhaustion is a ?-compact approximation.

The following result gives a criterion when an approximation represents the shape of an object
A
of
{\mathbf{D}}
.

Proposition 2.22
.

The object
A
admits a shape if and only if it admits an approximation
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
such that the canonical map

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(A_{n},Z_{i})\to{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(A,\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}Z_{i})

(2.13)

is an equivalence for all systems
(Z_{i})_{i\in I}
in
{\mathbf{D}}
. In this case
S(A)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}y(A_{n})
.

Proof.

We first assume that
A
admits a shape
(S(A),\eta_{A})
. By
2.8

the map
\eta_{A}:|S(A)|\to A
is an equivalence. There exists a system
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
{\mathbf{D}}
with

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}y(A_{n})\simeq S(A)\ .

(2.14)

We apply
|-|
and compose with
\eta_{A}
in order to get an equivalence

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}A_{n}\simeq A
, i.e,
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
is an approximation of
A
.
Finally (
2.13
)
is the composition of the two equivalences

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(A_{n},Z_{i})\simeq{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}y(A_{n}),\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}y(Z_{i}))\xrightarrow{\eta_{A}^{*}\circ\lvert-\rvert}{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(A,\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}Z_{i})\ .

(2.15)

For the converse we assume that
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
is an approximation of
A
such that (
2.13
) is an equivalence.
Then we set
S(A):=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}y(A_{n})

and define the equivalence
\eta_{A}:|S(A)|\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}A_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}A
.
We conclude that the second map in (
2.15
) is an equivalence showing that
(S(A),\eta_{A})
is a shape of
A
.
∎

Let
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
be an approximation of
A
.

Corollary 2.23
.

If

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}y(A_{n})
represents the shape of
A
, then
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
is a weakly compact approximation.

Proof.

We apply
\pi_{0}
to the equivalence (
2.13
). ∎

The following theorem shows that the converse of
2.23
holds under a stronger assumption.

Theorem 2.24
.

If
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
is a slp-compact exhaustion of
A
, then
A
admits a shape and
S(A)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}y(A_{n})
.

Proof.

We use the criterion
2.22
.
We consider a system
(Z_{i})_{i\in I}
and set
Z:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}Z_{i}
. By assumption the map of systems

(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(A_{n},Z_{i}))_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}\to({\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(A_{n},Z))_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}\ .

has the slp in the sense of
Section

2.4
.
Hence it follows from
Theorem

2.30
below
that

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(A_{n},Z_{i})\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(A_{n},\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}Z_{i})

(2.16)

is an equivalence.
∎

Corollary 2.25
.

If every object of
{\mathbf{D}}
admits an slp-compact exhaustion, then
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly assembled.

Theorem 2.26
.

If
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly assembled, then
every object of
{\mathbf{D}}
admits a strongly compact exhaustion.

Proof.

We fix
A
in
{\mathbf{D}}
. Then there exists a system
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
{\mathbf{D}}

such that

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}y(A_{n})\simeq S(A)\ .

Let
\epsilon:S\circ\lvert-\rvert\to{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
be the counit of the adjunction (
2.10
).
Since
S
is a right Bousfield localization the map

\epsilon_{S(\lvert y(A)\rvert)}:S(\lvert S(\lvert y(A)\rvert)\rvert)\to S(\lvert y(A)\rvert)
is an equivalence. Since the target and domain of
\epsilon
commute with countable filtered colimits
\epsilon_{S(\lvert y(A)\rvert)}
is the colimit of the system of maps
(\epsilon_{y(A_{n})}:S(\lvert y(A_{n}){\rvert)}\to y(A_{n}))_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
. The commutative diagram

\hskip-28.45274pt\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern implies that the lower horizontal map is an equivalence.
We interpret the family
({\mathtt{id}}_{y(A_{n})})_{n}
as an element of the right lower corner.
It has a pre-image
(f_{n})_{n}
in the left lower corner. Then
f_{n}
is a preimage of
{\mathtt{id}}_{y(A_{n})}
under

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{k{\in{\rm Nature}}}\epsilon_{y(A_{k}),\ast}:\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{k\in{\rm Nature}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})}(y(A_{n}),S(|y(A_{k})|))\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{k\in{\rm Nature}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})}(y(A_{n}),y(A_{k}))\ .

It induces a factorization

\textstyle{S(|y(A_{k(n)})|)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon_{y(A_{k(n)})}}
\textstyle{y(A_{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{y(A_{k(n)})}

for some
k(n)\geq n+1
. Therefore for all
n
in
{\rm Nature}
the structure map
A_{n}\to A_{k(n)}
is strongly compact by
2.10
and (
2.5
).
We can conclude that a cofinal subsystem of the system
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
is a strongly compact exhaustion of
A
.
∎

In the following we clarify the relation between weakly compact maps and slp compact maps.

Proposition 2.27
.

If
{\mathbf{D}}
admits finite limits and filtered colimits in
{\mathbf{D}}
commute with finite limits, then weakly compact maps are slp compact.

Proof.

We start with a general criterion for a square of anima to be liftable: The solid part of a square of anima

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

induces a point
\ast\to X^{K}\times_{Y^{K}}Y^{L}
and a canonical comparison map
X^{L}\to X^{K}\times_{Y^{K}}Y^{L}
. The above diagram admits a dashed lift if and only if the solid diagram below admits a dashed lift

Let

be a weakly compact map in
{\mathbf{D}}
and

a system with colimit Z. We have to solve the lifting problem

for all finite anima

. Due to the above criterion and the finiteness of

and

(2.17)

In order to obtain such a lift we consider the diagram

31.0434pt\raise-32.91226pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Z_{i}^{K}\times_{Z^{K}}Z^{L}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ ,

(2.18)

where the marked map is induced by the given point in
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(A,Z_{i}^{K}\times_{Z^{K}}Z^{L})
. We can now use the weak compactness of
f
and

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{(i\to j)\in I_{i/}}Z_{i}^{K}\times_{Z_{j}^{K}}Z_{j}^{L}\simeq Z_{i}^{K}\times_{Z^{K}}Z^{L}

in order to obtain the dashed arrow in (
2.18
). The composite

\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z_{i}^{K}\times_{Z_{j}^{K}}Z_{j}^{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathtt{pr}}_{Z_{j}^{L}}}
\textstyle{Z_{j}^{L}}

interpreted as a point in
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(B,Z_{i}^{L})
provides an appropriate lift in (
2.17
).
∎

This provides another argument for a Theorem of Clausen and Lurie.

Theorem 2.28

(Clausen, Lurie, see
[
KNP
, Thm. 2.2.11, Lemma 2.3.13]
)
.

We assume that
{\mathbf{D}}
is left exact, admits countable filtered colimits, and filtered colimits in
{\mathbf{D}}
commute with finite limits. If
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
is a weakly compact exhaustion of
A
, then
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}y(A_{n})
represents the shape of
A
.

Proof.

This is a consequence of
2.27
and
Theorem

2.24
.
∎

2.4
slp maps between towers of anima

The main result of this technical section is
Theorem

2.30
which goes into the proofs of
Theorem

2.24
and
Theorem

3.41
.

Definition 2.29
.

A map
(X_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}\to(Y_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}({\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathbf{An})

has the slp (shifted lifting property) if for every
n
in
{\rm Nature}
the commutative square

\textstyle{X_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y_{n}}
\textstyle{X_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

has the slp in the sense of
2.1
.

Theorem 2.30
.

If a map
f:(X_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}\to(Y_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}({\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathbf{An})
satisfies the slp, then the induced map

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}X_{n}\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}Y_{n}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

The idea is to show that the map induces a bijection on the level of
\pi_{0}
and isomorphisms
on homotopy groups for all choices of base points. For the argument we need some closure properties
of the class of squares with the slp. As a preparation for their derivation
we
consider an adjunction

L:{\mathbf{A}}\rightleftarrows{\mathbf{B}}:R

(2.19)

between
\infty
-categories. Furthermore let
f:A\to A^{\prime}
be a map in
{\mathbf{A}}
and
B
be an object in
{\mathbf{B}}
.

Lemma 2.31
.

The following are equivalent:

1.

The diagram

\textstyle{R(B)}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

admits a dashed completion.

2.

The diagram

\textstyle{B}
\textstyle{L(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{counit\circ L(g)}
\scriptstyle{L(f)}
\textstyle{L(A^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

admits a dashed completion.

Proof.

1
and get the triangle from Assertion
2
by applying
L
and post-composing the counit:

The left deformed triangle commutes due to the triangle identities for the adjunction (
2.19
).
∎

Lemma 2.32
.

Let

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

be a commutative diagram of anima such that the upper square is cartesian and the outer square is liftable. Then also the lower square
is liftable.

Proof.

Using (
2.1
) it suffices to show that there exists a commutative diagram

\textstyle{X^{\prime}}
\textstyle{K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

in
\mathbf{An}_{/Y^{\prime}}
(we drop the structure maps to
Y^{\prime}
). There exists an adjunction

f_{\sharp}:\mathbf{An}_{/Y^{\prime}}\leftrightarrows\mathbf{An}_{/Y}:f^{\ast},

where
f_{\sharp}
is given by postcomposition with
f
and
f^{\ast}
is given by pullback along
f
. We have an equivalence
f^{\ast}X\simeq X^{\prime}
. By
2.31
it suffices to solve the lifting problem

\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{f_{\sharp}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f_{\sharp}(L),\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

and this is possible by our assumption.
∎

For the following corollary it is convenient to identify a commutative square

\textstyle{X_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{0}}
\textstyle{Y_{0}}
\textstyle{X_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{Y_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

with a map
f:X\to Y
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(\Delta^{1,\mathrm{op}},\mathbf{An})
. We call
f
liftable, if the associated square is liftable.

Corollary 2.33
.

1.

The class of morphisms in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(\Delta^{1,\mathrm{op}},\mathbf{An})
satisfying the slp is closed under pullbacks.

2.

The class of morphisms in
{\mathbf{Fun}}({\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathbf{An})
satisfying the slp is closed under pullbacks.

We consider the adjunction

\Sigma_{+}:\mathbf{An}\rightleftarrows\mathbf{An}_{\ast/}:\Omega\ .

(2.20)

Let
f:(X,x)\to(Y,y)
be a map of pointed anima.

Lemma 2.34
.

The square

\textstyle{\Omega(X,x)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Omega f}
\textstyle{\Omega(Y,y)}
\textstyle{K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(2.21)

is liftable if and only if the associated square

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{\Sigma_{+}K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma_{+}L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

is liftable.

Proof.

We have an adjunction

(\Sigma_{+})_{/{(Y,y)}}:\mathbf{An}_{/\Omega(Y,y)}\rightleftarrows(\mathbf{An}_{\ast})_{/(Y,y)}:\Omega_{/(Y,y)}.

Here the non-obvious functor
(\Sigma_{+})_{/(Y,y)}
is given by the composite

\mathbf{An}_{/\Omega(Y,y)}\xrightarrow{(\Sigma_{+})_{/\Omega(Y,y)}}(\mathbf{An}_{\ast})_{/\Sigma_{+}(\Omega(Y,y))}\xrightarrow{counit_{\sharp}}(\mathbf{An}_{\ast})_{/(Y,y)},

where
counit_{\sharp}
is postcomposition with the counit
\Sigma_{+}(\Omega(Y,y))\to(Y,y)
of the adjunction (
2.20
). In view of

2.31
the square (
2.21
) is liftable if and only if the dashed completion

\textstyle{(X,x)}
\textstyle{\Sigma_{+}K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma_{+}L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

exists in
(\mathbf{An}_{\ast})_{/(Y,y)}
. Such a completion exists if and only if the underlying diagram in
\mathbf{An}_{/Y}
admits the corresponding dashed lift.
∎

We consider a commutative square

\textstyle{(X_{0},x_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(Y_{0},y_{0})}
\textstyle{(X_{1},x_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(Y_{1},y_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(2.22)

in
\mathbf{An}_{\ast/}
.

Corollary 2.35
.

1.

If the underlying square of (
2.22
) in
\mathbf{An}
has the
slp, then also

\textstyle{\Omega(X_{0},x_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega(Y_{0},y_{0})}
\textstyle{\Omega(X_{1},x_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega(Y_{1},y_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

has the slp.

2.

If
f:(X_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}\to(Y_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}
has the slp, then for all compatible choices of base points
x:{(x_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}}\to(X_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}},
the induced map

(\Omega(X_{n},x_{n}))_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}\to(\Omega(Y_{n},f(x_{n})))_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}

also has the slp.

Remark 2.36
.

In this remark
we recall the properties of the
\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}^{1}
-sequence.

1.

For every tower
(X_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}
and a compatible system of base points
x:{(x_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}}\to(X_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
there exists an exact sequence

\ast\to{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}}^{1}\pi_{1}(X_{n},x_{n})\to\pi_{0}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}X_{n},{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}x_{n}})\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}\pi_{0}(X_{n},x_{n})\to\ast

(2.23)

of pointed sets.

2.

The functor
{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}}^{1}
admits a factorization

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 53.56242pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-53.56242pt\raise
