This follows from the formula
{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}}^{1}G_{n}\cong\pi_{0}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}BG_{n})

for any system
(G_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}({\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathbf{Group})
.

3.

For any tower
(X_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}
in
\mathbf{An}
the map
\pi_{0}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}X_{n})\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}\pi_{0}(X_{n})
is surjective.

We start with a computation on
\pi_{0}
.

Lemma 2.37
.

If
(X_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}\to\underline{\ast}
has the slp, then
(\pi_{0}(X_{n}))_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}
is isomorphic to
{\underline{\ast}}
as a pro-system.

Proof.

First note that
\pi_{0}(X_{n})
is non-empty for all
n
in
{\rm Nature}
as the diagram

\textstyle{X_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast}
\textstyle{X_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\emptyset\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

admits a dashed lift. Secondly, the map
\pi_{0}(X_{n+1})\to\pi_{0}(X_{n})
is constant since for every map
S^{0}\to X_{n+1}
there exists a dashed lift

\textstyle{X_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast}
\textstyle{X_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

∎

We can now start the actual proof of
Theorem

2.30
.
Let
(X_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}\to(Y_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}
have the slp. Since slp maps are stable under pullback by
2.33
.
2
and an equivalence of anima is a map with trivial fibres at all base points, we can reduce to the case where
(Y_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}\simeq\underline{\ast}
. As slp maps are stable under loops with respect to each compatible system of base points by
2.35
.
2
, it suffices to check that
\pi_{0}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}X_{n})\cong\ast
. Since
\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}\pi_{0}(X_{n})\cong*
by
2.37
, we know by
2.36
.
3
that
\pi_{0}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}X_{n})
is non-empty. By the
{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}}^{1}
-sequence (
2.23
) it suffices to check that for all compatible families of base points
x:(x_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}\to(X_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}
we have
{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}}^{1}\pi_{0}(\Omega(X_{n},x_{n}))\cong\ast
. This is true by
2.36
.
2
since
(\Omega(X_{n},x_{n}))_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}
has the slp by
2.35
.
2
and therefore
(\pi_{0}(\Omega(X_{n},x_{n})))_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}\simeq\underline{*}
in
\mathbf{Pro}(\mathbf{Group})
, again by
2.37
.
∎

2.5
Phantoms

In this section we introduce the notion of a strong phantom morphism and analyse some of its properties and how they interact with the condition on the
\infty
-category of being compactly assembled.
For a different treatment of phantom maps see
[
Efia
, Appendix E]
.
For the general definitions it suffices to assume that
{\mathbf{D}}
is a pointed
\infty
-category which admits all countable filtered colimits. Later we will add the assumption of stability.

Definition 2.38
.

A morphism
g:B\to C
in
{\mathbf{D}}
is called a strong phantom morphism
if the composition
A\xrightarrow{f}B\xrightarrow{g}C
vanishes for all weakly compact maps
f
.

A strong phantom object is an object whose identity is a strong phantom morphism.

Since the weakly compact maps form a two-sided ideal in
{\mathbf{D}}
also the strong phantom maps form a two-sided ideal.

Remark 2.39
.

Classically a morphism
B\to C
is called a phantom map if the composition
A\to B\to C
vanishes for any map from a compact object
A
. A phantom object is an object whose identity morphism is a phantom map. If
{\mathbf{D}}

is compactly generated, then
{\mathbf{D}}
does not have non-trivial phantom objects, and being a phantom morphism
is a restrictive condition. In the other extreme, if
{\mathbf{D}}
has only few (or even no) non-trivial compact objects, then
being a phantom morphism or object is not very (or not at all) restrictive. We use the adjective strong in order to
distinguish the stronger conditions introduced in
2.38
from the classical ones.

In
2.38
we defined the set of strong phantom morphisms as the left annihilator of the set of weakly compact morphisms.
In principle one could also define bigger sets of phantoms as left annihilators of the possibly smaller sets of slp compact or strongly compact morphisms. We will not consider these variants further, but note that
in a compactly assembled category all these variants coincide anyway by

2.14

∎

Remark 2.40
.

We consider a morphism
f:B\to C
in
{\mathbf{D}}
. Following
[
Efia
]
it is called a pure monomorphism
if the fibre
A\to B
of
f
exists and is a strong phantom morphism.
Similarly, it is called a pure epimorphism, if the cofibre
C\to D
exists and is a strong phantom morphism.

If
{\mathbf{D}}
is stable, then in
[
Bun23
]
a fibre sequence
A\to B\to C

was called a phantom extension if the map
B\to C
is a pure epimorphism.
For example, the projection map of a weakly quasi-diagonal extension of
C^{*}
-algebras induces a pure epimorphism in
E
-theory, see
4.50
for details. Therefore weakly quasi-diagonal extensions of
C^{*}
-algebras become
phantom extensions in
E
-theory. For further applications of phantoms in
C^{*}
-algebra theory we refer to
[
Bun23
]
.

Still assuming that
{\mathbf{D}}
is a stable, a morphism
A\to B
is called a phantom equivalence if its cofibre (or equivalently its fibre) is a strong phantom object. For applications of this notion to the Farrel-Jones conjecture or the coarse Baum-Connes conjecture see
[
BKW21
]
or

[
Bun24a
]
.

∎

In order to check whether a morphism
\phi:B\to C
is a strong phantom is suffices to consider a weakly compact
approximation.

Lemma 2.41
.

If
(B_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
is a weakly compact approximation for
B
, then a morphism
\phi:B\to C
is a strong phantom map if and only if the compositions
B_{n}\to B\xrightarrow{\phi}C
vanish for all
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.

Proof.

If
\phi
is a strong phantom map, then the compositions
B_{n}\to B\xrightarrow{\phi}C
clearly vanish.
For the converse we consider a weakly compact map
f:A\to B
and must show that
\phi\circ f\simeq 0
. In view of
2.3
it has a factorization

A\to B_{n}\to B
for some
n
in
{\rm Nature}
. Hence the composition
A\xrightarrow{f}B\xrightarrow{\phi}C
has a factorization

A\to B_{n}\to B\xrightarrow{\phi}C
and therefore vanishes.
∎

From now one we assume that
{\mathbf{D}}
is stable and countably cocomplete.

Definition 2.42
.

1.

A sequence
(g_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
of morphisms
g_{n}:B\to C
is called summable if
there exists a morphism
\hat{g}:B\to\bigoplus_{{\rm Nature}}C
with the components
(g_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
.

2.

A morphism
g:B\to C
is called a summable phantom map if the constant sequence
(g)_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
is summable.

In
Item

1
we say that
\hat{g}
witnesses the summability of the sequence.

Remark 2.43
.

Assume that
(g_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
is a summable sequence of maps
g_{n}:B\to C

with summability witnessed by
\hat{g}:B\to\bigoplus_{n\in{\rm Nature}}C
. Then we can define the sum
of the family as the composition

{}^{\hat{g}}\sum_{n\in{\rm Nature}}g_{n}:B\xrightarrow{\hat{g}}\bigoplus_{n\in{\rm Nature}}C\xrightarrow{\mathrm{fold}}C\ .

(2.24)

We added the superscript
\hat{g}
in order to indicate the possible dependence of the sum on the choice of the witness.
One can check that the sum of a summable sequence is well-defined up to strong phantoms.

∎

Lemma 2.44
.

A summable map is a strong phantom map.

Proof.

Let
g:B\to C
be a summable map with summability witnessed by
\hat{g}
.
We consider any weakly compact map
f:A\to B
. By
2.3
we get a factorization of the composition
A\xrightarrow{f}B\xrightarrow{\hat{g}}\bigoplus_{{\rm Nature}}C

over a map
A\to\bigoplus_{i=0}^{k}C
for some
k
. This implies that the composition
g\circ f:A\to B\xrightarrow{\hat{g}}\bigoplus_{{\rm Nature}}C\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{pr}}_{n}}C
vanishes for
n>k
.
∎

Remark 2.45
.

Let
(g_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
be a summable sequence of maps from
B
to
C
.
Then one could ask wether this implies that the maps
g_{n}
are strong phantoms for almost all
n
.
For any weakly compact map
f:A\to B
we indeed have a factorization
of
\hat{g}\circ f
over
A\to B\to\bigoplus_{n=0}^{k_{f}}C
which shows that
g_{n}\circ f\simeq 0
for all
n
in
{\rm Nature}
with
n>k_{f}
. This does not suffice to conclude that the maps
g_{n}
are phantom for large
n
since
k_{f}
may depend on
f
.
∎

We consider a sequence of maps
(g_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
from
B
to
C
in
{\mathbf{D}}
.

Proposition 2.46
.

If
B
admits a weakly compact approximation and all but finitely many members of the sequence
(g_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
are strong phantom maps, then the sequence is summable. In particular every strong phantom map out of
B
is summable.

Proof.

Without loss of generality we can assume that each member of the sequence
(g_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
is a strong phantom. Let
(B_{i})_{i\in{\rm Nature}}
be a weakly compact approximation for
B
. Then for every
k
in
{\rm Nature}
, the compositions

B_{i}\to B\xrightarrow{g_{k}}C
vanish for all
i
in
{\rm Nature}
and the map
g_{k}:B\to C
is the map induced by a commutative diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.28647pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.28647pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 33.28647pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.42302pt\raise-10.45277pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.25555pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{0}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 78.21463pt\raise-28.86482pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise The map induced on colimits
B\to\bigoplus_{{\rm Nature}}C
by this diagram witnesses the summability of the sequence
(g_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
.
∎

Proposition 2.47
.

We consider a composition
A\xrightarrow{f}B\xrightarrow{g}C
of two strong phantom maps.
If
A
admits a weakly compact exhaustion, then
g\circ f\simeq 0
.

Proof.

Let
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
be a weakly compact exhaustion of
A
with structure maps
\alpha_{n}:A_{n}\to A_{n+1}
. We then have a Milnor sequence of abelian groups

0\to{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}}^{1}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}[A_{n},\Omega B]\to[A,B]\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}[A_{n},B]\to 0\ .

The image of
f
in
[A,B]
under the second map vanishes so that

f
comes from an element
\hat{f}
in
{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}}^{1}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}[A_{n},\Omega B]
.
We represent the
{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}}^{1}
-term of a system
(G_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}
of abelian groups with structure maps
\gamma_{n}:G_{n+1}\to G_{n}

as a quotient of
\prod_{n\in{\rm Nature}}G_{n}
by the image of the endomorphism
{\mathtt{id}}-s
, where
s
sends
a family
(g_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
to the family
(\gamma_{n}(g_{n+1}))_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
. In this way
\hat{f}

is represented by a family
(\hat{f}_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
with
\hat{f}_{n}
in
[A_{n},\Omega B]
.
We also have a Milnor sequence

0\to{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}}^{1}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}[A_{n},\Omega C]\to[A,C]\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}[A_{n},C]\to 0\ .

The element
g\circ f
is represented by the element in
{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}}^{1}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}[A_{n},C]

represented by the family
(g\circ\hat{f}_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
.
The family
(g\circ\hat{f}_{n+1}\circ\alpha_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
represents the same element in
{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}}^{1}_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}[A_{n},C]
.
But this is the zero family since
g
is a strong phantom map and
\alpha_{n}
is weakly compact for every
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.
∎

Corollary 2.48
.

If
B
is a strong phantom object admitting a weakly compact exhaustion, then
B\simeq 0
. In particular, if
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly assembled, then every strong phantom object is trivial.

Proof.

We have
{\mathtt{id}}_{B}\simeq{\mathtt{id}}_{B}\circ{\mathtt{id}}_{B}\simeq 0
by
2.47
.
If
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly assembled, then we use in addition that every object admits even a strongly compact exhaustion by
Theorem

2.26
.
∎

Corollary 2.49
.

Let
f:B\to C
be an equivalence and
\phi:B\to C
be a strong phantom map. If
B
admits a weakly compact exhaustion, then
f-\phi
is an equivalence.

Proof.

We have

(f-\phi)^{-1}\simeq f^{-1}+f^{-1}\circ\phi\circ f^{-1}\ .

∎

Let
F:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{D}}
be a functor between countably cocomplete stable
\infty
-categories,
and consider a strong phantom map
\phi:B\to C
.

Proposition 2.50
.

If
F
preserves countable sums and
B
admits a weakly compact approximation,
then
F(\phi)
is a strong phantom map.

Proof.

The phantom
\phi
is summable by
2.46
.
Let
\hat{\phi}:B\to\bigoplus_{{\rm Nature}}C
be a witness of summability.
Then

F(B)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F(\hat{\phi})}}{{\to}}F(\bigoplus_{{\rm Nature}}C)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\leftarrow}}\bigoplus_{{\rm Nature}}F(C)

witnesses summability of
F(\phi)
. By
2.44
we conclude that
F(\phi)
is a strong phantom map.
∎

We finally have the following characterization of phantom maps in a compactly assembled stable
{\mathbf{D}}
.

Proposition 2.51
.

A map
f:D\to E
is a strong phantom
if and only if for all countable colimit preserving functors
F:{\mathbf{D}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
the map
\pi_{0}(F(f))
of abelian groups is the zero map.

Proof.

If
f
is strong phantom and
F:{\mathbf{D}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
a countable colimit preserving functor, then by
2.50
also
F(f)
is a strong phantom. Since the sphere spectrum
\mathbb{S}
is compact in
\mathbf{Sp}
the map
\pi_{{0}}(F(f))=\pi_{{0}}({\mathtt{map}}(\mathbb{S},F(f)))
is
0
.

For the converse we consider a compact map
g:C\to D
and a map
f:D\to E
satisfying the vanishing condition. Since
g
is compact, it admits a lift
\hat{g}
along

\lvert-\rvert:{\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})}(y(C),S(D))\to{\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(C,D)\ .

Since the functor
{F(-):=}{\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}}(y(C),S(-)):{\mathbf{D}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
preserves countable colimits we have
\pi_{0}(F(f))(\hat{g})=0
. This finally implies that
f{\circ}g\simeq\lvert S(f)\circ\hat{g}\rvert\simeq 0
and therefore
f
is a strong phantom.
∎

3
C^{*}
-algebras

3.1
Semi-projectivity and shapes for
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras

In this section we recall the theory of of semi-projective maps between
C^{*}
-algebras from
[
Bla85
]
. We thereby generalize the classical constructions in a straightforward manner from the
non-equivariant to the equivariant case. For notational simplicity we will formulate the theory only for separable
C^{*}
-algebras
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
, but the material has a straightforward version in the general case with the clear exception of the existence of shape systems. In order to do homotopy theory in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}

we form
the Dwyer-Kan localization
L_{h}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
at homotopy equivalences.
We interpret
further results of
[
Bla85
]
in this context. We will see that semi-projective maps have properties that are analogous to properties of compact maps considered in
Section

2.1
. But the
\infty
-category

GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}

does not fit precisely into the framework of
Section

2.1
since it it not known to admit all countable filtered colimits. Note that the colimits
Z:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}Z_{i}
below are always interpreted in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and not in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
. In order to resolve the lack of countable filtered colimits we will introduce the functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
in

Section

3.5
.

Let
Z
be in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
(I_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
be an increasing family of invariant ideals in
Z
. The family
(I_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
is then called a presentation of the invariant direct limit ideal

I:=\overline{\bigcup_{n\in{\rm Nature}}I_{n}}

of
Z
.
The following definition generalizes
[
Bla85
, Def. 2.10]
to the equivariant case.

Definition 3.1
.

A morphism
f:A\to B
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is called semi-projective if for any

Z
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
, increasing family of invariant ideals
(I_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
with direct limit ideal
I
, and the bold part of the diagram

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Z/I_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z/I}

there exists
n
in
{\rm Nature}
and the dotted completion.

The semi-projective maps form a two-sided ideal in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

Definition 3.2
.

An algebra
A
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is called semi-projective if
{\mathtt{id}}_{A}
is semi-projective.

Construction 3.3
.

In this construction we present the typical source of semi-projective maps.
Let
B
be a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra with norm
\|-\|_{B}
and
(b_{k})_{k\in K}
be a finite family of elements in
B
.
Let furthermore
(p_{l})_{l\in L}
be a finite family of non-commutative polynomials in variables

(x_{k,g})_{(k,g)\in K\times G}\cup(x_{k,g}^{*})_{(k,g)\in K\times G}
. We assume that this family contains the monomials
x_{k,e}
for all
k
in
K
in order to ensure that the set of relations used below is admissible in the sense of
[
Bla85
, Def. 1.1]
.
We write
p_{l}(b)
in
B
for the evaluation of
p_{l}
at
x_{k,g}=gb_{k}
for all
k
in
K
and
g
in
G
.
Let
(r_{l})_{l\in L}
be a family of elements of
{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\|p_{l}(b)\|_{B}<r_{l}
for all
l
in
L
. We can then define the universal
C^{*}
-algebra

A
(in the sense of
[
Bla85
, Def. 1.2]
) generated by a family of elements
(b^{\prime}_{k,g})_{(k,g)\in K\times G}
subject to the relations
\|h^{*}p_{l}(b^{\prime})\|\leq r_{l}
for all
h
in
G
and
l
in
L
, where
h^{*}p(b^{\prime})
is the evaluation of
p
at the family
(b^{\prime}_{k,hg})_{(k,g)\in K\times G}
.
The
C^{*}
-algebra
A
has a canonical
G
-action which extends the map
(h,b^{\prime}_{k,g})\mapsto b^{\prime}_{k,hg}
on the generators.
We furthermore have a natural homomorphism
f:A\to B
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
fixed by the conditions

f(b^{\prime}_{k,g})=gb_{k}
for all
k
in
K
and
g
in
G
.
∎

Definition 3.4
.

The ideal of explicit semi-projective maps is
the ideal in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
generated by maps
arising from the construction
3.3
.

Proposition 3.5

(
[
Bla85
, Prop. 2.35]
)
.

An explicit semi-projective map is semi-projective.

Proof.

Since the semi-projective maps form a two-sided ideal it suffices to show that the maps
f:A\to B
constructed in
3.3
are semi-projective.
In this argument we will add an index in order to indicate to which
C^{*}
-algebra a norm belongs.
Let
(I_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
be an increasing family of invariant ideals of a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
Z
with direct limit ideal
I
. We let
\iota:Z\to Z/I
and
\iota_{j}:Z\to Z/I_{j}
for
j
in
{\rm Nature}
denote the projections. We consider a map
h:B\to Z/I
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. We can choose elements
\tilde{b}_{k}
in
Z
for all
k
in
K
such that

h(b_{k})=\iota(\tilde{b}_{k})
.
Since

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{j\in{\rm Nature}}\|\iota_{j}(p_{l}(\tilde{b}))\|_{Z/I_{j}}=\|\iota(p_{l}(\tilde{b}))\|_{Z/I}=\|h(p_{l}(b))\|_{Z/I}\leq\|p_{l}(b)\|_{B}<r_{l}

(see e.g.
[
BEL
, Prop. 7.15]
for an argument for the first equality)
there exists
n
in
{\rm Nature}

such that
\|\iota_{n}(p_{l}(\tilde{b}))\|_{Z/I_{n}}\leq r_{l}
for all
l
in
L
.
Then also
\|\iota_{n}(p_{l}(g\tilde{b}))\|_{Z/I_{n}}\leq r_{l}
for all
g
in
G
, where
g\tilde{b}:=(g\tilde{b}_{k})_{k\in K}
.
By the universal property of
A
there exists an equivariant
map
\tilde{f}:A\to Z/I_{n}
fixed by
\tilde{f}(b^{\prime}_{k,g})=g\iota_{n}(\tilde{b}_{k})
.
Hence we get the desired square

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{f}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Z/I_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{h}}
\textstyle{Z/I}

in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.
∎

Remark 3.6
.

In this remark we introduce the localization of the category of separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras at the homotopy equivalences.
The category
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
of
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras has a topological enrichment by compactly generated topological spaces such that for any compact topological space
W
and
A,B
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
the topolgical hom-space
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}}(A,B)
is determined by

{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{Top}}}(W,\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}}(A,B))\cong{\mathtt{Hom}}_{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}}(A,C(W,B))\ .

(3.1)

Using the topological enrichment we can talk about paths of morphisms or homotopies between homomorphisms. In particular we get a notion of homotopy equivalences.

We now restrict to separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras and let

L_{h}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{sep}}

(3.2)

denote the Dwyer-Kan localization of
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
at the set of homotopy equivalences. The
\infty
-category
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{sep}}
is the homotopy coherent nerve of the Kan-enriched simplicial category
obtained by replacing the topological hom-spaces
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(A,B)
by their
singular complexes. By definition of
a Dwyer-Kan localization for any
\infty
-category
{{\mathbf{C}}}
we have an equivalence

L_{h}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ ,

(3.3)

where the superscript
h
indicates homotopy invariant functors (see
3.14
.
1
).

Since for every
A
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
the tensor product
A\otimes-
preserves homotopy equivalences the Dwyer-Kan localization (
3.2
) is symmetric monoidal. For any symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category pull-back along the symmetric monoidal refinement of
L_{h}
induces an equivalence

L_{h}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{h}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ ,

(3.4)

where the subscript
\otimes/\mathrm{lax}
indicates symmetric monoidal or lax symmetric monoidal functors.

The
\infty
-category
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
is pointed and left-exact, and the tensor product on this category is bi-left exact.
A morphism
f:A\to B
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is called a Schochet fibration
[
Sch84
]

if the induced map

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(C,A)\to\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(C,B)

of topological
{\mathtt{Hom}}
-spaces is a Serre fibration
of topological spaces for every
C
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. A cartesian square

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D}

in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is called Schochet fibrant if
B\to D
(and hence also
A\to C
) are Schochet fibrations.
A functor is called Schochet exact if it sends Schochet fibrant cartesian squares to cartesian squares.
The functor
L_{h}
is Schochet exact
and pull-back along
L_{h}
induces for every left-exact
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
an equivalence

L_{h}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,\mathrm{Sch}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ ,

(3.5)

where the superscripts
\mathrm{Sch}
and
\mathrm{lex}
indicate Schochet exact and finite limit-preserving functors, respectivley. For the arguments see

[
Bun24b
, Sec. 3]
.
∎

Let
f:A\to B
be a map in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
, and let

(Z_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
be a system in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
with
Z:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}Z_{i}
.
Recall the shifted lifting property (slp) from
2.1
.

Proposition 3.7

(
[
Bla85
, Thm. 3.1 & 3.3]
)
.

If
f
is semi-projective, then the square of anima

\textstyle{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}}(A,Z_{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{Map}}_{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}}(A,Z)}
\textstyle{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}}(B,Z_{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{Map}}_{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}}(B,Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

has the slp for
\emptyset\to*
and
S^{0}\to*
.

Proof.

We first show the slp for
*\to 0
. We must show that
for any map

B\to Z
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
there exists
j
in
{\rm Nature}
and a homotopy commutative diagram

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Z_{j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}Z_{n}}

in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. We can copy the proof of
[
Bla85
, Thm. 3.1]
. We simply observe that all the algebras constructed in this proof carry canonical
G
-actions, and that the morphisms are equivariant.

We now show the slp for
S^{0}\to*
.
We let
\alpha_{n}:Z_{n}\to Z
and
\alpha_{m,n}:Z_{n}\to Z_{m}
for
m\geq n
denote the structure maps of the system.
We consider
two maps

\tilde{h},\tilde{h}:B\to Z_{n}

such that
\alpha_{n}\circ\tilde{h}:B\to Z
is homotopic to
\alpha_{n}\circ\tilde{h}^{\prime}:B\to Z
.
We must show that there exists
m
in
{\rm Nature}
with
m\geq n

such that
A\xrightarrow{f}B\xrightarrow{\tilde{h}}Z_{n}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{m,n}}Z_{m}
and

A\xrightarrow{f}B\xrightarrow{\tilde{h}^{\prime}}Z_{n}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{m,n}}Z_{m}
are homotopic.
To this end we can again copy the argument for

[
Bla85
, Thm. 3.3]
word by word.
∎

For explicit semi-projective maps we can show a much stronger lifting property.
Let
(Z_{i})_{i\in I}
be a countable filtered system in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
with
Z:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}Z_{i}
.

Proposition 3.8
.

If
f:A\to B
is an explicit semi-projective homomorphism of
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras, then the square of anima

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 65.6799pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-65.3865pt\raise has the slp.

Proof.

It is easy to see that the class of maps
f:A\to B
for which the statement holds true is a two-sided ideal in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. It therefore suffices to consider the case that
f:A\to B
arises from
3.3
.
We consider a map
K\to L
of finite anima and the bold part of

(3.7)

Since
X
is compact the map marked by
(1)
factorizes over a finite stage of the colimit
and gives rise to the map
l
.
The maps denoted by
r
with decorations are induced by restriction along
g
,
and the maps
\alpha
with decorations are induced by structure maps of the system
(Z_{i})_{i\in I}
and its colimit. The value of
k
is still open.

The following lemma provides the diagram (
3.7
) together with the homotoypy
\beta
as desired.

Lemma 3.9
.

There exists
k
in
I
with
j\leq k
and a dotted arrow in (
3.7
) such that the left square commutes up to homotopy
\beta
,
the other square with left upper corner commutes strictly, and
r_{Z,*}\beta
is constant.

Proof.

Let
b
be in
B
. We claim that for every
\epsilon
in
(0,\infty)
there exists
k
in
I
with
j\leq k

and an element
\phi
in
C(Y)\otimes Z_{k}
such that

\|\alpha_{Y,k}(\phi)-h(b)\|\leq\epsilon
and
r_{Z}(h(b))=\alpha_{X,k}(r_{Z_{k}}(\phi))
.
By the Choi-Effros theorem
[
CE76
]
we can choose a completely positive contractive split
s:C(X)\to C(Y)
such that
r\circ s={\mathtt{id}}_{C(X)}
.
We extend
s
as
s_{Z}:=s\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{Z}
.
Since
C(Y)\otimes-
preserves filtered colimits we can first choose

k
in
I
with
j\leq k
and
\phi^{\prime}
in
C(Y)\otimes Z_{k}
such that
\|\alpha_{Y,k}(\phi^{\prime})-h(b)\|\leq\epsilon/3
.
Then we set

\phi:=\phi^{\prime}-s_{Z_{k}}(r_{Z_{k}}(\phi^{\prime}))+s_{Z_{k}}(\alpha_{X,k,j}(l(b)))

in
C(Y)\otimes Z_{k}
.
We then have

\displaystyle r_{Z_{k}}(\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r_{Z_{k}}(\phi^{\prime})-r_{Z_{k}}(s_{Z_{k}}(r_{Z_{k}}(\phi^{\prime})))+r_{Z_{k}}(s_{Z_{k}}(\alpha_{X,k,j}(l(b))))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha_{X,k,j}(l(b))

We further have

\displaystyle\alpha_{X,k}(\alpha_{X,k,j}(l(b)))-\alpha_{X,k}(r_{Z_{k}}(\phi^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r_{Z}(h(b))-\alpha_{X,k}(r_{Z_{k}}(\phi^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r_{Z}(\alpha_{Y,k}(\phi^{\prime}))-\alpha_{X,k}(r_{Z_{k}}(\phi^{\prime}))+r_{Z}(h(b)-\alpha_{Y,k}(\phi^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r_{Z}(h(b)-\alpha_{Y,k}(\phi^{\prime}))

and therefore

\|\alpha_{X,k}(\alpha_{X,k,j}(l(b))-r_{Z_{k}}(\phi^{\prime}))\|\leq\epsilon/3\ .

Hence we can choose
k
sufficiently large such that

\|\alpha_{X,k,j}(l(b))-r_{Z_{k}}(\phi^{\prime}))\|\leq 2\epsilon/3\ .

Then

\|\phi-\phi^{\prime}\|\leq 2\epsilon/3
and therefore
\|\alpha_{Y,k}(\phi)-h(b)\|\leq\epsilon
.

We now construct the dotted arrow. We fix
\epsilon
in
(0,\infty)
dependent on
A
such that the following works.
For every
G
-orbits of generators (with representative
a_{i}
)
we choose
\phi_{i}
for
f(a_{i})
as above. Then we get the dotted arrow.
We let
\psi_{i,t}
be the straight interpolation between
h(f(a_{i}))
and
\alpha_{Y,k}(\phi_{i})
.
Then we get a homotopy
\beta:A\to C(Y\times I)\otimes Z
whose
value on
a_{i}
is
\psi_{i,t}
.
Since
r_{Z}(\alpha_{Y,k}(\phi_{i}))=\alpha_{X,k}(r_{Z_{k}}(\phi))=\alpha_{X,j}(l(f(a_{i})))=r_{Z}(f(h(a_{i})))

we see that
r_{Z,*}\beta
is the constant homotopy.
∎

This completes the proof of
3.8

∎

Let
A
be in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

Definition 3.10

(
[
Bla85
, Def. 4.1]
)
.

A shape system for
A
is a system
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}

such that
A\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}A_{n}
and
A_{n}\to A_{n+1}
is semi-projective for every
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.
If all these maps are explicit semi-projectives then this is called an explicit shape system.

By
3.5
an explicit shape system is in particular a shape system.
Our main motivation for introducing the notion of explicit shape systems is the following consequence of
3.8
.

Corollary 3.11
.

If
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
is an explicit shape system for
A
and
(Z_{i})_{i\in I}
is a system in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
with
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}Z_{i}\cong Z
, then
the map of systems

(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}{\mathtt{Map}}_{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A_{n},Z_{i}))_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}\to({\mathtt{Map}}_{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}}(A_{n},Z))_{n\in{\rm Nature}^{\mathrm{op}}}

has the slp in the sense of
2.29
.

We consider the full subcategory
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
of
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
of separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras.

Proposition 3.12

(
[
Bla85
, Thm. 4.3]
)
.

Every separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra admits an explicit shape system.

Proof.

One can copy the proof of
[
Bla85
, Thm. 4.3]
which is based on
3.3
. Let us nevertheless provide a sketch. Let
A
be in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.
We consider a countable subset
(a_{k})_{k\in{\rm Nature}}
of
A
such that
(ga_{k})_{(k,g)\in{\rm Nature}\times G}
is dense in
A
.
We then consider the set of generators
(a_{k,g})_{(k,g)\in{\rm Nature}\times G}
.
Every non-commutative polynomial
p
over
{\mathbb{Q}}[i]
in variables
(x_{k,g})_{(k,g)\in{\rm Nature}\times G}\cup(x^{*}_{k,g})_{(k,g)\in{\rm Nature}\times G}
gives rise to a relation
\|p(a)\|\leq\|{\mathtt{ev}}(p(a))\|_{A}
, where
{\mathtt{ev}}(p(a))
is the image of the evaluation of
p
at
(a_{k,g})_{(k,g)\in{\rm Nature}\times G}
in
A
under the map which sends
a_{k,g}
to
ga_{k}
. The set
R
of these polynomials is countable and
A
is isomorphic to the
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra generated by the set
(a_{k,g})_{(k,g)\in{\rm Nature}\times G}
with respect to these relations and the
G
-action extending

ha_{k,g}=a_{g,hg}
. We let
(p_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
be an indexing of

R
. We let
R_{n}
be the union of the subset of those relations in the segment of the sequence for
k=0,\dots,n
which only involve the variables

a_{k,g}
for
k=0,\dots,n
and
g
in
G
with the set of monomials
\{x_{k,e}\mid k=0,\dots,n\}
. We then define
A_{n}
as the universal
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
generated by
(a_{k,g})_{(k,g)\in\{0,\dots,n\}\times G}
subject to the relations

\|h^{*}p_{l}(a)\|\leq\|{\mathtt{ev}}(p_{l}(a))\|_{A}+1/n
for all
l
in
R_{n}
and
h
in
H
. The connecting maps
A_{n}\to A_{n+1}
are explicit semi-projective since they arise from
3.3
. Furthermore,
we have an isomorphism
A\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}A_{n}
.
∎

Explicit shape systems can be chosen functorially for
{\rm Nature}
-indexed systems. Let
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
be a system in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}

Proposition 3.13
.

There exists commutative
diagram

\textstyle{A_{0,0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{1,0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{2,0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{3,0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dots}
\textstyle{A_{0,1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{1,1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{2,1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{3,1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dots}
\textstyle{A_{0,2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{1,2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{2,2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{3,2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dots}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{A_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dots}

in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
such that the lower line is the colimit of the diagram taken vertically and
(A_{n,k})_{k\in{\rm Nature}}
is an explicit shape system for
A_{n}
every
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.

Proof.

We construct the diagram inductively using the argument for
3.12
.
Assume that we have constructed
A_{n,k}
and the maps for
n<n_{0}
and all
k
in
{\rm Nature}
.
We then construct the explicit shape system
(A_{i}^{\prime})_{i\in{\rm Nature}}
of
A_{n_{0}}
as in the proof of
3.12
.
We thereby make sure that the countable subset of
A_{n_{0}}
used in the construction
contains the images of the generators of the
A_{n_{0}-1,i}
for all
i
in
{\rm Nature}
.
In order to define the diagram

\textstyle{A_{n_{0}-1,0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{n_{0},0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{n_{0}-1,1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{n_{0},1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{n_{0}-1,2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{n_{0},2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\vdots}
\textstyle{\vdots}

we choose an increasing sequence of integers
(k(i))_{i\in{\rm Nature}}

and set
A_{n_{0},i}:=A^{\prime}_{k(i)}
. We thereby choose
k(i)
so large such that
the image of every generator
x
of
A_{n_{0}-1,i}
in
A_{n_{0}}
is a generator
x^{\prime}
of
A^{\prime}_{k(i)}
such that

\|x^{\prime}\|_{A^{\prime}_{n_{0},k(i)}}<\|x\|_{A_{n_{0}-1,i}}
. We can then define
A_{n_{0}-1,i}\to A_{n_{0},i}
such that it sends
x
to
x^{\prime}
.
∎

3.2
Axiomatic characterization of
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}

The classical equivariant
E
-theory functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
in (
1.4
)
is a functor from separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras to an additive category constructed in
[
GHT00
, Def. 6.8]
. The functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
in (
1.4
) to the ordinary asymptotic morphism category
[
GHT00
, Def. 2.13]
constitutes an intermediate step in the construction of
E
-theory.
In the present paper we will consider refinements of both functors
to functors with values in
\infty
-categories. We think of these refinements as means to do homotopy theory with separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras reflecting some additional invariance properties.
While
L_{h}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
from
3.6
is the correct framework for just doing homotopy theory, the functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
provides the correct version of homotopy theory
which is also compatible with filtered colimits. If one adds Morita invariance and exactness to the picture, then

\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
will be the appropriate home.
This point of view suggests to first characterize these functors axiomatically
in terms of universal properties. We then prove existence by providing constructions.
Note that the constructions yield additional information about these functors which are not obvious from the axiomatic characterization alone, e.g. the property of being compactly assembled asserted by our main
Theorem

1.5
.

Before we state the characterizations
3.16
and
3.15
we recall in
3.14
all the
C^{*}
-algebraic notions
necessary to formulate the universal properties.
We will show that an exact and countable sum-preserving functor is automatically filtered colimit preserving and apply this to
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.
At the end of this section we explain how the functors
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
can be extended as
s
-finitary functors to all
C^{*}
-algebras in a formal way.

In
Section

3.3
,
Section

3.4
and
Section

3.5
we
present three independent constructions of an equivariant
E
-theory functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. The verification that all three constructions yield equivalent functors which satisfy the desired universal properties will be completed in

Section

3.6
. The functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
will be constructed in
Section

3.5

and again constitutes an intermediate step towards
E
-theory.

Since in this section we also want to formulate the universal properties for the extension of the functors to all
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
(as opposed to separable algebras
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
) we will describe the necessary
C^{*}
-algebraic notions in this generality.

We equip
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
with the symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes
induced by the maximal tensor product.
The choice of the maximal tensor product (in contrast e.g. to the minimal one) is important since we need that the tensor product preserves exact sequences and
filtered colimits in each argument.
The tensor product of two separable
C^{*}
-algebras is again separable so that
\otimes
restricts to
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. We furthermore note that
\otimes
is bi-continuous for the topological enrichment of
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

If
(A_{i})_{i\in I}
is a family in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
, then we can form the categorical product
\prod_{i\in I}A_{i}
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. The elements of this product are uniformly bounded families
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
with
a_{i}
in
A_{i}
1
1

1

Note that this description is only correct for discrete groups
G
which is our standing assumption.
. The sum
\bigoplus_{i\in I}A_{i}
is the
G
-
C^{*}
-subalgebra
of the product generated by families
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
with finitely many non-zero entries.
We have canonical inclusions
\epsilon_{i}:A_{i}\to\bigoplus_{i\in I}A_{i}
for all
i
in
I
.
But note that the sum is not the categorical coproduct of the family. The latter would be represented
by the free product
\ast_{i\in I}A_{i}
.
A sum or coproduct of a countable family of separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras is again a separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra. In contrast, an infinite product of non-trivial
C^{*}
-algebras is never separable.

Remark 3.14
.

We start with a list of properties which may be satisfied by functors defined on
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
or at least on its subcategory
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
of separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras.

1.

homotopy invariance: The enrichment of
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
in topological spaces described in
3.6
yields a notion of homotopy equivalence. A morphism
f:A\to B
is a homotopy equivalence if there exists a morphism
g:B\to A
such that the compositions

f\circ g
and
g\circ f
are homotopic to the identities of
A
and
B
, respectively. A functor defined on
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
or
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is called homotopy invariant if it sends homotopy equivalences to equivalences.

2.

K_{G}
-stability: We define the separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
K_{G}:=K(L^{2}(G)\otimes\ell^{2})
with the

G
-action induced by the left-regular representation on
L^{2}(G)
. A morphism
f:A\to B
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is called a
K_{G}
-equivalence if
f\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{K_{G}}:A\otimes K_{G}\to B\otimes K_{G}
is a homotopy equivalence.
A functor is called
K_{G}
-stable, if it sends
K_{G}
-equivalences to equivalences.
Since
K_{G}
is separable the notion of
K_{G}
-stablility is also well-defined for functors only defined on separable algebras.

3.

exactness: A functor
F
to a pointed category is called exact, if it sends the zero algebra to the zero object and
exact sequences of
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras to fibre sequences.
More precisely, writing an exact sequence of
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras as a commutative square

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C}

which is cartesian and cocartesian, we require
that
F(0)\simeq 0
and that the resulting commutative square

\textstyle{F(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F(C)}

is cartesian.

4.

countable sum-preserving: A functor
F
is called countable sum-preserving
if for any countable family
(A_{i})_{i\in I}
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
the coproduct
\coprod_{i\in I}F(A_{i})
in the target of
F
exists and the canonical map

\sqcup_{i\in I}F(\epsilon_{i}):\coprod_{i\in I}F(A_{i})\to F(\bigoplus_{i\in I}A_{i})

is an equivalence.
Since a countable sum of separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras is again separable, the condition of being countable sum preserving is also well-defined for functors only defined on separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras.

5.

countable filtered colimit preserving: For every system
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
the colimit
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}F(A_{n})
in the target of
F
exists and the canonical map

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}F(A_{n})\to F(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}A_{n})

is an equivalence. Since the colimit of a system
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
of separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras is again separable this property makes also sense for functors only defined on separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras.

6.

s
-finitariness: This property is only defined for functors
F
defined on all of
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
A functor
F
is called s-finitary if for every
A
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
the colimit
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}F(A^{\prime})
exists in the target of
F
and the canonical morphism

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}F(A^{\prime})\to F(A)

is an equivalence. Here
A^{\prime}\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A
is the poset of
G
-invariant separable subalgebras
A^{\prime}
of
A
. The functor
F
is s-finitary if and only if
F
preserves
\aleph_{1}
-filtered colimits.
In order to see this note that the realization functor

{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{{\mathrm{sep}}})\to GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}

defined as the left Kan-extension of the inclusion
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
along
y:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{{\mathrm{sep}}})

is an equivalence with inverse

GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{{\mathrm{sep}}})\ ,\quad A\mapsto\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}y(A^{\prime})\ .

Definition 3.15
.

The functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is the initial
functor

a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:{G}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}

to an
\infty
-category admitting countable filtered colimits
which is homotopy invariant and preserves filtered colimits.

The universal property required in
3.15
asserts that precomposition
with
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
induces for every
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
admitting countable filtered colimits an equivalence

(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil}}(\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,\mathrm{cfil}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ ,

where the superscript
\mathrm{cfil}
indicates countable filtered colimit preserving functors (for
h
see
3.6
).
It is clear that
3.15
characterizes the functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
essentially uniquely. The construction of this functor will be completed with
3.46
.

Definition 3.16
.

The equivariant
E
-theory functor for separable
C^{*}
-algebras is the initial
functor

\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}

to a countably cocomplete stable
\infty
-category
which is homotopy invariant,
K_{G}
-stable, exact and countable sum-preserving.

The universal property required in
3.16
asserts that precomposition with
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
induces for every countably cocomplete stable
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
an equivalence

(\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{\omega}}(\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,K_{G},\mathrm{ex},\oplus}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ ,

where the superscripts
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{\omega}
,
h
,
K_{G}
,
\mathrm{ex}
,
and
\oplus
indicate the corresponding property of functors: countable colimit preserving, homotopy invariant,
K_{G}
-stable, exact and countable sum preserving.
It is again clear that
3.16
characterizes the functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
essentially uniquely.
The statements in
Section

3.6
complete the proof of existence.

Note that the
\infty
-categories
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
have canonical symmetric monoidal structures
such that the functors
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
have canonical symmetric monoidal refinements
which are characterized by analogous universal properties. For details we refer to the subsequent subsections.

Note that in
3.16
we require the condition of being countable sum preserving since
this property can also be formulated for the classical functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
.
The more natural property of being countable filtered colimit preserving is not expected for
the classical functors since the functor
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})\simeq\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}

does not preserve filtered colimits. For the same reason in the case of
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
there is no nice characterization of the classical functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
by a universal property.

The following general observation will ensure that
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
nevertheless preserves all countable filtered colimits.

Proposition 3.17
.

If
F:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
is a homotopy invariant, exact and countable sum preserving functor to a countably cocomplete stable
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
, then

F
preserves countable filtered colimits.

Proof.

We adapt the proof given in
[
MN06
, Prop. 2.6]
to the
\infty
-categorical context and add some details.
It suffices to
show that the functor
F
preserves
{\rm Nature}
-indexed colimits.

Let
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
be a system in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
with structure maps
\alpha_{n}:A_{n}\to A_{n+1}
for all
n
in
{\rm Nature}
and
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}A_{n}\simeq A
with structure maps
\iota_{n}:A_{n}\to A
.
Then we define the telescope

T
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
as the subalgebra of
families
(f_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
\prod_{n\in{\rm Nature}}C([n,n+1],A_{n})
which satisfy the following conditions:

1.

f(0)=0

2.

\alpha_{n}(f_{n}(n+1))=f_{n+1}(n+1)
for all
n
in
{\rm Nature}

3.

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{t\to\infty}\|f_{n(t)}(t)\|=0
,
where
n(t)
in
{\rm Nature}
is such that
t\in(n(t),n(t)+1]
for every
t
in
[0,\infty)
.

The evaluation
e:(f_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}\mapsto(f_{n}(n+1))_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
fits into
an exact sequence

0\to\bigoplus_{n\in{\rm Nature}}S(A_{n})\to T\xrightarrow{e}\bigoplus_{n\in{\rm Nature}}A_{n}\to 0

(3.8)

in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
, where
S(B):=C_{0}((0,1),B)
for any
B
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
We apply
F
and use that it is exact and preserves countable sums in order to get a fibre sequence

F(T)\xrightarrow{F(e)}\bigoplus_{n\in{\rm Nature}}F(A_{n})\xrightarrow{1-s}\bigoplus_{n\in{\rm Nature}}F(A_{n})\xrightarrow{}\Sigma F(T)\ ,

(3.9)

where
s
sends a family
(x_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
to
(F(\alpha_{n-1})(x_{n-1}))_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
, and where we set
F(\alpha_{-1})(x_{-1}):=0
.
The fibre sequence exhibits
\Sigma F(T)
as the colimit of the system
(F(A_{n}))_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
.

Remark 3.18
.

In order to see the formula for the map
1-s
in the middle one can consider the map of exact sequences

with

for integers

. We have a map

where
\tilde{c}
is given by the same formula as
c
.
Since
\tilde{T}
and
C_{0}((0,\infty],A)
are contractible, the map of fibre sequences obtained by applying
F
reduces to the square

\textstyle{F(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\Sigma F(T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma F(c)}
\textstyle{F(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\Sigma F(S(A))}

which shows that
F(c)
is an equivalence.
∎

Remark 3.19
.

The analogue of
3.17
for functors
F:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{\mathbf{C}}

is also true with the same proof.
∎

The following is a consequence of

3.17
.

Corollary 3.20
.

The equivariant
E
-theory functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
preserves all countable filtered colimits.

In order to complete the discussion of the equivariant
E
-theory we will discuss induction and restriction functors and crossed products and adjunctions relating them in
3.62
and
3.63
.

In the following we introduce the s-finitary extension of the functors
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
to all
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras and state their universal properties.

Definition 3.21
.

We define the presentable stable
\infty
-categories

\mathrm{A}^{G}:={\mathtt{Ind}}_{\aleph_{1}}(\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})\ ,\qquad\mathrm{E}^{G}:={\mathtt{Ind}}_{\aleph_{1}}(\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})\ .

(3.10)

Note that here
\aleph_{1}
is a subscript and
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})
indicates the
\aleph_{1}
-ind completion of
{\mathbf{C}}
, i.e., the
\infty
-category obtained from
{\mathbf{C}}
by adding freely all
\aleph_{1}
-filtered colimits.

2
2

2

This ind completion should not be confused with
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})
which stands for freely adding countable (not countably!) filtered colimits.
We let
y^{\prime}:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})
the
canonical embedding which preserves countable filtered colimits.

Definition 3.22
.

We define the functors

\mathrm{a}^{G}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{A}^{G}\ ,\qquad\mathrm{e}^{G}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}

(3.11)

as left Kan-extensions

In the statement below the superscript
\mathrm{sfin}
stands for the condition of being
s
-finitary, see
3.14
.
6
. The following proposition states the universal propertes of the functors described in
3.22
.

Proposition 3.23
.

1.

Pull-back along
\mathrm{a}^{G}
induces for every
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
with filtered colimits an equivalence

(\mathrm{a}^{G})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{fil}}(\mathrm{A}^{G},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{sfin}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(3.12)

2.

Pull-back along
\mathrm{e}^{G}
induces for every cocomplete stable
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
an equivalence

(\mathrm{e}^{G})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}(\mathrm{E}^{G},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,K_{G},\mathrm{ex},\oplus,\mathrm{sfin}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(3.13)

Proof.

This follows immediately from
3.15
or
3.16
and the universal property of
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\aleph_{1}}
. See also
[
BEL
, Sec. 3]
or
[
Bun24b
, Sec. 8]
for more detailed arguments.
∎

The symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
or
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
(note that it preserves countable colimits in each argument) induces an entry-wise filtered colimit preserving symmetric monoidal structure on the
\aleph_{1}
-
{\mathtt{Ind}}
-completions
\mathrm{A}^{G}
or
\mathrm{E}^{G}
.

Proposition 3.24
.

1.

The functor
\mathrm{a}^{G}
has a symmetric monoidal refinement such that
the pull-back along
\mathrm{a}^{G}
induces for every symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
admitting filtered colimits with an entry-wise filtered colimit preserving tensor product
an equivalence

(\mathrm{a}^{G})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{{\mathrm{fil}}}(\mathrm{A}^{G},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{h,\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{sfin}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(3.14)

2.

The functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}
has a symmetric monoidal refinement such that
the pull-back along
\mathrm{e}^{G}
induces for every cocomplete symmetric monoidal stable
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
with bi-cocontinuous tensor product
an equivalence

(\mathrm{e}^{G})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}(\mathrm{E}^{G},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{h,K_{G},\mathrm{ex},\oplus,\mathrm{sfin}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(3.15)

Sketch.

The arguments are similar as the corresponding parts of
[
Bun24b
, Sec. 8]
or the proof of
[
BEL
, Prop. 3.8]
.
∎

3.3
Construction of
{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}}
as a localization at
E
-equivalences

In this section we construct a functor

\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}

in analogy to the constructions of the
\infty
-categorical version of
KK
-theory in
[
LN18
]
and equivariant
KK
-theory
in
[
BEL
]
as the Dwyer-Kan localization of
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
at the classical
E
-theory equivalences.
In order to distinguish the functor constructed in the present section from the functors constructed in the subsequent sections
Section

3.4
and
Section

3.5
we will momentarily add the subscript
GHT
in order to make clear that its construction is based on the work of Guentner, Higson and Trout
[
GHT00
]
.

Recall that

\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}

denotes the classical equivariant
E
-theory functor introduced in
[
GHT00
, Def. 6.8]
. A morphism
f:A\to B
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is called a classical
E
-theory equivalence if
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(f):\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(A)\to\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(B)
is an isomorphism in
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
.

Definition 3.25
.

We define

\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}

as the Dwyer-Kan localization
of
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
at the classical
E
-theory equivalences.

By definition, pull-back along
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
induces for any
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
an equivalence

(\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{W_{\mathrm{GHT}}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ ,

(3.16)

where
W_{\mathrm{GHT}}
stands for the full subcategory of functors which send classical
E
-theory equivalences to equivalences.
In
3.31
below we will show that
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
also has the universal property required by
3.16
.

Note that the classical
E
-theory functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
is homotopy invariant (by construction) and
K_{G}
-stable
(see
[
GHT00
, Prop. 6.10]
).

Corollary 3.26
.

The functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
is homotopy invariant and
K_{G}
-stable.

By the universal property of the functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
we get the dashed arrow in the following square

\textstyle{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ho}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{ho}\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}

(3.17)

such the corresponding upper triangle commutes. Since
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
is an ordinary category, by the universal property of
\mathrm{ho}
we further get the dotted arrow
\phi
and the other commutative triangle. Note that
\phi
is conservative by construction.
We will show in
3.29
below that
\phi
is an equivalence.

In order to discuss finite limits in
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
we closely follow
[
BEL
, Sec. 2]
or
[
Bun24b
, Sec. 3]
. First of all, the category
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is pointed and admits all finite limits. In order to descend these limits to the localization
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
we use that
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}

has the structure of a category of fibrant objects
[
Bro73
]
, see also
[
Uuy13
, Def. 1.1]
,
[
BEL
, Def. 2.7]
, or
[
Cis19
, Def 7.4.12 and 7.5.7]
for an
\infty
-categorical version) such that:

1.

The fibrations are the surjective homomorphisms.

2.

The weak equivalences are the classical
E
-theory equivalences.

A similar structure for the classical
KK
-theory equivalences has first been considered in
[
Uuy13
]
.
The existence of the structure of a category of fibrant objects implies that
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
precisely inverts the classical
E
-equivalences.
Using
[
Cis19
, Prop. 7.5.6]
we can further conclude (see also
[
Bun24b
, Prop. 3.16]
for similar arguments):

Corollary 3.27
.

The category
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
is pointed, left-exact and the functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
is exact. Moreover, every fibre sequence in
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
is induced up to equivalence by a short exact sequence in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

Proposition 3.28
.

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
is stable.

Proof.

For the moment let
F:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
be any exact and homotopy invariant functor.
For
A
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
the suspension
S(A)
fits into an exact sequence

{\mathtt{ev}}_{0}
is surjective, using the exactness of

we get the cartesian square

(3.21)

of horizontal fibre sequences. The commutativity of the left triangle is given by the usual Puppe sequence argument showing
that
F(\partial_{f})
represents the fibre
of
F(C(f))\to F(Z(f))
.

We now specialize to the homotopy invariant and exact functor
F=\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
.
We can conclude that
\phi(d_{f})
(with
\phi
as in (
3.17
)) induces the boundary maps in the long exact sequences in classical
E
-theory
[
GHT00
, Thm. 6.20]
.

Following
[
Cun84
, Sec. 4]
we use the Toeplitz extension

0\to K\to{\mathcal{T}}_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\to}}S({\mathbb{C}})\to 0

(3.22)

in order to introduce Bott periodicity (see e.g.
[
Bun24b
, (6.1), (6.3) and (6.4)]
for details).
We form tensor product of the Toeplitz extension with
A
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
and apply
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
in order to get a fibre sequence

\Omega^{2}\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\beta_{A}}}{{\to}}\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}(A)\to\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}(A\otimes{\mathcal{T}}_{0})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}({\mathtt{id}}_{A}\otimes\pi)}\Omega\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}(A)\ ,

(3.23)

where
\beta_{A}:=d_{{\mathtt{id}}_{A}\otimes\pi}
is an instance of
d_{f}
in (
3.21
).
Here we used
K_{G}
-stability in order to remove tensor factors
K
, the isomorphism
{\mathbb{C}}\otimes A\cong A
and the equivalences
\Omega^{2}\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}(A)\simeq\Omega\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}(S(A))
and (
3.18
) in order to rewrite the terms appropriately. By (
3.21
) we know that
\phi(\beta_{A})\simeq\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(\partial_{{\mathtt{id}}_{A}\otimes\pi})
and therefore coincides with the Bott periodicity isomorphism from
[
GHT00
, Prop. 6.16]
(which is based on
[
Cun84
, Sec. 4]
). Since
\phi
is conservative we conclude that
\beta_{A}
is an equivalence.
The family
(\beta_{A})_{A\in GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
implements an equivalence of endofunctors
\beta:\Omega^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}}
of the left-exact
\infty
-category
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}

which shows that
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
is a stable
\infty
-category.
∎

Proposition 3.29
.

The functor
\phi:\mathrm{ho}\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
in (
3.17
) is an equivalence.

Proof.

The functor
\mathrm{ho}\circ\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{ho}\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
is a homotopy invariant and
K_{G}
-stable.
Since
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
is exact and
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
is stable by
3.28
the composition
\mathrm{ho}\circ\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}

is a half-exact functor to an additive category. In view of the universal property of
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}

formulated in the introduction of
[
GHT00
]
we get an inverse functor
\psi:\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}\to\mathrm{ho}\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
such that

\textstyle{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ho}\circ\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}}

commutes. Using the universal properties of both functors
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
and
\mathrm{ho}\circ\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
we can then conclude that

\phi
and
\psi
are inverse to each other.
∎

Lemma 3.30
.

The stable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
is countably cocomplete and the functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
is countable sum preserving.

Proof.

[
GHT00
, Prop. 7.1]
states that the functor

\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}

sends countable sums to coproducts.
Hence by
3.29
the same applies to
\mathrm{ho}\circ\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
. Since
\mathrm{ho}
is conservative and preserves coproducts
we conclude that
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
also sends countable sums to coproducts.
In particular,
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
admits countable coproducts. In fact, they are represented by countable sums in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. Since
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
is stable the existence of countable coproducts implies that
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
is countably cocomplete.
∎

Proposition 3.31
.

The precomposition by
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
induces for every countably cocomplete stable
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
an equivalence

(\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{\omega}}(\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,K_{G},\mathrm{ex},\oplus}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(3.24)

Proof.

The argument is similar to the proof of
[
BEL
, Prop. 2.23]
.
By definition the precomposition by
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
induces for any
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
an equivalence

{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{W_{{\mathrm{GHT}}}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ ,

where the superscript
W_{{\mathrm{GHT}}}
stands for functors which invert the classical
E
-theory equivalences.
We now assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
is stable. We claim that there is an inclusion

{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{h},K_{G},\mathrm{ex}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\subseteq{\mathbf{Fun}}^{{\mathrm{GHT}}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(3.25)

So assume that
F:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
is homotopy invariant,
K_{G}
-stable and exact. Then
\mathrm{ho}\circ F
is a homotopy invariant,
K_{G}
-stable and half-exact functor to the additive category
\mathrm{ho}{\mathbf{C}}
. In view of the universal property of
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
formulated in the introduction of
[
GHT00
]

we have a factorization

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 27.80066pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-27.80066pt\raise In particular,
\mathrm{ho}\circ F
sends classical
E
-theory equivalences to isomorphisms. Since
\mathrm{ho}
is conservative, the functor
F
sends classical
E
-theory equivalences to equivalences. This shows the claim.

We now build the following diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 53.35437pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-53.35437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
