143.486pt\raise-49.46559pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eqref{rtokpgpergertgetrg9}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 143.486pt\raise-57.0289pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-41.86836pt\raise-65.94116pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT},{\mathbf{C}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 58.59802pt\raise-70.66971pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.56187pt\raise-58.73503pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.22836pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT})^{*}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 87.5777pt\raise-65.94116pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 87.5777pt\raise-65.94116pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}^{{\mathrm{GHT}}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

(3.26)

For the lower square we only need that
{\mathbf{C}}
is a stable
\infty
-category.
Since
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is exact the restriction of
(\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT})^{*}
to exact functors induces the dashed functor which is fully faithful by construction. Using
the last statement of
3.27
one checks that it is essentially surjective.

If
{\mathbf{C}}
is in addition
countably cocomplete, then we also get the upper square.
Since
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
is countable sum preserving we get the further restriction to a fully faithful functor represented by the dotted arrow. Since countable coproducts in
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
are represented by countable sums in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
we conclude that the dotted arrow is also essentially surjective.
∎

Proposition 3.32
.

The stable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
has a canonical bi-exact symmetric monoidal structure
and
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
canonically refines to a symmetric monoidal functor.
The tensor product on
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
preserves countable colimits in each variable.
Pull-back along the symmetric monoidal refinement of
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
induces an equivalence

(\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{\omega}}(\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{h,K_{G},\mathrm{ex},\oplus}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ ,

(3.27)

Proof.

One checks that
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
from
3.25

is a symmetric monoidal Dwyer-Kan localization.
To this end we observe that for every
A
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
the functor
A\otimes-:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
preserves classical
E
-theory equivalences.
This follows from
[
GHT00
, Thm. 6.21 ]
stating that the maximal tensor product
descends to the classical equivariant
E
-theory.
Since
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}\circ(A\otimes-):GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
is homotopy invariant,
K_{G}
-stable,
exact and countable sum preserving we can conclude that the tensor product on
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
is bi-exact and preserves countable coproducts. It therefore preserves all countable colimits.
This argument is similar to the corressponding argument for
equivariant
KK
-theory
[
BEL
, Prop. 2.20]
.
∎

Remark 3.33
.

In
Section

3.6
we provide an alternative arguments for
3.29
and
3.31

which do not rely on the universal property of
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
as stated in
[
GHT00
]
. The careful reader may notice that the latter is stated in the introduction of
[
GHT00
]
but the details of its verification are left to the reader.

∎

3.4
Construction of
{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}}
by forcing the universal properties

In this section we describe the construction of a functor

\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}

(3.28)

by forcing the universal property required in
3.16
. We follow the line of argument of
[
Bun24b
]
in which the non-equivariant case is worked out. Our main motivation for this second construction is to provide a purely homotopy theoretic description of equivariant
E
-theory which is independent of
[
GHT00
]
.

We define the functor in (
3.28
) by the following chain of symmetric monoidal Dwyer-Kan localizations

\displaystyle\hat{\mathrm{e}}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{L_{h}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\xrightarrow{R_{K_{G}}L_{\hat{K}_{G}}}S_{K_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}

(3.29)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\xrightarrow{L_{\mathrm{ex}{,\oplus}}}S_{K_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,\mathrm{ex}{,\oplus}}\xrightarrow{\Omega^{2}}{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}}

1.

The functor
L_{h}
is the symmetric monoidal Dwyer-Kan localization at the homotopy equivalences, see
3.6
. The resulting
\infty
-category
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
is left-exact, pointed and has countable coproducts which are represented by countable free products of separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras. The functor
L_{h}
sends Schochet fibrant squares to cartesian squares and preserves countable coproducts. The arguments are the same as in the non-equivariant case discussed in
[
Bun24b
, Sec. 3]
.

2.

The functor
R_{K_{G}}L_{\hat{K}_{G}}
is the symmetric monoidal Dwyer-Kan localization at the
K_{G}
-equivalences, see
3.14
.
2
. It is the composition of a left and a right Bousfield localization which we will discuss in detail in
3.34
. The resulting
\infty
-category
S_{K_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
is left-exact and semi-additive. It furthermore admits countable coproducts which are represented by countable free products of algebras, but also by countable sums. Since the tensor product with a separable algebra
preserves countable sums,

R_{K_{G}}L_{\hat{K}_{G}}
preserves countable coproducts.

3.

The functor
L_{\mathrm{ex}{,\oplus}}
is the symmetric monoidal left exact and countable sum-preserving Dwyer-Kan localization (see
3.35
) generated by the collection of morphisms
the functor
R_{K_{G}}L_{\hat{K}_{G}}(L_{h}(\iota_{f}))
(see (
3.20
) for
\iota_{f}
) for all exact sequences
(
3.19
). The resulting
\infty
-category
S_{K_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,\mathrm{ex}}

is left-exact, semi-additive and admits countable coproducts, and the functor
L_{\mathrm{ex},\oplus}
preserves countable coproducts. The composition

GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\xrightarrow{L_{\mathrm{ex},{\oplus}}\circ R_{K_{G}}\circ L_{\hat{K}_{G}}\circ L_{h}}S_{K_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,\mathrm{ex}{,\oplus}}

is homotopy invariant,
K_{G}
-stable, exact and sends countable sums to countable coproducts, see
3.14
.
3
.
The arguments are similar as in the non-equivariant case disussed in
[
Bun24b
, Sec. 5]
.

4.

The left exact functor
\Omega^{2}
is the right-adjoint of a symmetric monoidal right Bousfield localization

\mathrm{incl}:{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}}\leftrightarrows S_{K_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,\mathrm{ex}{,\oplus}}:\Omega^{2}\ ,

(3.30)

where
{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}}
is the full subcategory of
S_{K_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,\mathrm{ex}{,\oplus}}
of group objects (see
3.36
). The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
is stable
and the composition

{\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\xrightarrow{\Omega^{2}\circ L_{\mathrm{ex}{,\oplus}}\circ R_{K_{G}}\circ L_{\hat{K}_{G}}\circ L_{h}}{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}}

(3.31)

is homotopy invariant,
K_{G}
-stable, exact, and countable sum-preserving.
The arguments are the same as in the non-equivariant case
[
Bun24b
, Sec. 7]
, and the functor (
3.31
) is the countable sum-preserving analogue of the
E
-theory functor
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
from
[
Bun24b
, Def. 7.9]
.

Remark 3.34
.

The details of the localization
R_{K_{G}}L_{\hat{K}_{G}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\to S_{K_{G}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
at the
K_{G}
-equivalences differs slightly from
the non-equivariant case considered in
[
Bun24b
, Sec. 4]
.
The arguments rely on the following analytic fact. If
H\to H^{\prime}
is an isometric inclusion of
\infty
-dimensional separable Hilbert spaces, then the induced corner inclusion
K(H)\to K(H^{\prime})
is a homotopy equivalence.
If
H
is a Hilbert space with a unitary
G
-action
\rho
, then we have an equivariant unitary isomorphism

L^{2}(G)\otimes H\cong L^{2}(G)\otimes\underline{H}
sending
\delta_{g}\otimes h
to

\delta_{g}\otimes\rho(g^{-1})h
, where
\delta_{g}
in
L^{2}(G)
is the basis element corresponding to
g
in
G
and
\underline{H}
denotes
H
with the trivial
G
-action.

Assume now that
H\to H^{\prime}
is an equivariant isometric inclusion of separable Hilbert spaces with unitary
G
-action. The isomorphisms above and the isomorphism
K_{G}\cong K(L^{2}(G))\otimes K(\ell^{2})
induce the vertical isomorphisms
in

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 50.2785pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-29.02269pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{K_{G}\otimes K(H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 94.13155pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-16.16446pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cong}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.68889pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 94.13155pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{K_{G}\otimes K(H^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 124.557pt\raise-16.16446pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cong}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 124.557pt\raise-23.68889pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-50.2785pt\raise-32.32892pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{K(L^{2}(G))\otimes K(\ell^{2}\otimes\underline{H})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.2785pt\raise-32.32892pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 74.2785pt\raise-32.32892pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{K(L^{2}(G))\otimes K(\ell^{2}\otimes\underline{H^{\prime}})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

Since the corner inclusion
K(\ell^{2}\otimes\underline{H})\to K(\ell^{2}\otimes\underline{H^{\prime}})
is a homotopy equivalence we see that

K_{G}\otimes K(H)\to K_{G}\otimes K(H^{\prime})
is a homotopy equivalence
and
K(H)\to K(H^{\prime})
is a
K_{G}
-equivalence. Consequently,
R_{K_{G}}L_{\hat{K}_{G}}
inverts the maps

K(H)\otimes A\to K(H^{\prime})\otimes A
for any
A
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

In the non-equivariant case we have a left upper corner inclusion
\epsilon:{\mathbb{C}}\to K:=K(\ell^{2})
sending
1
in
{\mathbb{C}}
to a one-dimensional projection in
K
. Then the stabilized corner inclusion

\epsilon\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{K}:K\cong{\mathbb{C}}\otimes K\to K\otimes K
is a homotopy equivalence. This turns
K
into an idempotent algebra in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
. By the general theory
[
Lura
, Sec, 4.8.2]
, the idempotent algebra
K
gives rise to a symmetric monoidal left Bousfield localization

L_{K}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\leftrightarrows L_{K}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}:\mathrm{incl}

with left-adjoint
L_{K}:=K\otimes-
. In the equivariant case for compact
G
, using the subspace of
G
-invariant functions on
G
, there still exists a left upper corner inclusion

{\mathbb{C}}\to K_{G}
and we again get a left Bousfield localization

L_{K_{G}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\leftrightarrows L_{K_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}:\mathrm{incl}\ .

If
G
is non-compact, then we procced in two steps.
We first consider the corner inclusion

\epsilon:{\mathbb{C}}\to\hat{K}_{G}:=K((L^{2}(G)\oplus{\mathbb{C}})\otimes\ell^{2})

(3.32)

sending
1
to the one-dimensional
G
-invariant projection in
\hat{K}_{G}
onto the subspace generated by
(0\oplus 1)\otimes e_{0}
, where
e_{0}
is the first basis vector of
\ell^{2}
.
One can find a commutative diagram of horizontal equivariant unitary inclusions of Hilbert spaces

\textstyle{(L^{2}(G)\oplus{\mathbb{C}})\otimes\ell^{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{x\mapsto((0{\oplus}1)\otimes e_{0})\otimes x}
\textstyle{((L^{2}(G)\oplus{\mathbb{C}})\otimes\ell^{2})\otimes((L^{2}(G)\oplus{\mathbb{C}})\otimes\ell^{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{(L^{2}(G)\oplus{\mathbb{C}})\otimes\ell^{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(L^{2}(G)\oplus{\mathbb{C}})\otimes H}

where the lower horizontal map is induced by an isometric inclusion
\ell^{2}\to H
of separable Hilbert spaces with trivial
G
-action. We can conclude that the map

\epsilon\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{\hat{K}_{G}}:\hat{K}_{G}\cong{\mathbb{C}}\otimes\hat{K}_{G}\to\hat{K}_{G}\otimes\hat{K}_{G}
is
isomorphic to the induced map
K(L^{2}(G)\oplus{\mathbb{C}})\otimes K(\ell^{2})\to K(L^{2}(G)\oplus{\mathbb{C}})\otimes K(H)

and therefore a homotopy equivalence. It follows that
\hat{K}_{G}
is an idempotent algebra in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
and that we have a symmetric monoidal left Bousfield localiztio

L_{\hat{K}_{G}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\leftrightarrows L_{\hat{K}_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}:\mathrm{incl}

(3.33)

with
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}=\hat{K}_{G}\otimes-
.
Note that
\hat{K}_{G}
represents the tensor unit of
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
.

We now consider the corner inclusion
\alpha:K_{G}\to\hat{K}_{G}
induced by the inclusion
L^{2}(G)\to L^{2}(G)\oplus{\mathbb{C}}
. We know that
\alpha\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{K_{G}}:K_{G}\otimes K_{G}\to\hat{K}_{G}\otimes K_{G}
is a homotopy equivalence and therefore an equivalence in
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
. Thus

K_{G}
becomes an idempotent coalgebra in
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
and gives rise to a symmetric monoidal right Bousfield localization

\mathrm{incl}:S_{K_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\leftrightarrows L_{\hat{K}_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}:R_{K_{G}}

with right-adjoint
R_{K_{G}}:=K_{G}\otimes-
.

We now consider the composition
of Dwyer-Kan localizations

R_{K_{G}}L_{\hat{K}_{G}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\to S_{K_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\ .

(3.34)

We check that this composition precisely inverts the
K_{G}
-equivalences.
If
f:A\to B
is
K_{G}
-equivalence in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
, then by definition
K_{G}\otimes f
is a homotopy equivalence.
But then also

\hat{K}_{G}\otimes K_{G}\otimes f
is a homotopy equivalence and
R_{K_{G}}L_{\hat{K}_{G}}(f)
is an equivalence.

If
R_{K_{G}}L_{\hat{K}_{G}}(f)
is an equivalence, then
\hat{K}_{G}\otimes K_{G}\otimes f

is a homotopy equivalence. Since also

\epsilon\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{K_{G}}
is a homotopy equivalence we conclude from

\textstyle{K_{G}\otimes A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{K_{G}\otimes A}}
\scriptstyle{K_{G}\otimes f}
\textstyle{\hat{K}_{G}\otimes K_{G}\otimes A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{K}_{G}\otimes K_{G}\otimes f}
\textstyle{K_{G}\otimes A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{K_{G}\otimes B}}
\textstyle{\hat{K}_{G}\otimes K_{G}\otimes B}

that

K_{G}\otimes f
is a homotopy equivalence.
Consequently,
f
is a
K_{G}
-equivalence.

The composition

{G}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\xrightarrow{L_{h}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\xrightarrow{R_{K_{G}}L_{\hat{K}_{G}}}S_{K_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}

in particular inverts left-upper corner inclusion
A\to A\otimes K
. This implies as in
[
Bun24b
, Sec. 4]
that
it sends the canonical maps
*_{i\in I}A_{i}\to\oplus_{i\in I}A_{i}
for countable families

(A_{i})_{i\in I}
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}

to equivalences. This fact for finite
I
implies the
semi-additivity of
S_{K_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
. It furthermore implies that
this category admits countable coproducts which are represented by
countable sums in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
, and that the functor

R_{K_{G}}L_{\hat{K}_{G}}\circ L_{h}
sends countable sums to countable coproducts.

∎

Remark 3.35
.

In order to obtain the left exact and countable sum-preserving Dwyer-Kan localization
L_{\mathrm{ex},\oplus}
we must form the closure of the generating set
W
of morphisms
R_{K_{G}}L_{\hat{K}_{G}}(L_{h}(\iota_{f}))
mentioned in
Item

3
under 2-out-of-3, pull-backs, and countable sums. Then for every left-exact semi-additive
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
admitting countable coproducts we have by construction the equivalences

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex},{\coprod_{\omega}}}(S_{K_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,\mathrm{ex}{,\oplus}},{\mathbf{C}})\xrightarrow[\simeq]{{L_{\mathrm{ex},\oplus}}^{*}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{lex},{\coprod_{\omega}},W}(S_{K_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h},{\mathbf{C}})

(3.35)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\simeq]{(R_{K_{G}}L_{\hat{K}_{G}}L_{h})^{\ast}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,K_{G},\mathrm{ex}+\mathrm{Sch}{,\oplus}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ ,

(3.36)

where the superscript
\mathrm{Sch}
stands for Schochet
exactness (see
3.6
), the symbol
W
for functors inverting maps in
W
, and
\coprod_{\omega}
and
\oplus
indicate functors preserving countable coproducts, or sending countable sums to countable coproducts, respectively.

∎

Remark 3.36
.

Since
S_{K_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h,\mathrm{ex}{,\oplus}}
is a semi-additive
\infty
-category,
every object
A
in this category is a commutative monoid. The object
A
is a group objects
A
if the shear map

A\times A\xrightarrow{(a,a^{\prime})\mapsto(a+a^{\prime},a)}A\times A

is an equivalence.

The counit

\Omega^{2}\to{\mathtt{id}}
of the right Bousfield localization (
3.30
) is induced for every
A
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
by the boundary maps
{\beta_{A}}
of the fibre sequences (compare with (
3.23
))

\Omega^{2}({\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}}(A))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\beta_{A}}}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}}(A)

associated to the exact sequences obtained by forming the tensor product of the Toeplitz sequence (
3.22
) with
A
.

∎

The first assertion in the following corollary is the equivariant analogue of
[
Bun24b
, (7.8)]
and an immediate consequence of the construction and (
3.35
) (also using
[
Bun24b
, Lem. 5.2]
in order to get rid of the Schochet exactness condition).
The second follows from
3.17
.
For the remaining two assertions
we use that

\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
is a composition of symmetric monoidal Dwyer-Kan localizations.

Corollary 3.37
.

1.

The precomposition by
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
induces for every countably cocomplete stable
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
an equivalence

(\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{\omega}}(\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,K_{G},\mathrm{ex},\oplus}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(3.37)

2.

\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
admits countable colimits and
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
preserves countably filtered colimits.

3.

The stable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
has a canonical bi-exact symmetric monoidal structure preserving countable colimits in each variable and
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
canonically refines to a symmetric monoidal functor.

4.

Pull-back along the symmetric monoidal refinement of
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
induces an equivalence

(\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{\omega}}(\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{h,K_{G},\mathrm{ex},\oplus}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(3.38)

We next prepare the argument showing that
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
is itself a Dwyer-Kan localization.
Let

{\mathbf{A}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Phi}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{B}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Psi}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{C}}

be a sequence of Dwyer-Kan localizations of
\infty
-categories.
In general we do not expect that the composition
\Psi\circ\Phi:{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
is a Dywer-Kan localization. But the following simple Lemma provides a sufficient condition for this to be the case.

Lemma 3.38
.

If
\Phi
is essentially surjective on morphisms, then

\Psi\circ\Phi:{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
is a Dwyer-Kan localizations.

Proof.

Let
W
be the class of morphisms which are inverted by
\Psi\circ\Phi
.
Then we want to show that this composition is the Dwyer-Kan localization at
W
. Let
\Theta:{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{D}}

be the Dwyer-Kan localization at
W
. Then by the universal property
of
\Theta
we have a triangle

In order to construct an inverse of

Since
\Theta
inverts all morphisms which are inverted by
\Phi
we get the dashed arrow
\Sigma
from the universal property of
\Phi
. We now use the universal property of
\Psi
in order to get the dotted arrow.
Let
f
be a morphism in
{\mathbf{B}}
which is inverted by
\Psi
.
We must show that
\Sigma(f)
is an equivalence.
By our assumption
f\simeq\Phi(\tilde{f})
for some morphism in

{\mathbf{A}}
. Since
\Psi(f)\simeq\Psi(\Phi(\tilde{f}))
is an equivalence, so is
\Theta(\tilde{f})\simeq\Sigma(f)
.
We therefore get the dotted arrow. In order to show
\Delta
and
\Gamma
are inverse to each other we
argue with the following diagrams:

The left diagram implies by the universal property of
\Theta
that
the composition
{\mathbf{D}}\to{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{D}}
is equivalent to the identity of
{\mathbf{D}}
.
In the right diagram we first use the universal property of
\Phi
in order to see that

\Psi\simeq\Delta\circ\Sigma
. We then use the universal property of

\Psi
in order to conclude that
\Delta\circ\Gamma
is
is equivalent to the identity of
{\mathbf{C}}
.
∎

The following results helps to check the
assumption of
3.38

Lemma 3.39
.

If
\Phi:{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{B}}
is a left-exact Dwyer-Kan localization between left-exact
\infty
-categories, then

\Phi
is essentially surjective on morphisms.

Proof.

The class
W
of morphisms inverted by
\Phi
satisfies a calculus of fractions.
∎

Proposition 3.40
.

The functor
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
is a symmetric monoidal Dwyer-Kan localization.

Proof.

By construction

\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
in (
3.29
) is a composition of the Dwyer-Kan localization
L_{h}
and a sequence of
left-exact Dwyer-Kan localizations. Furthermore
L_{h}
is obviously
essentially surjective on morphisms.
Using
3.38

and
3.39

finitely many times we conclude that
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}

is a Dwyer-Kan localization.
Since we already know that the tensor product on
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
descends through

\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
it is clearly a symmetric monoidal Dwyer-Kan localization.
∎

3.5
Construction of
{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}}
as the universal finitary invariant

In this section we construct the functor

\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}

as a composition

GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\xrightarrow{a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\xrightarrow{}\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}

(3.39)

The first is the universal homotopy invariant and countable filtered colimit preserving functor (see
3.46
), and the
second is a Dwyer-Kan localization forcing
K_{G}
-stability and stability using functors
R_{K_{G}}L_{\hat{K}_{G}}
and
\Omega^{2}
similarly as in (
3.29
). But note that we do not enforce exactness. In fact, exactness turns out to be an automatic property by
3.58
. In
3.50
, using the shape theory
[
Dad94
]
, we observe that
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
provides an
\infty
-categorical enhancement of the asymptotic morphism category
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
introduced in
[
GHT00
]
.
This comparison is relevant for our construction since we want to import the Bott map represented by an asymptotic morphism in
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
and the verification of its properties from the classical theory.

We construct the functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
as the composition

a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\xrightarrow{L_{h}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\xrightarrow{y}{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})\xrightarrow{R}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\ ,

(3.40)

where
L_{h}
is the Dwyer-Kan localization at the homotopy equivalences (explained in some detail in
3.6
),

y
has the usual meaning as introduced at the beginning of
Section

2.2
, and
R
is a Dwyer-Kan localization which we will describe in detail in the following.

We abbreviate the composition of the first two maps in (
3.40
) by
yL_{h}:=y\circ L_{h}
. For any system
(Z_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
we have a canonical map

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}yL_{h}(Z_{n})\to yL_{h}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}Z_{n})

(3.41)

in
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})
. We denote by

W_{R}
the set of the maps (
3.41
) for all systems
(Z_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. Then we define the functor

R:{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})\to\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}

as the Dwyer-Kan localization
at
W_{R}
.

We have already seen in
3.6
that
L_{h}
is a symmetric monoidal Dwyer-Kan localization for the maximal tensor product on
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. Its target is a pointed and left-exact symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
with a bi-left exact tensor product. The countable ind-completion
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})

of a pointed left exact
\infty
-category is again pointed and left-exact.
It has countable filtered colimits and these commute with finite limits. Furthermore the symmetric monoidal structure of
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
induces a bi-left exact symmetric monoidal structure on
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})
and a canonical symmetric monoidal refinement
of
y
. This tensor product preserves countable filtered colimits in each argument.
These structures are used to interpret
Theorem

3.41
.
4
below.

Theorem 3.41
.

1.

R
is the right-adjoint of a right Bousfield localization

L:\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\leftrightarrows{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}):R\ .

(3.42)

2.

The essential image of
L
is spanned by the objects
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}L_{h}(yA_{n})
for all explicit shape systems
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
.

3.

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is pointed, left-exact and has all countable filtered colimits. In
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
countable filtered colimits commute with finite limits. The functor
R
preserves countable filtered colimits.

4.

R
is a symmetric monoidal Dwyer-Kan localization and the induced tensor product on
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is bi-left exact and preserves countable filtered colimits in each argument.

Proof.

In order to show Assertion
1
we use the following well-known criterion to detect Bousfield localizations.

Remark 3.42
.

Let
R:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{D}}
be a Dwyer-Kan localization generated by a set
W
of morphisms in
{\mathbf{C}}
.
We say that an object
A
in
{\mathbf{C}}
is
W
-local if the functor
{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(A,-):{\mathbf{C}}\to\mathbf{An}
sends the morphisms in
W
to equivalences. A morphism
\epsilon:A^{\prime}\to A
in
{\mathbf{C}}
such that

R(\epsilon)
is an equivalence and
A^{\prime}
is
W
-local is called a
W
-local replacement of
A
.

If every object of
{\mathbf{C}}
admits a
W
-local replacement, then
R
is a right Bousfield localization. Furthermore, for any
W
-local
A
and any
B
in
{\mathbf{C}}
the map

R:{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(A,B)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(R(A),R(B))

(3.43)

is an equivalence.
If
{\mathbf{C}}
is left exact, then
R
is automatically a left-exact Dwyer-Kan localization and
{\mathbf{D}}
is again left exact.
Similarly, if
{\mathbf{C}}
is pointed, then so is
{\mathbf{D}}
.

Assume that
f:A\to B
is a morphism in
{\mathbf{C}}
such that
R(f)
is an equivalence and
\epsilon:B^{\prime}\to B
is a
W
-local replacement. Then
\delta:B^{\prime}\to A
is a

-local replacement
of

, where
\delta
is a preimage of
\epsilon
under the equivalence

f_{*}:{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(B^{\prime},A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(B^{\prime},B)
.
∎

We first claim that all objects of the form

for some

-local replacements. By

. We claim that then

) and therefore belongs to

) induces an equivalence

.
Expanding definitions this map is equivalent to the map

We now use that the map of systems

has the slp by

)
is an equivalence by

2.30
. This finishes the proof of the claim.

A general object of
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})

We now show Assertion

We must show that the morphism

is an equivalence, but this follows from the diagram once the other equivalences have been justified.
The morphism (1) is an equivalence since it is induced by the maps
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{k\in{\rm Nature}}yL_{h}(A_{n,k})\to yL_{h}(A_{n})
which belong to

for all

. A similar reasoning applies to the maps

. For

in

by a cofinality argument. In order to see that

is an equivalence we use the following remark.

Remark 3.43
.

In the context of a right Bousfield localization as in
3.42
, assume that
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
is a system consisting of
W
-local objects
such that
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}A_{n}
exists. Let
s:(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}\to(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{k\in{\rm Nature}}A_{k})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
be the structure map of the colimit. Then the map
R(s):(R(A_{n}))_{n\in{\rm Nature}}\to(R(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{k\in{\rm Nature}}A_{k}))_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
is the structure map for the colimit of
(R(A_{n}))_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
. This means that the canonical map

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}R(A_{n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}R(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}A_{n})

exists and is an equivalence.
∎

In order to apply this remark for the equivalence
(5)

we observe
that

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{k\in{\rm Nature}}yL_{h}(A_{n,k})
is
W_{R}
-local for every
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.

In order to see that countable filtered colimits in
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
commute with finite limits we employ the following general fact. Let
{\mathbf{D}}
be an
\infty
-category admitting finite limits and countable filtered colimits.

Lemma 3.44
.

The realization functor
\lvert-\rvert:{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})\to{\mathbf{D}}
commutes with finite limits if and only if
countable filtered colimits commute with finite limits in
{\mathbf{D}}
.

Proof.

We first assume that the realization functor commutes with finite limits.
Given a diagram
D:I\times K\to{\mathbf{D}}
with
I
countable filtered and
K
finite, we have to verify that the canonical map

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{k\in K}D(i,k)\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{k\in K}\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}D(i,k)

(3.45)

is an equivalence. Since filtered colimits commute with finite limits in anima, the canonical map

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}y(\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{k\in K}D(i,k))\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{k\in K}y(D(i,k))\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{k\in K}\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}y(D(i,k))

(3.46)

in
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})
is an equivalence. Applying the left exact functor
\lvert-\rvert:{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})\to{\mathbf{D}}
to the equivalence (
3.46
) we obtain (
3.45
) which is therefore also an equivalence.

The converse direction is obvious. ∎

for
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}

(3.47)

which commutes, because
R
preserves countable filtered colimits. We want to show that
\lvert-\rvert^{\prime\prime}
preserves finite limits. Since
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(R)
is a left exact localization (it is even a right Bousfield localization) and therefore admits an apropriate calculus of fractions, the functor
\lvert-\rvert^{\prime\prime}
is left exact if and only if the composite
\lvert-\rvert^{\prime\prime}{\circ}{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(R)
is left exact. By the commutativity of (
3.47
) this is equivalent to
R{\circ}\lvert-\rvert^{\prime}
being left exact. Finally, both
R
and
\lvert-\rvert^{\prime}
are left exact as right Bousfield localizations. For
\lvert-\rvert^{\prime}
this follows from the fact that
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})
is compactly assembled by
3.64
.

Remark 3.45
.

We will show in
3.68
below that
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is compactly assembled, for which the main ingredient is the right Bousfiled localization
R
. The above argument can actually be directly adapted to show that in every compactly assembled category
{\mathbf{D}}
admitting finite limits, countable filtered colimits commute with finite limits. The crucial point is to replace
R
in the above argument by the right Bousfield localization
\lvert-\rvert:{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})\to{\mathbf{D}}
.
∎

For Assertion
4
we must first show for any
\hat{A}
in
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})
that
\hat{A}\otimes-
sends the morphisms in
W_{R}
to morphisms which are inverted by
R
. Thus let
(Z_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
be a system in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and

be the canonical map (
3.41
). We must show that the morphism

R(\hat{A}\otimes f)
is an equivalence.
Since
R
(by Assertion
3
) and the tensor product in
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})
preserve countable filtered colimits
and this category is generated under countable filtered colimits by the image of
y

we can assume that
\hat{A}\simeq y(A)
for some
A
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
. We now argue using the following commutative diagram:

3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 122.11832pt\raise-48.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern The two upper vertical equivalences reflect the fact that
y
is symmetric monoidal (and also that
\otimes
preserves filtered colimits for the left one), the morphism
!!
belongs to
W_{R}
and is therefore inverted by
R
, and the equivalence
!
follows from the fact that the maximal tensor product in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
preserves countable filtered colimits. We can conclude that

R
also inverts the upper horizontal maps.

The bi-left exactness of the tensor product on

{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})
implies the bi-left exactness of the tensor product on
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. Indeed, using that
R
is a left-exact Dwyer-Kan localization, for every
A
in
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})
, the functor

R(A)\otimes-:\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is left exact if and only if its pull-back

R(A)\otimes R(-)\simeq R(A\otimes-):{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})\to\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}

along
R
is left-exact. But this is the case since
the tensor product on
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})
is bi-left exact and
R
is left-exact.
Also note that it suffices to consider the functors
R(A)\otimes-
for all
A
in
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})
since
R
is essentially surjective.

Finally the tensor product in
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
preserves countable filtered colimits in each variable since it is given by the formula
(A,B)\mapsto R(L(A)\otimes L(B))
and all functors in this formula (note that
\otimes
on the right hand side is the tensor product in
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})
and
L
is the left-adjoint of
R
as in (
3.42
))
preserve countable filtered colimits.

This finishes the proof of
Theorem

3.41
.
∎

We now show that the functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
from (
3.40
) satisfies the universal property required in
3.15
. The superscript
\mathrm{cfil}
indicates countable filtered colimit preserving functors.

Proposition 3.46
.

The functor

a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is homotopy invariant and preserves countable filtered colimits.
Moreover, for any
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
admitting countable filtered colimits the pull-back along
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
induces an equivalence

(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil}}(\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,\mathrm{cfil}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(3.48)

Proof.

The functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is homotopy invariant since the first functor
L_{h}
in (
3.40
) is so.
In order to show that
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
preserves countable filtered colimits we consider a system
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. We then observe that the comparison map

\hskip-14.22636pt{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A_{n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{\simeq}}}\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}Ry(L_{h}(A_{n})){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\to}}}R(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}yL_{h}(A_{n}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!!}}{{\to}}Ry(L_{h}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}A_{n})){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{\simeq}}a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}A_{n}))}

is an equivalence. In fact, the map marked by
!
is an equivalence since
R
commutes with countable filtered colimits by
Theorem

3.41
.
3
. Furthermore, the map marked by
!!
is an equivalence since it is obtained by applying
R
to a morphism of the form (
3.41
) from the set
W_{R}
.

In order to verify the universal properties we analyse the corresponding universal properties of all three functors appearing in (
3.40
). Since
R
is a Dwyer-Kan localization we have an equivalence

R^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{W_{R}}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}),{\mathbf{C}})\ .

Since
R
preserves countable filtered colimits this equivalence restricts to an equivalence

R^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil}}({\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{W_{R},\mathrm{cfil}}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}),{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(3.49)

Thereby, in order to see essential surjectivity note that if
R^{*}F
preserves countable filtered colimits, then

L^{*}R^{*}F\simeq F
also preserves countable filtered colimits.

It is the universal property of
y
that

y^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil}}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}),{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h},{\mathbf{C}})

is an equivalence. It restricts to an equivalence

y^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{W_{R},\mathrm{cfil}}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}),{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{fin}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h},{\mathbf{C}})\ ,

(3.50)

where the superscript
\mathrm{fin}
stands for finitary functors, by which we mean functors
F
such that
FL_{h}
preserves countable filtered colimits. To see essential surjectivity we consider a functor
F
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil}}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}),{\mathbf{C}})
such that
y^{*}F
is finitary. Then
F
inverts the morphisms (
3.41
) in
W_{R}
.
Indeed, we have the equivalences

F(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}yL_{h}(Z_{n}))\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}F(yL_{h}(Z_{n}))\simeq F(yL_{h}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}Z_{n}))

since
F
preserves countable filtered colimits and
y^{*}F
is finitary.

Finally, the equivalence (
3.3
) restricts to an equivalence

L_{h}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{fin}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,\mathrm{cfil}}({\mathbf{Fun}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})

(3.51)

by definition of the property of being finitary. We obtain the equivalence (
3.48
) by composing the equivalences (
3.49
), (
3.50
), and (
3.51
).
∎

In the following we state two more universal properties of the functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
involving the symmetric monoidal structures and left-exactness.
We first note that
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is by (
3.40
) a composition of functors which have symmetric monoidal refinements. In the following the subscript
\otimes/\mathrm{lax}
refers to symmetric monoidal or lax symmetric monoidal functors.

Proposition 3.47
.

The functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
has a symmetric monoidal refinement such that for any symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
with countable filtered colimits we have equivalences

(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{\mathrm{cfil}}(\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{h,\mathrm{cfil}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(3.52)

Proof.

The proof is completely analogous to the one of
3.46
. It uses that
L_{h}
(see (
3.4
)) and
R
(by
Theorem

3.41
.
4
) are symmetric monoidal Dwyer-Kan localizations, and the universal property of
y
.
∎

In the following
\mathrm{lex}
stands for left-exact functors and the superscript
\mathrm{Sch}
means Schochet exactness as explained in
3.6
.

Proposition 3.48
.

The functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is Schochet exact and for any left-exact
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
with countable filtered colimits which commute with finite limits we have an equivalence

(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{lex}}(\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{Sch}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(3.53)

Proof.

The equivalence (
3.5
)
restricts to an equivalence

L_{h}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{fin},\mathrm{lex}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{Sch}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})

(3.54)

by the definition of the notion of being finitary (see the proof of
3.46
for this notion).
We now argue that the equivalence (
3.50
) restricts to an equivalence

y^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{W_{R},\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{lex}}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}),{\mathbf{C}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{fin},\mathrm{lex}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h},{\mathbf{C}})

(3.55)

provided that
{\mathbf{C}}
is left exact and has countable filtered colimits which are exact.
First of all
y^{*}
preserves left-exact functors since
y
preserves finite limits.
In order to see essential surjectivity assume that
F
is in
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{W_{R},\mathrm{cfil}}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}),{\mathbf{C}})

such that
y^{*}F
is left-exact. Then
F
is the left Kan-extension of the left exact functor
y^{*}F
.
In view of
F\simeq{y_{!}y^{*}F\simeq}\lvert-\rvert_{{\mathbf{C}}}\circ{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(y^{*}F)}
it is left-exact since

\lvert-\rvert_{{\mathbf{C}}}
is left-exact by the assumption on
{\mathbf{C}}
and
{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(y^{*}F)}
is clearly left-exact.

Finally, the functor

R
, being a right Bousfield localization, is a left-exact Dwyer-Kan localization so that the equivalence
(
3.49
) restricts to an equivalence

R^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{lex}}(\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{W_{R},\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{lex}}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}),{\mathbf{C}})

(3.56)

for every left-exact
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
with countable filtered colimits.
The equivalence (
3.53
) is given by the
composition of the equivalences
(
3.56
),(
3.55
), and (
3.54
).∎

We now recall the results of
[
Dad94
]
and relate them with our theory. This reference deals with the case of the trivial group
G
. But using the equivariant generalization of Blackadar’s theory described in
Section

3.1

and the equivariant theory of asymptotic morphisms developed in
[
GHT00
]
the results of
[
Dad94
]
have straightforward equivariant generalizations.
In particular, in order to apply
[
Dad94
]
to the equivariant case we
just consider all
C^{*}
-algebras appearing in this paper with their natural
G
-action
and all homomorphisms are equivariant.

Let
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
be the category of separable
G
-
C^{\ast}
-algebras and homotopy classes of asymptotic morphisms
[
GHT00
]
. It comes with a canonical functor

a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}\ .

The maximal tensor product on
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
descends to a symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
such that
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
has a canonical symmetric monoidal refinement.
The combination of
[
Dad94
, Thm 3.5]
and
[
Dad94
, Thm 3.7]
can be stated as follows:

Theorem 3.49
.

There exists a right Bousfield localization

L_{0}:\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}\leftrightarrows\mathrm{ho}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})):R_{0}

such that the essential image of
L_{0}
consists of objects of the form
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}yL_{h}(A_{n})
for shape systems

(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.
3
3

3

The right-adjoint
R_{0}
is denoted by
L
in the reference
[
Dad94
]
since it is a version of a colimit functor.

The following proposition shows that
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
provides an
\infty
-categorical enhancement of
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
.

Proposition 3.50
.

There exists an essentially unique equivalence
filling the triangle

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 21.73686pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

(3.57)

Moreover,
\mathrm{ho}(R)
and
R_{0}
are symmetric monoidal Bousfield localizations and the commutative triangle above refines to one in symmetric monoidal categories.

Proof.

Since
R
is a right Bousfield localization, also
\mathrm{ho}(R)
is a right Bousfield localization whose left adjoint we will denote by
\mathrm{ho}(L)
. The functor
R_{0}
is a right Bousfield localization by
Theorem

3.49
.
In order to compare these right Bousfield localizations it suffices to compare their classes of local objects, i.e., the essential images of their left-adjoints. Comparing

Theorem

3.41
.
2
with
Theorem

3.49
we see that

\mathrm{EssIm}(\mathrm{ho}(L))\subseteq\mathrm{EssIm}(L_{0})

since explicit shape systems are in particular shape systems.
We will show that

\mathrm{EssIm}(L_{0})\subseteq\mathrm{EssIm}(\mathrm{ho}(L))\ .

To this end let
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
be any shape system for
A
. Then we have an isomorphism
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}yL_{h}(A_{n})\cong L_{0}(A)
in
\mathrm{EssIm}(L_{0})
. By
3.12
we can choose an explicit shape system
(A_{n}^{\prime})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
for
A
.
Then we have an equivalence
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}yL_{h}(A_{n})\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}yL_{h}(A^{\prime}_{n})
in

\mathrm{ho}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}))
since both objects represent
L_{0}(A)
.
We conclude that the object
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}yL_{h}(A_{n})
is in
\mathrm{EssIm}(\mathrm{ho}(L))
.

Providing a symmetric monoidal refinement of the dashed functor is the same as specifying a symmetric monoidal refinement of
R_{0}
, because
\mathrm{ho}(R)
is a symmetric monoidal Bousfield loclization. The tensor product in
\mathrm{ho}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}))
is given by

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}y(A_{n})\otimes\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{m\in{\rm Nature}}y(B_{m}):=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}y(A_{n}\otimes B_{n})

for any pair of systems
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
,
(B_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h}
.
Using the explicit formula for
R_{0}
given in
[
Dad94
, 2.2]

one checks that
R_{0}
has a canonical symmetric monoidal refinement.
∎

Remark 3.51
.

We have a commutative square

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 27.80066pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-27.80066pt\raise (3.58)

While the functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
has nice characterizations in terms universal properties (see
3.46
and
3.47
) we can not characterize
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
in a similar manner. The reason is that the universal properties for
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
involve countable filtered colimits, but
\mathrm{ho}
does not preserve such colimits.
∎

We now consider the second map in (
3.39
) which we decompose as a composition

\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\xrightarrow{L_{K_{G}}}L_{\hat{K}_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\xrightarrow{R_{K_{G}}}S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\xrightarrow{R_{S^{2}}}\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}\ .

(3.59)

Thereby
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}
is a left Bousfield localization and
R_{K_{G}}
and
R_{S^{2}}
are right Bousfield localizations. We call
S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
the
K_{G}
-stabilization of
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. All localizations are in addition symmetric monoidal, and the right-adjoints preserve countable filtered colimits. In the following we describe the functors in detail.

The first two localizations are analogous to the ones discussed in
3.34
. Recall that we have a map

\epsilon:{\mathbb{C}}\to\hat{K}_{G}
which presents
\hat{K}_{G}
as an idempotent algebra in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
.
Since
Ry:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\to\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is symmetric monoidal we get an idempotent algebra
Ry(\hat{K}_{G})
in
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. It induces the symmetric monoidal left Bousfield localization

L_{\hat{K}_{G}}:=Ry(\hat{\mathrm{K}}_{G})\otimes-:\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\leftrightarrows L_{\hat{K}_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:\mathrm{incl}\ .

The following statements are general facts about left Bousfield localizations induced by an idempotent algebra
in a situation where the tensor product is bi-left exact and preserves countable filtered colimits in each argument.
The right adjoint of the localization
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}
preserves countable filtered colimits, and the tensor product in
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
preserves countable filtered colimits in each argument. Furthermore, the functor
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}
is a left-exact Dwyer-Kan localization,
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is pointed and left exact,
the tensor product in
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is also bi-left exact, and countable filtered colimits in this category commute with finite limits.

It follows from the universal property of the left adjoint
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}
in (
3.33
) that we have a symmetric monoidal factorization

\textstyle{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L_{\hat{K}_{G}}Ry}
\scriptstyle{L_{\hat{K}_{G}}}
\textstyle{L_{\hat{K}_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
\textstyle{L_{\hat{K}_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bar{L}_{\hat{K}_{G}}}

as indicated by the dotted arrow.
The inclusion
\alpha:K_{G}\to\hat{K}_{G}
presents
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}(K_{G})
as an idempotent coalgebra in

L_{\hat{K}_{G}}GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
. Hence
\bar{L}_{\hat{K}_{G}}(L_{\hat{K}_{G}}(K_{G}))
has the structure of an idempotent coalgebra in
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.
We therefore get a symmetric monoidal right Bousfield localization

\mathrm{incl}:S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\leftrightarrows L_{\hat{K}_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:R_{K_{G}}:=\bar{L}_{\hat{K}_{G}}(L_{\hat{K}_{G}}(K_{G}))\otimes-\ .

The following statements are again general facts about a right Bousfield localization induced by an idempotent coalgebra
in a situation where the tensor product is bi-left exact and preserves countable filtered colimits in each argument.
The functor
R_{K_{G}}
preserves countable filtered colimits. The tensor product in
S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
preserves countable filtered colimits in each argument.
The localization
R_{K_{G}}
is a left-exact Dwyer Kan localization and

S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is left exact and pointed. In this category
countable filtered colimits commute with finite limits and the tensor product is bi-left exact.

We next state the universal property of the composition

a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},K_{G}}:=R_{K_{G}}\circ L_{\hat{K}_{G}}\circ a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\ .

Proposition 3.52
.

The functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},K_{G}}
is homotopy invariant, Schochet exact, preserves countable filtered colimits, and is
K_{G}
-stable. Moreover, pull-back along this functor induces for any
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
admitting countable filtered colimits an equivalence

(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},K_{G}})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil}}(S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,\mathrm{cfil},K_{G}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(3.60)

Proof.

It is clear by construction that
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},K_{G}}
has the asserted properties. It remains to verify the equivalence
(
3.60
). A map
f
in
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
will be called
K_{G}
-equivalence if
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(K_{G})\otimes f
is an equivalence in
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. Then
by construction we have an equivalence

L_{\hat{K}_{G}}^{*}R^{*}_{K_{G}}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil}}(S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil},K_{G}}(\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ ,

(3.61)

where the superscript
K_{G}
in the right-hand side means functors which send
K_{G}
-equivalences in the sense above to equivalences. It remains to show that the equivalence (
3.48
) restricts to an equivalence

(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil},K_{G}}(\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,\mathrm{cfil},K_{G}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(3.62)

The open part is essential surjectivity. Let
F
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil}}(\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})
be such that

(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})^{*}F
is
K_{G}
-stable as defined in
3.14
.
2
. By definition this means that
(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})^{*}F

sends all
K_{G}
-equivalences in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
to
equivalences. But since the localizations
L_{\hat{\mathrm{K}}_{G}}
and
R_{K_{G}}
are generated by idempotent algebras or coalgebras
we must only show that
F
inverts the maps
\hat{A}\to a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(\hat{K}_{G})\otimes\hat{A}
and
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(K_{G})\otimes\hat{A}\to a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(\hat{K}_{G})\otimes\hat{A}
for all objects
\hat{A}
of
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. Since

a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is essentially surjective we can choose an equivalence
\hat{A}\simeq a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)
for some
A
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. Then these maps are images under
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
of the
K_{G}
-equivalences

A\to\hat{K}_{G}\otimes A
and
K_{G}\otimes A\to\hat{K}_{G}\otimes A

in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and therefore
inverted by
(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})^{*}F
by assumption.
∎

We now state the universal properties of
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},K_{G}}
involving symmetric monoidal structures and finite limits.

Proposition 3.53
.

The functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},K_{G}}
is symmetric monoidal and for every symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
admitting countable filtered colimits we have an equivalence

(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},K_{G}})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{\mathrm{cfil}}(S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{h,\mathrm{cfil},K_{G}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(3.63)

Proof.

In order to see this we compose the restriction of (
3.52
) to
K_{G}
-stable functors with the symmetric monoidal version

L_{\hat{K}_{G}}^{*}R^{*}_{K_{G}}:{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{\mathrm{cfil}}(S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{\mathrm{cfil},K_{G}}(\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})

of (
3.61
) which ist justified by the fact that
R_{K_{G}}
and
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}
are symmetric monoidal localizations.
∎

Proposition 3.54
.

The functor
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},K_{G}}
is Schochet exact and
for every
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
with finite limits and exact countable filtered colimits we have an equivalence

(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},K_{G}})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{lex}}(S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,\mathrm{cfil},K_{G},\mathrm{Sch}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(3.64)

Proof.

We compose the restriction
of (
3.53
) to
K_{G}
-stable functors
with the functor

L_{\hat{K}_{G}}^{*}R^{*}_{K_{G}}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{lex}}(S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{lex},K_{G}}(\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

First of all
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}^{*},R^{*}_{K_{G}}
preserves left-exact functors since
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}
and
R_{K_{G}}
preserve finite limits.
For essential surjectivity we argue as in the justification of the equivalence (
3.62
) above. Alternatively one could use (
3.62
) and (
3.53
) and that
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}^{*},R^{*}_{K_{G}}
are left exact localizations.
∎

In the last step we construct the localization denoted by
R_{S^{2}}
in (
3.59
).
To this end we import the Bott map

\beta:a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(S^{2}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes K_{G})\to a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(K_{G})

in
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
from
[
GHT00
, Prop 6.16]
. Recall the definition of
{\mathtt{Hom}}
-sets in the classical equivariant
E
-theory

{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}}(\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(A),\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(B)):={\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}}(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(S^{2}(A)\otimes K_{G}),a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(S^{2}(B)\otimes K_{G}))\ .

(3.65)

The statement
[
GHT00
, Prop 6.16]
that
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(S^{2}({\mathbb{C}}))\otimes\beta
is an equivalence in
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
boils down to
the statement that

\hskip-14.22636pta^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(S^{2}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes K_{G})\otimes a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(S^{2}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes K_{G})\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{id}}\otimes\beta}a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(S^{2}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes K_{G})\otimes a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(K_{G})\cong a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(S^{2}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes K_{G})

is an isomorphism in
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
. We now consider the symmetric monoidal localization
\mathrm{ho}(S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})\simeq R_{0,K_{G}}L_{0,\hat{K}_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
of
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
at the
K_{G}
-equivalences
(the equivalence follows from
3.50
). We let
S^{2}
in
\mathrm{ho}(S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})
denote the object represented by
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(S^{2}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes K_{G})
under the equivalence above.
Since
K_{G}
represents the tensor unit of
\mathrm{ho}(S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})
we can consider the pair

(S^{2},\beta)

as an idempotent coalgebra in
\mathrm{ho}(S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})
. Since the structure of
an idempotent coalgebra
can be lifted along the functor
\mathrm{ho}
, we get an idempotent coalgebra
(S^{2},\beta)
in
S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. Then
R_{S^{2}}
in (
3.59
)
is defined as the right Bousfield localization induced by this coalgebra.

Proposition 3.55
.

1.

The category
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
is pointed, left-exact and admits countable filtered colimits which commute with finite limits.

2.

R_{S^{2}}
is a left-exact Dwyer-Kan localization and preserves countable filtered colimits.

3.

R_{S^{2}}
is a symmetric monoidal Dwyer-Kan localization and the induced tensor product on
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
is bi-left exact and preserves countable filtered colimits in each argument.

4.

The category
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
is stable.

5.

The functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
is homotopy invariant Schochet exact,
K_{G}
-stable and preserves countable filtered colimits.

6.

Pull-back along
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
induces for every stable countably cocomplete
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
an equivalence

(\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}})^{\ast}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{lex}}(\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}},{\mathbf{C}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,K_{G},\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{Sch}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(3.66)

7.

Pull-back along the symmetric monoidal refinement of
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
induces for every countably cocomplete symmetric monoidal stable
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
an equivalence

(\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}})^{\ast}:{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{lex}}(\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}},{\mathbf{C}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{h,K_{G},\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{Sch}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(3.67)

Proof.

The Assertions
1
,
2
and
3
follow from the corresponding properties of
S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and its symmetric monoidal structure and the fact that
R_{S^{2}}
is a right Bousfield localization induced by a idempotent coalgebra. The arguments are analogous to the case of
R_{K_{G}}
above.

In order to see Assertion
4
we note that
the functor
S^{2}\otimes-
is equivalent to the loop endofunctor
\Omega^{2}
, see the proof of
3.28
for a related argument. The map

\beta:S^{2}\to{\mathbf{1}}
in
S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is inverted by
R_{S^{2}}
and induces an equivalence
S^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathtt{id}}
on
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
. We thus get an equivalence of functors
\Omega^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathtt{id}}
which implies stability.

In order to show Assertion
5
we use
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}\simeq R_{S^{2}}\circ a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},K_{G}}
,
3.52
stating that
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},K_{G}}
has these properties, and Assertion
2
ensuring that the stated properties are preserved upon composition with
R_{S^{2}}
.

We now show Assertion
6
.
Let
W_{R_{2}}
be the set of morphisms
\beta\otimes A:S^{2}\otimes A\to A
for all
A
in
S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. Then by definition of
R_{S^{2}}
, for every
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
we have an equivalence

R_{S^{2}}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{W_{S^{2}}}(S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

We now assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
is stable and countably cocomplete. Then it
restricts to the first equivalence in the composition

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{lex}}(\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle R_{S^{2}}^{*},\simeq}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle\>{\mathbf{Fun}}^{W_{S^{2}},\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{lex}}(S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})

(3.68)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle\>\>{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{lex}}(S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})\ .

We must show that the second functor is an equivalence, too.
It is here we use that
{\mathbf{C}}
is stable. Let
F
be in
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{lex}}(S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})
. We then must show that

F
inverts the morphisms in
W_{S^{2}}
. But this follows from

\Omega^{2}F(\beta\otimes A)\simeq F(\Omega^{2}\beta\otimes A)\simeq F(S^{2}\otimes\beta\otimes A)

and the fact that
S^{2}\otimes\beta
is an equivalence. Here we used
the left-exactness of
F
for the first equivalence and
the fact that
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},K_{G}}
is homotopy invariant and reduced for the second (see the argument for Assertion
4
).
The composition of (
3.68
) and (
3.64
) yields the desired equivalence.

For Assertion
7
we use that
R_{S^{2}}
is a symmetric monoidal localization which implies the symmetric monoidal analogue

R_{S^{2}}^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{lex}}(\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}_{\otimes/\mathrm{lax}}^{\mathrm{cfil},\mathrm{lex}}(S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}},{\mathbf{C}})

(3.69)

of (
3.68
).
We get (
3.67
) by composing (
3.69
) with
the restriction of (
3.63
) to left-exact, respectively Schochet exact functors (combine
3.53
and
3.54
).
∎

The following proposition shows that the homotopy category of
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
is canonically equivalent to the classical equivariant
E
-theory category.

Proposition 3.56
.

There exists a canonical commutative square

\textstyle{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ho}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\phi}

(3.70)

of symmetric monoidal functors where
\phi
is an equivalence

Proof.

We expand the diagram as

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 42.66632pt\raise-59.66005pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ho}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 72.63396pt\raise-65.0906pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.93478pt\raise-65.0906pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 72.63396pt\raise-65.0906pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
