143.07921pt\raise-58.9795pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 142.35837pt\raise-69.81915pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 181.88333pt\raise-65.0906pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 151.5361pt\raise-65.0906pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 181.88333pt\raise-65.0906pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ ,

(3.71)

where the right vertical arrow is the composition of the one-categorical left-and right Bousfield localizations

R_{0,S^{2},K_{G}}:\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}\xrightarrow{L_{0,\hat{K}_{G}}}L_{0,\hat{K}_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}\xrightarrow{R_{0,K_{G}}}S_{0,K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}\xrightarrow{R_{0,S^{2}}}\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}

induced by the idempotent algebra
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(\hat{K}_{G})
in
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}

and the coalgebras
L_{0,\hat{K}_{G}}(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(K_{G}))
in
L_{0,\hat{K}_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
and

R_{K_{G}}L_{0,\hat{K}_{G}}(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(S^{2}(({\mathbb{C}})\otimes K_{G})))
in
S_{0,K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
.
In order to justify that this gives the classical
E
-theory we first observe that for
A,B
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
the mapping set in the localization
are given by

{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}}(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(A\otimes S^{2}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes\hat{K}_{G}\otimes K_{G}),a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}(S\otimes S^{2}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes\hat{K}_{G}\otimes K_{G}))\ .

The map
\epsilon:{\mathbb{C}}\to\hat{K}_{G}
from (
3.32
) induces a homotopy equivalence
K_{G}\to\hat{K}_{G}\otimes K_{G}
.
Using this homotopy equivalence and the homotopy invariance of
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
we can replace the tensor factor
\hat{K}_{G}\otimes K_{G}
by
K_{G}
and get the usual formula (
3.65
). The functor

\mathrm{ho}(R_{S^{2}}R_{K_{G}}L_{\hat{K}_{G}})
simillarly decomposes as a sequence of localizations

\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{ho}(L_{\hat{K}_{G}})}\mathrm{ho}(L_{\hat{K}_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{ho}(R_{K_{G}})}\mathrm{ho}(S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{ho}(R_{S^{2}})}\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}})\ .

This implies that the equivalence
\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
obtained in
3.50
descends to the desired equivalence
\phi
in (
3.71
).
∎

3.6
Comparison of the models for
\mathrm{E}
-theory

In the preceding three sections we have constructed three functors

\hskip-14.22636pt\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}\ ,\quad{\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}}\ ,\quad\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}

from separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras to
stable
\infty
-categories which are characterized by slightly different
universal properties (
3.16
) for
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
,
(
3.37
) for
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
, and (
3.66
) for
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
.
In order to recall,
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
is by definition the universal functor from
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
to an
\infty
-category which inverts the
classical
E
-theory equivalences.
The functor
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
is by construction the universal homotopy invariant,
K_{G}
-stable, exact and countable sum preserving functor to a countably cocomplete stable
\infty
-category. Finally,
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
is by construction the universal homotopy invariant, countable filtered colimit preserving,
K_{G}
-stable and Schochet exact functor
to a countably cocomplete stable
\infty
-category.

By an inspection of the universal properties above we observe that
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
misses the exactness property of the other two functors.
But
3.58
shows that

\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
is automatically exact. This will be used in
3.59
in order to verify that
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
is equivalent to
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
. In
3.40
we have seen that
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}

is also a Dwyer-Kan localization. We use this information in
3.60
in order to show that
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
and
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
are equivalent as well.

Remark 3.57
.

This argumentation is independent of the verification of the universal property of the classical equivariant
E
-theory functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}
stated in
[
GHT00
]
. If one is willing to use it one could immediately conclude that

\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
and
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}
are equivalent as symmetric monoidal functors in an essentially unique manner by comparing
(
3.38
) and (
3.27
). See also
3.33
.
∎

Proposition 3.58
.

The functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
is exact.

Proof.

In view of the universal property of
L_{h}
from (
3.2
) and the homotopy invariance of
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
and
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}

the commutative square (
3.70
) induces a commutative square

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 31.27348pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-31.27348pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.6011pt\raise 7.2339pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.20059pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},h,0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 67.02701pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-16.45613pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.20059pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},h,\mathrm{fin}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 67.02701pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},0}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-17.93478pt\raise-32.91226pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 33.98608pt\raise-27.4817pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ho}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.27348pt\raise-32.91226pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 55.27348pt\raise-32.91226pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 82.37497pt\raise-16.45613pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 71.56664pt\raise-16.45613pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 82.37497pt\raise-8.3611pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

(3.72)

We consider a short exact sequence
0\to A\to B\to C\to 0
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and let
c:L_{h}(A)\to{\mathrm{Fib}}(L_{h}(B)\to L_{h}(C))
be the induced comparison map in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h}
.
We must show that this map gets inverted by the canonical functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},h,\mathrm{fin}}
.
The functor
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},h,0}
inverts
c
by
[
GHT00
, Prop. 5.14]
. Since
\mathrm{ho}
is conservative
this implies that also
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},h,\mathrm{fin}}
inverts
c
.
∎

Proposition 3.59
.

There exists an essentially unique commutative triangle

\textstyle{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}}

of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories.

Proof.

We use that a left exact and countable filtered colimit preserving functor between countably cocomplete stable
\infty
-categories is the same as a countable colimit preserving functor.
Since
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
is exact by
3.58
we obtain the symmetric monoidal and countable colimit preserving dashed arrow from the universal property (
3.38
). Since an exact functor is Schochet exact,

\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
admits countable colimits and

\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
preserves filtered colimits by
3.37
.
2
we get a potential inverse symmetric monoidal and countable colimit preserving arrow from (
3.67
). Using the universal properties of
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
and
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
again we see that the these arrows are indeed mutually inverse to each other.
∎

Proposition 3.60
.

There exists an essentially unique commutative triangle

\textstyle{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}}

of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories.

Proof.

By
3.40
we know that

\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep},\oplus}^{G}
is a symmetric monoidal Dwyer-Kan localization. By
3.59
and
3.56
this functor inverts precisely the classical
E
-theory equivalences. Since this is the defining property of the symmetric monoidal Dwyer-Kan localization
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},GHT}

we get the assertion.
∎

From now on we can safely omit the subscripts indicating the particular constructions of the equivariant
E
-theory functors and just use the notation

\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}

for any of them.

Recall from
[
BEL
, Thm. 1.5]
that

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}

is the universal homotopy invariant,
K_{G}
-stable and semi-exact functor to a stable
\infty
-category, where semi-exactness means that it sends exact sequences in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
with an equivariant completely positive contractive split to a fibre sequence.
In addition we know that
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
admits countable colimits, and that the functor

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
preserves countable sums. Pull-back along
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
therefore induces for every countably cocomplete stable
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
an equivalence

(\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G})^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{\omega}}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G},{\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h,K_{G},\mathrm{se},\oplus}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{{\mathrm{sep}}},{\mathbf{C}})\ ,

where the superscript
\mathrm{se}
indicates semi-exact functors.
The only difference to
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is that the latter sends all exact sequences to fibre sequences. We therefore have a natural commutative diagram:

\textstyle{GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c}
\textstyle{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}

(3.73)

Proposition 3.61
.

The functor
c
presents its targets as a Verdier quotient.

Proof.

We let
N
denote the kernel of
c
. Then the functor

GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{{\mathrm{sep}}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}/N

(3.74)

is exact since by the exactness of
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
for every exact sequence
0\to A\to B\to C\to 0
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{{\mathrm{sep}}}
the object

{\mathrm{Fib}}(\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A)\to{\mathrm{Fib}}(\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(B)\to\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(C)))

belongs to
N
. In addition, the functor in (
3.74
) is homotopy invariant,
K_{G}
-stable and countable sum-preserving since
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
has these properties.
By the universal property of
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and the properties of (
3.74
) discussed above we obtain an inverse
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}/N
of the canonical functor
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}/N\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
induced by
c
.
∎

We finally use
3.61
in order to descend some group-change adjunctions from equivariant
KK
-theory to
E
-theory.
Let
H
be a subgroup of
G
and let
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}_{H}
and
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}
be the functors induced by the induction and restriction functors on the level of separable
C^{*}
-algebras.

Proposition 3.62
.

We have an adjunction

{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}:\mathrm{E}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}{\rightleftarrows}\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}

(3.75)

where
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}
preserves countable colimits.

Proof.

We start from the adjunction

{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}:\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{H}{\rightleftarrows}\mathrm{KK}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}

(3.76)

obtained by restricting the adjunction in
[
BEL
, Thm. 1.23.1]
to the subcategories of compact objects.
Since the induction and restriction functors on the level of separable
C^{*}
-algebras preserve exact sequences and countable sums
they descend through the Verdier quotient
c
in
3.61
. Hence the adjunction (
3.76
) induces the adjunction (
3.75
) whose right-adjoint also preserves countable colimits.
∎

In the following
-\rtimes_{\max}G
and
\mathrm{Res}_{G}
denote the functors induced by the functors on separable
C^{*}
-algebras given by the maximal crossed product and the functor equipping a
C^{*}
-algebra with the trivial
G
-action.

Proposition 3.63
.

1.

If
G
is finite, then we have an adjunction

\mathrm{Res}_{G}:\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}\leftrightarrows\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}:-\rtimes_{\max}G

(3.77)

whose right-adjoint preserves countable colimits.

2.

We have an adjunction

-\rtimes_{\max}G:\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\leftrightarrows\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}:\mathrm{Res}_{G}

(3.78)

whose right-adjoint preserves countable colimits.

Proof.

We argue similarly as in the proof of
3.62
in order to descend the corresponding adjunctions in
KK
-theory
[
BEL
, Thm.1.23.3 & 4]
to
E
-theory. Here we use that
\mathrm{Res}_{G}
and
-\rtimes_{\max}G
on the level of separable
C^{*}
-algebras preserve exact sequences and countable sums.
∎

3.7
\mathrm{E}
-theory is compactly assembled

Recall
2.12
characterising an
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
admitting countable filtered colimits as compactly assembled
if its realization functor is a right-adjoint of an adjunction

S:{\mathbf{C}}\leftrightarrows{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}}):|-|\ .

The left-adjoint
S
is called the shape functor. It is automatically fully faithful by
2.8
.

The main result of this section is
Theorem

3.67
stating that the countably cocomplete stable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
representing equivariant
E
-theory introduced in
3.16
is compactly assembled.
The idea is to use the construction of the version
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
of this category by a series of
left- and right Bousfield localizations

\hskip-14.22636pt{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})\xrightarrow{R}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\xrightarrow{L_{\hat{K}_{G}}}L_{\hat{K}_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\xrightarrow{R_{K_{G}}}S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\xrightarrow{R_{S^{2}}}\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}

(3.79)

which are described in detail in
Section

3.5
. We start with the observation that an
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}
-completion is always compactly assembled and then use that the property of being compactly assembled
is inherited by appropriate Bousfield localizations.

Proposition 3.64
.

For any
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
the category
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})
is compactly assembled and its shape functor is given by
\hat{y}:={\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(y):{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})\to{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}}))
.

Proof.

(compare with
[
Lurb
, Ex. 21.1.2.13]
)
We consider the functors
y:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})
and
y_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})}:{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})\to{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}}))
. We let
|-|_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})}
denote the left-adjoint of
y_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})}
. In order to show that
\hat{y}
is a further left adjoint of
|-|_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})}
we start with the bi-natural equivalence

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}}))}(y_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})}\circ y(-),y_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})}(-))

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})}(y(-),-)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\>\>{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})}(y(-),|y_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}}(-)|_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})})

of functors
{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{op}}\times{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}}({\mathbf{C}})\to\mathbf{An}
. Left Kan-extension along
{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{op}}\times{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}}({\mathbf{C}})\to{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{op}}\times{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}}))
induces the bi-natural equivalence

{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}}))}(y_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})}\circ y(-),-)\simeq{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})}(y(-),|-|_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})})\ .

Finally right Kan extension along
{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{op}}\times{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}}))\to{\mathtt{Ind}}^{{\aleph_{1}}}({\mathbf{C}})^{\mathrm{op}}\times{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}}))
induces the required bi-natural equivalence

{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}}))}(\hat{y}(-),-)\simeq{\mathtt{Map}}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})}(-,|-|_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{C}})})\ .

∎

Let

L:{\mathbf{D}}\leftrightarrows{\mathbf{C}}:R

be a right Bousfield localization and recall that notation
\hat{R}
from (
2.11
).

Proposition 3.65
.

If
R
preserves countable filtered colimits and
{\mathbf{C}}
is compactly assembled, then
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly assembled
and its shape functor is given by
\hat{R}\circ S_{{\mathbf{C}}}\circ L
.

Proof.

The equivalence (
2.12
) can be rewritten as a commutative square

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 23.85411pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.59717pt\raise 0.0pt\raise-16.49501pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{R}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 77.85411pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{D}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

We furthermore have a square

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 23.85411pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.59717pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.85413pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.63815pt\raise-25.86919pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.1014pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{R}\circ S_{{\mathbf{C}}}\circ L}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 47.85411pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 77.85411pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{D}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

The vertical maps in both squares are Dwyer-Kan localizations, and the two upper horizontal arrows are adjoint to each other. This implies by
[
Cis19
, Prop. 7.1.14]
that the lower to morphisms are also adjoint to each other. So
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly assembled and the shape functor of
{\mathbf{D}}
is given by the composition

\hat{R}\circ S_{{\mathbf{C}}}\circ L
.
∎

Let

L:{\mathbf{C}}\rightleftarrows{\mathbf{D}}:R

be a left Bousfield localization and let
\hat{L}
be as in (
2.11
).

Proposition 3.66
.

If
R
preserves countable filtered colimits and
{\mathbf{C}}
is compactly assembled, then
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly assembled
and its shape functor is given by
\hat{L}\circ S_{{\mathbf{C}}}\circ R
.

Proof.

By
2.17
the functor
L
preserves shapes.
Since
L
is essentially surjective it is then clear from
2.19
that
{\mathbf{D}}
is compactly assembled.
Furthermore, by
2.17
we have
\hat{L}\circ S_{{\mathbf{C}}}\simeq S_{{\mathbf{D}}}\circ L
.
We precompose with
R
and use
L\circ R\simeq{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
on the right-hand side in order to conclude the formula for the shape functor of
{\mathbf{D}}
.
∎

Theorem 3.67
.

The category
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is compactly assembled.

Proof.

According to
Section

3.6
we can use the construction of
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
as

\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},\mathrm{fin}}
. The latter is presented by the sequence (
3.79
) of left- and right Bousfield localizations of
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})
. This initial category is compactly assembled by
3.64
. We then use
3.66
and
3.65
in order to see that being compactly assembled is inherited by the localizations.
To this end we use that the right-adjoints of all these Bousfield localizations preserve countable filtered colimits
as seen in
Section

3.5
.
∎

The proof of
Theorem

3.67
shows that also the intermediate categories in the sequence (
3.79
) are compactly assembled. For later use we record:

Corollary 3.68
.

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is compactly assembled.

We say that
A
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is twice suspended and
K_{G}
-stable if it isomorphic to
A\otimes S^{2}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes K_{G}
for some
A^{\prime}
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.
We let
S_{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
and
S_{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
denote the shape functors for
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.
Recall from
3.10
the notion of a shape system in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

Proposition 3.69
.

Assume that
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
is a shape system for
A
.

1.

We have an equivalence
S_{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(A)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}y_{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A_{n}))
.

2.

If
A
is twice syspended and
K_{G}
-stable, then
S_{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(A)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}y_{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A_{n}))
.

Proof.

By
3.65
we have an equivalence

S_{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(A)\simeq\hat{R}(S_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})}(L(A)))\ .

From the proof of
Theorem

3.41
we know that

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}yL_{h}(A_{n})
is a
W_{R}
-local replacement of
yL_{h}(A)
.
Therefore
L(A)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}yL_{h}(A_{n})
. By
3.64

we get

S_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})}(L(A))\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}y_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})}(yL_{h}(A_{n}))\ .

We now apply
\hat{R}
and use
y_{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}R\simeq\hat{R}y_{{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep},h})}
in order to get

S_{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(A)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}y_{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(RyL_{h}(A_{n}))

which in view of (
3.40
) is the desired equivalence in Assertion
1
.

In order to show Assertion
2
we note that
the adjoint of the composition of Bousfield localizations
R_{S^{2}}R_{K_{G}}L_{\hat{K}_{G}}:\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
from (
3.59
) provides a fully faithful inclusion
I:\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
whose image consists of
the objects of the form
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)
for twice suspended and
K_{G}
-stable algebras. Using
the explicit formulas for the shape functors in
3.65
and
3.66

we conclude that

S_{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A))\simeq\hat{R}_{S^{2}}\hat{R}_{K_{G}}\hat{L}_{\hat{K}_{G}}S_{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(I(A))\ .

The formula for
S_{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
from Assertion
1
implies the desired formula for
S_{\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
.
∎

The following proposition provides many examples of compact morphisms in
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

Proposition 3.70
.

Let
A\to B
be a semi-projective morphism in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

1.

The induced morphism
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(f):a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)\to a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(B)
is strongly compact in
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

2.

If
B
is twice suspended and
K_{G}
-stable, then
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(f):\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)\to\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(B)
is strongly compact in
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

Proof.

We choose a shape system
(B_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
for
B
.
By semi-projectivity of
f
get a factorization

f:A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f^{\prime}}}{{\to}}B_{n}\to B
of
f
. This gives a map

g:y_{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle y_{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(f^{\prime}))}}{{\to}}y_{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(B_{n}))\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}y_{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(B_{n}))\simeq S_{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(B)

where we use
3.69
.
1
for the last equivalence.
The morphism
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(f)
is equivalent to the composition

a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)\simeq|y_{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A))|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle|g|}}{{\to}}|S_{\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(B))|\simeq a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(B)\ .

We now use that

|g|
is strongly compact by
2.10
. This finishes the proof of Assertion
1
.

The proof of Assertion
2
is completely analoguous using
3.69
.
2
.
∎

We finally explain what we know about examples of compact objects in
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.
If
A
is a semi-projective algebra in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
(i.e.,
{\mathtt{id}}_{A}
is semi-projective),
then
yL_{h}(A)
is
W_{R}
-local and compact in
{\mathtt{Ind}}^{\aleph_{1}}(GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{h,\mathrm{sep}})
. It therefore remains compact in the localization
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
. We conclude:

Corollary 3.71
.

For any semi-projective algebra
A
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
the object
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)
in
\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is compact.

For trivial
G
known examples are
{\mathbb{C}}
, the one-fold suspension
S({\mathbb{C}})
of
{\mathbb{C}}
, the Cuntz algebra
{\mathcal{O}}_{\infty}
, the Toeplitz algebra, see
[
Bla85
]
. Furthermore, the class of semi-projective algebras is closed under forming finite free products.

The left Bousfield localization
L_{\hat{K}_{G}}:\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to L_{\hat{K}_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
preserves compactness since its right-adjoint preserves
countable filtered colimits. In general, the right Bousfield localization
R_{K_{G}}:L_{\hat{K}_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
in the composition (
3.79
) may destroy compactness. But if
G
is finite, then this localization is an equivalence and therefore preserves compactness.
For the further discussion let us therefore assume that
G
is finite.
If the resulting object
a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep},K_{G}}(A)
in
S_{K_{G}}a^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is local for the localization
R_{S^{2}}
, it remains compact after applying

R_{S^{2}}
.

By the equivariant version of
[
DL94
, Thm. 4.3]
group objects in
S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
are local for
R_{S^{2}}
.
Typical examples of group objects are objects in the image of the loop functor. As looping in
S_{K_{G}}\mathrm{A}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
corresponds to suspending in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
we see that for finite groups
G

a semi-projective algebra of the form
A\cong S({\mathbb{C}})\otimes A^{\prime}
(we say once suspended) induces a compact object
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)
in
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

Corollary 3.72
.

If
G
is finite and

A
in
GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is once suspended and semi-projective, then
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)
is compact.

The only example of a once suspended and semi-projective
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra we know is
S({\mathbb{C}})
.

Corollary 3.73
.

If
G
is finite, then
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}})
is a compact object of
\mathrm{E}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

Proof.

We have
\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}})\simeq\Sigma\mathrm{e}^{G}_{\mathrm{sep}}(S({\mathbb{C}}))
and
\Sigma
preserves compact objects.
∎

3.8
Further properties of
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}

In this section we address some further structural questions about the categories
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.
In particular we show that
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is strictly larger than the bootstrap class (otherwise
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
would be compactly assembled for a trivial reason), and that
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is not rigid. In addition we state interesting questions to which we currently do not have answers. At the end of this section we use homological functors in order to provide criteria for a map between separable
C^{*}
-algebras to induce an equivalence or a strong phantom map in
E
-theory.

We start with some preparations from
\infty
-category theory.
We consider a symmetric monoidal countably cocomplete stable
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}
with compact tensor unit
1
(see
1.3
) and with the property that the tensor product preserves countable colimits in each variable. The following is an interpretation of the notion of rigid categories in the countably cocomplete setting, for rigid cateogries in the presentable setting see
[
Efib
]
,
[
Ram
]
.

Definition 3.74
.

1.

A map
f:D\to E
in
{\mathbf{D}}
is trace-class if there exists an object
D^{\prime}
in
{\mathbf{D}}
, a map
g:1\to D^{\prime}\otimes E
and a map
h:D\otimes D^{\prime}\to 1
such that
f
is equivalent to the composite

D\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{id}}_{D}\otimes g}D\otimes D^{\prime}\otimes E\xrightarrow{h\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{E}}E\ .

2.

A trace-class exhaustion of
D
in
{\mathbf{D}}
is a system

(D_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
with
D\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}D_{n}

and such that the structure maps
D_{n}\to D_{n+1}
are trace-class for all
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.

3.

D
in
{\mathbf{D}}
is called trace-class exhaustible if it admits a trace-class exhaustion.

4.

The category
{\mathbf{D}}
is rigid if every object admits a trace-class exhaustion.

Remark 3.75
.

A category
{\mathbf{D}}
is rigid in the sense above if and only if
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})
is rigid as a presentable category (see
[
Ram
, Cor. 4.55]
).
∎

Example 3.76
.

If
R
a commutative ring spectrum, then the category
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R)^{\aleph_{1}}
is rigid. This follows from the fact that
{\mathbf{Mod}}(R)^{\aleph_{1}}
is generated under countable filtered colimits by dualizable objects, and since every map between dualizable objects is trace-class.
∎

Proposition 3.77
.

1.

Every trace-class map is weakly compact.

2.

Every rigid category is compactly assembled.

Proof.

For Assertion
1
we verify the condition described in
2.3
using the same notation. To this end we consider a countable system
(Z_{i})_{i\in I}
with
Z\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}Z_{i}
and let
k:E\to Z
be some morphism.
Using the compactness of
1
and the fact that the functor
D^{\prime}\otimes-
preserves filtered colimits we can find a lift indicated by the dotted arrow in

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{D^{\prime}\otimes Z_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathtt{id}}_{D^{\prime}}\otimes\iota}
\textstyle{D^{\prime}\otimes E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathtt{id}}_{D^{\prime}}\otimes k}
\textstyle{D^{\prime}\otimes Z}

for some
i
in
I
where
\iota:Z_{i}\to Z
is the structure map.
We then apply
D\otimes-
and extend the diagram as follows:

3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-56.36008pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 81.55618pt\raise-47.8556pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.92778pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{h\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{Z}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 81.55618pt\raise-56.36008pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.56801pt\raise-58.26283pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{k}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.78535pt\raise-63.69339pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 74.78535pt\raise-63.69339pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Z}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 140.49791pt\raise-63.69339pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

The second claim follows from the first and
Theorem

2.28
.
∎

We consider the commutative ring spectrum
{S}\coloneqq{\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(1,1)
. The symmetric monoidal structure on
{\mathbf{D}}
induces a lax symmetric monoidal functor

R:{\mathbf{D}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}({S})\ ,

(3.80)

such that there exists a commutative triangle

\textstyle{{\mathbf{Mod}}({S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{D}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{{\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(1,-)}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Sp}}

of lax symmetric monoidal functors so that the structure map
{S}\to R(1)
is an equivalence. Since
1
is compact in
{\mathbf{D}}
the fuctor
{\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(1,-)
and therefore also
R
preserve all countable colimits.

Definition 3.78
.

1.

The bootstrap class
{\mathcal{B}}
in
{\mathbf{D}}
is the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{D}}
generated by the tensor unit under countable colimits.

2.

The Künneth class
\mathcal{K}
in
{\mathbf{D}}
is the full subcategory spanned by the objects
D
in
{\mathbf{D}}
such that for all
E
in
{\mathbf{D}}
the structure map

R(D)\otimes_{{S}}R(E)\to R(D\otimes E)

is an equivalence.

3.

The class
{\mathcal{T}}
is the class of trace-class exhaustible objects.

Proposition 3.79
.

1.

\mathcal{K}
is a stable subcategory closed under countable colimits and
{\mathcal{B}}\subseteq\mathcal{K}
.

2.

{\mathcal{T}}
is a countably cocomplete stable subcategory.

3.

{\mathcal{T}}\cap\mathcal{K}={\mathcal{B}}
.

Proof.

Since
R
and
\otimes
preserve countable colimits the Künneth class is closed under countable colimits.
Since the generator
1
of the bootstrap class is clearly in the Künneth class
we can conclude
Assertion
1
.

For Assertion
2
we invoke
[
KNP
, Lemma 4.4.11]
implying that pushouts of trace-class exhaustible objects are again trace class exhaustible, that
\Omega
preserves
{\mathcal{T}}
and finite coproducts of trace class exhaustible objects are clearly trace-class exhaustible. It remains to show that
{\mathcal{T}}
is closed under countable coproducts. Let
(D_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
be a family in
{\mathcal{T}}
and choose for every
n
in
{\rm Nature}
a trace-class exhaustion
(D_{n,m})_{m\in{\rm Nature}}
of
D_{n}
. Then the system
(D_{0,n}\oplus\cdots\oplus D_{n,n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
with the structure maps

D_{0,n}\oplus\cdots\oplus D_{n,n}\xrightarrow{\alpha}D_{0,n}\oplus\cdots\oplus D_{n,n}\oplus D_{n+1,n}\xrightarrow{\beta}D_{0,n+1}\oplus\cdots\oplus D_{n+1,n+1}

(3.81)

is a trace-class exhaustion of
\bigoplus_{n\in{\rm Nature}}D_{n}
. In this formula
\alpha
is the inclusion of the first
n+1
-summands, and
\beta
is the sum of the trace-class structure maps
D_{i,n}\to D_{i,n+1}
. Since trace-class maps are closed under finite sums and form an ideal the map in (
3.81
) is trace-class as required.

We now turn to Assertion
3
. We first consider
D
in
{\mathcal{T}}\cap\mathcal{K}
satisfying additionally
R(D)\simeq 0
. Since
D
is in
\mathcal{K}
this implies that every trace-class map with codomain
D
vanishes since for all
C
in
{\mathbf{D}}
, we have

{\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}(1,C\otimes D)\simeq{\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathbf{Mod}}(S)}(S,R(C\otimes D))\overset{\text{Künneth}}{\simeq}{\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathbf{Mod}}(S)}(S,R(C)\otimes_{S}0)\simeq 0\ .

Since
D
is trace-class exhaustible this implies
D\simeq 0
as follows: We can choose a trace-class exhaustion
(D_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
of
D
. Then
every structure map
D_{n}\to{D}
is trace class and hence trivial. Since a trace-class map is weakly compact by
3.77
.
2
, for every
n
in
{\rm Nature}
there exists
m
in
{\rm Nature}
large enough such that
D_{n}\to D_{m}
is trivial. This implies that
D\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}D_{n}\simeq 0
as desired.

In light of the above argument, given
D
in
{\mathcal{T}}\cap\mathcal{K}
not necessarily satisfying
R(D)\simeq 0
, it suffices to find an approximation
C\to D
with
C
in the bootstrap class such that
R(C)\to R(D)
is an equivalence.
By Lurie’s version of the Schwede-Shipley theorem we get the right Bousfield localization in the upper part of the following diagram

3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbf{D}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 71.34373pt\raise-24.24028pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{R}$}}}\kern 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 101.65845pt\raise-16.24751pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{y}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 111.9652pt\raise-8.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-64.99893pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 54.36108pt\raise-64.99893pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 103.15273pt\raise-64.99893pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 111.9652pt\raise-56.98111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 111.9652pt\raise-37.99501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 49.60579pt\raise-50.79501pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\perp}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.07391pt\raise-62.8428pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.8978pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{R^{\prime}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 13.59561pt\raise-40.49196pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

This crucially uses the assumption that
1
is compact in
{\mathbf{D}}
which implies that the tensor unit
y(1)
of
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})
is compact as well.
The functor
\hat{R}
is the canonical refinement of

{\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})}(y(1),-):{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})\to\mathbf{Sp}

to a lax symmetric monoidal
{\mathbf{Mod}}(S)
-valued functor. As indicated the restriction of
\hat{R}
along
y
coincides with
R
.
The fully faithful symmetric monoidal functor
\hat{F}
preserves colimits and is uniquely determined by the fact that it sends
R
to
y(1)
. Its restriction to
{\mathbf{Mod}}(S)^{\aleph_{1}}
(indicated by the dotted arrow) therefore takes values in the bootstrap class of
{\mathbf{D}}
. Here we view
{\mathbf{D}}
as a full subcategory of
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{\aleph_{1}}({\mathbf{D}})
via
y
.

We let
{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{K}\cap{\mathcal{T}}
denote the
full subcategory of
{\mathbf{D}}
on objects in the Künneth class which in addition admit a trace class exhaustion.
A symmetric monoidal functor preserves trace-class maps. Since
\hat{F}
is symmetric monoidal and colimit preserving it preserves trace class exhaustions.
Since
{\mathbf{Mod}}(S)^{\aleph_{1}}
is rigid by
3.76
we see that the dotted arrow admits a further factorization over a symmetric monoidal functor
F
as indicated.
We now show that the restriction
R^{\prime}
of
R
to
{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}
takes values in
{\mathbf{Mod}}(S)^{\aleph_{1}}
. Let
E
be in
{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}
and choose a trace-class exhaustion
(E_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
of
E
. Since
E
belongs to the Künneth class the maps
R(E_{n})\to R(E_{{n+1}})
are still trace class and in particular compact. Since
{\mathbf{Mod}}(S)
is compactly generated this implies that
R(E_{n})\to R(E_{m})
factorizes over a compact object for
m
large enough. Thus
R(E)
lies in
{\mathbf{Mod}}(S)^{\aleph_{1}}
.
We conclude that the adjunction
\hat{F}\dashv\hat{R}
restricts to an adjunction

F\dashv R^{\prime}
.

The object
C:=FR^{\prime}(D)
belongs to the bootstrap class.
Since
F
is fully faithful the counit
C\simeq FR^{\prime}(D)\to D

induces an equivalence

R(C)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}R(D)
as desired.
∎

Remark 3.80
.

The argument above implies an equivalence

F:{\mathbf{Mod}}(S)^{\aleph_{1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathcal{T}}\cap\mathcal{K}

with inverse
R^{\prime}
.
∎

We have the countably cocomplete stable
\infty
-categories
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.
On
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
we consider the two symmetric monoidal structures

\otimes_{\min}
and
\otimes
induced by the minimal and the maximal tensor product on
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
, respectively, while on

\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
we only have the symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes
induced by the maximal tensor product.
In any case the tensor product preserves countable colimits in each variable.
The tensor units are given by
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}})
and
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}})
, respectivly.

Lemma 3.81
.

The tensor units in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
are compact.

Proof.

The case of
E
-theory is
3.73
.

For the case of
KK
-theory
we use that
K
-theory functor
K:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
is corepresented in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
by
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}})
:

K(-)\simeq{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}}),\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}(-)):C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

Using the description of the
K
-theory groups of
C^{*}
-algebras in terms of homotopy classes of projections and unitaries one can check (the well-known fact) that
K
preserves countable sums. Since
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is surjective on objects and preserves countable sums this implies the claim.
∎

We can apply the general definitions from above to
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

Definition 3.82
.

1.

We let
{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
denote the bootstrap classes in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

2.

We let
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\max}
,
{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\max}
and
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\min}
,
{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\min}
denote the Künneth classes and the classes of trace-class exhaustible objects
in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
for the tensor products
\otimes
and
\otimes_{\min}
.

3.

We let
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
and
{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
denote the Künneth class and the class of trace-class exhaustible objects in
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

Remark 3.83
.

Given a separable
C^{*}
-algebra
A

it is an interesting question to descide when
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)
belongs to
{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
. If one knows that
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
, then
3.79
implies that
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)\in{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
if and only if
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)\in\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
. The latter condition is equivalent to the condition that the natural transformation

K(A)\otimes_{KU}K(-)\to K(A\otimes-)

of functors from
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to
{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
is an equivalence. This condition only involves
K
-theory and not bivariant
E
-theory.

It is therefore an interesting question to determine for which
C^{\ast}
-algebras
A
the
E
-theory class
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)
admits trace-class a exhaustion. The same applies to
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)
in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
with respect to
\otimes
and
\otimes_{\min}
.
∎

A major source of the complexity in the context of
KK
-theory is the difference between the classes
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\min}
and
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\max}
. This is the content of the following theorem due to Uuye
[
Uuy11
, Ex. 5.1, Ex. 5.3]
which relies heavily on deep results of Skandalis
[
Ska88
]
and Ozawa
[
Oza03
, App. A]
.

Theorem 3.84
.

The classes
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\min}\backslash\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\max}
and
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\max}\backslash\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\min}
are non-empty.

Interesting examples of
C^{*}
-algebras whose
KK
-classes belong to
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\min}
are the reduced group
C^{\ast}
-algebras
C^{*}_{r}(G)
for groups
G
satsfying the Baum-Connes conjecture with coefficients. Using Skandalis
[
Ska88
]
one can find a group
G
satisfing the Baum-Connes conjecture with coefficients and such that
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}(C^{\ast}_{r}(G))\not\in\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\max}
.

A canonical source for objects in
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\max}
is the kernel of the comparison functor
c:\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
in (
3.73
). An example of a
C^{*}
-algebra whose
KK
-class is in the kernel of
c
but not in not
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\min}
is given in Ozawa
[
Oza03
, App. A]
.

Proposition 3.85
.

1.

The class
{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
is strictly smaller than
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

2.

The class
{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
is strictly smaller than
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

3.

The class
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
is strictly smaller than
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

4.

The category
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is not rigid with respect to
\otimes
.

5.

The category
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is not rigid with respect to
\otimes_{\min}
.

Let us note that the first three points of the above proposition are well-known.

Proof.

We first observe that Assertion
1
follows from Assertion
2
, and Assertion
2
in turn follows from Assertion
3
because
{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
is contained in
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
. In order to prove Assertion
3
we choose, using
3.85
, an object
A
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
such that
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)\not\in\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\max}
. Since the comparison functor
c:\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
induces an equivalence

{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}}),\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}(-))\simeq{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}}),{\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}(-)})\simeq K(-){:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)}

of lax symmetric monoidal functors we conclude that
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)\not\in\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
.

In order to prove Assertion
4
we choose
A
in
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\max}\backslash\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\min}
using
3.85
. If
{A}
admits a trace-class exhaustion with respect to
\otimes
, being in
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\max}
implies by
3.79
that
A
belongs to the bootstrap class. This is a contradiction to the assumption that
A\notin\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\min}
.

The proof of Assertion
5
, is, up to switching the role of max and min, the same as the one of Assertion
4
.
∎

The above proposition shows that
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
can not be compactly assembled for the trivial reason that the category is equivalent to the compactly generated category
{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
. It similarly implies that
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
can not be generated by dualizable objects, but it leaves the following questions open.

Problem 3.86
.

1.

Is
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
compactly generated?

2.

Is
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
rigd?

3.

Is
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}
compactly assembled or even compactly generated?

An important property for
C^{*}
-algebras is nuclearity:

Definition 3.87
.

1.

A
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is nuclear if
the natural transformation

(A\otimes-)\to(A\otimes_{\min}-)

(3.82)

of endofunctors of
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is an isomorphism.

2.

The
E
-theoretic nuclear class
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
is the smallest countably cocomplete stable subcategory
of
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
containing
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)
for all nuclear
A
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

3.

The
KK
-theoretic nuclear class
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
is the smallest countably cocomplete stable subcategory
of
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
containing
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)
for all nuclear
A
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
.

Note that
{\mathbb{C}}
is nuclear.
We get inclusions

{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}\subseteq\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}\ ,\qquad{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}\subseteq\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}\ .

The following is a famous classical question:

Problem 3.88
.

Do we have equalities

{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}=\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}},\qquad{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}=\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}\ ?

Proposition 3.89
.

1.

For all
A
in
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
the comparison map
c:\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}
induces an equivalence

{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(A,-)\to{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}(c(A),c(-))\ .

(3.83)

2.

The assertions
{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}=\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}=\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
are equivalent.

Proof.

By stability it suffices to show that the map in (
3.83
) induces an isomorphism on
\pi_{0}
. If
A
is the
KK
-class of a separable nuclear
C^{*}
-algebra this is a classical result due to Skandalis
[
Ska88
]
. Since the class of
KK
-classes
A
for which the map in (
3.83
) is an equivalence is closed under countable colimits and finite limits the Assertion
1
follows.

We now show Assertion
2
. Since the comparison map
c
preserves countable colimits the
KK
-theoretic assertion implies the
E
-theoretic one. For the converse we consider the following square

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 18.14247pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.93414pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.55678pt\raise 6.756pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.7421pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{c_{|{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 72.3508pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-5.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 86.85435pt\raise-5.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 86.85435pt\raise-16.45613pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 86.85435pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-18.14247pt\raise-32.91226pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 37.30217pt\raise-38.96054pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\subseteq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.4422pt\raise-26.15627pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

(3.84)

The upper functor is an equivalence since both bootstrap classes are generated by the compact objects
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}})
or
\mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{sep}}({\mathbb{C}})
, respectively, and the canonical map between their endomorphism algebras is an equivalence. The lower functor is fully faithful by Assertion
1
. In view of the diagram (
3.84
) the left vertical map is an equivalence.
∎

The
3.88
is at least compatible with
3.85
.
2
.

Proposition 3.90
.

We have

\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}\not=\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}}\not=\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{sep}}\ .

(3.85)

These assertions are well-known, but we include an argument for the sake of completeness.

Proof.

As the
KK
-theoretic claim is implied by the
E
-theoretic one we only consider the latter. We pick
A
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
such that
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A)\in\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\min}\backslash\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\max}
. Then there exists
B
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}

which is not contained in the kernel of the functor

F:={\mathrm{Fib}}\left(K(A\otimes-)\to K(A\otimes_{\min}-)\right):C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

(3.86)

Since
A
is in
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}_{\min}
the functor

K(A\otimes_{\min}-):C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)

is exact in addition to being homotopy-invariant,
\mathrm{K}
-stable and sum-preserving. The same applies to
K(A\otimes_{\max}-)
. The functor
F
in
(
3.86
) therefore has a factorization

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 23.86942pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.86942pt\raise 96.3153pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.86942pt\raise-25.23291pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
